# Credence — repo guide for Claude Code sessions

## 🚨 WHICH APP TO EDIT — READ FIRST

Three things share confusingly similar paths. Two are live; one is dead.

- ✅ **UI / anything user-facing → `frontend/` ONLY** — the **Next.js** app (Vercel, auto-deploys on push to main).
- ✅ **API / backend → `app/backend/`** — the **live FastAPI** service (deployed to Lambda via `infrastructure/lambda/deploy.sh`; `app.backend.main` is the entrypoint). This is live — edit it for backend work.
- ⛔ **DEPRECATED legacy Streamlit UI → `app/streamlit_app.py`, `app/home.py`, `app/pages/`, `app/utils/`** — **not deployed, not the product. Do NOT edit** unless a task *explicitly* names "legacy Streamlit."

**Footgun (this is what bit a session on 2026-06-18):** the dead Streamlit UI files sit at the **top of `app/`, right next to the live `app/backend/`**, and Next.js has its *own* router dir at `frontend/app/`. So `app/` is **half-alive**: `app/backend/` = keep; everything else in `app/` = legacy UI = don't touch. "The app's UI" is **always `frontend/`**, never `app/home.py` / `app/pages/`.

**First action in any app/UI session:** run `cat frontend/package.json` and confirm Next.js (`"next"` in deps, `"dev": "next dev"`). If you're doing **UI** work and find yourself in `streamlit_app.py`, `st.set_page_config`, `app/home.py`, or `app/pages/*.py`, **STOP — wrong place → go to `frontend/`.**

## App quick map
- UI (Next.js): `frontend/app/**`, `frontend/components/**`, `frontend/lib/**`, `frontend/hooks/**`, `frontend/data/**`
- Backend API (live FastAPI): `app/backend/**` (`main.py`, `routers/`, `models/`, `services/`); deploy via `infrastructure/lambda/deploy.sh`
- Serving-store writers: `scripts/` (e.g. `write_serving_store.py`); dbt marts in `dbt/`
- Serving store read order: **DynamoDB** (`credence-prod-serving-cache`) → **S3 api-cache** (`credence-prod-s3-api-cache`, us-east-1) → Snowflake (last resort, never at request time). ⚠️ **Railway PostgreSQL was DECOMMISSIONED at INC-16-P2 — it is NOT the serving store** (a stale doc line cost a session real time 2026-08-04). ⚠️ **TRAP: `scripts/write_serving_store.py` deliberately KEEPS the legacy `_pg_*` helper names + `pg`/`pg_conn` locals (documented L373–377) even though the handle is now a boto3 DynamoDB `Table` — grepping for `pg` there reinforces the WRONG conclusion; and a dead `DATABASE_URL`/`thomas.proxy.rlwy.net` may sit in a worktree `.env` (not in `env.required`, so the box ignores it) = a live tripwire. Verify the SERVED artifact (DynamoDB / the S3 api-cache), never a Railway handle.**
- ⛔ Legacy Streamlit (do not edit): `app/streamlit_app.py`, `app/home.py`, `app/pages/**`, `app/utils/**`
- **Changelog:** any user-facing change adds an entry to `frontend/data/changelog.json` as its final step. (Streamlit gets none — it isn't shipped.) 🗓️ Weeks are MONDAY–SUNDAY; the `week` field should be the Monday of the change's week. **As of E9.18 (2026-06-27) this is auto-enforced** — the changelog render auto-snaps any `week` date to its Monday + merges same-week blocks, and `betting_ml/tests/test_changelog_guard.py` (fast gate) fails the build on a non-Monday `week` key or duplicate Monday. So a wrong date can't ship; just add your item (Monday-of-week preferred).

## Where the plans live
- Model + application roadmap (single source of truth): `quant_sports_intel_models/baseball/edge_program/build_roadmap.md`
- Story specs: `quant_sports_intel_models/baseball/edge_program/edge_program_implementation_guide.md` (§0.2 = app architecture + the app-target rule)
- Per-story run prompts: `quant_sports_intel_models/baseball/edge_program/story_prompts.md`

## Conventions (see guide §0.1)
`dbtf` (not `dbt`); Snowflake via MCP, fully-qualified, no `USE`, never on a request path; `uv run python`; hand long-running scripts to the operator (see the 🚦 rule below); **git: commit + push to your OWN session branch off `dev`, NEVER to `dev`/`main` directly — operator merges via PR (see 🌿 below; this REPLACES the old "do not commit/push" rule)**; market-blind for non-market models; honest framing for anything user-facing (no win-rate / edge claims — `best_alpha = 0`).

## 🌿 PER-SESSION BRANCHES + PUBLISH-FROM-MERGED-DEV (2026-08-02 — TRIAL, to fix cross-session collisions)
**WHY:** concurrent sessions kept colliding — NF1.5b + NF3.4 both edited `export_draft_board_json.py` / shared TS in ONE working tree (a silent stomp + a near-miss premature publish). There are TWO collision CLASSES, and each needs its own rule:
1. **CODE collision → EACH SESSION WORKS ON ITS OWN BRANCH.** FIRST action of every session: `git checkout dev && git pull && git checkout -b <session-branch>` (name it for the story, e.g. `nf3.2-receipts`). Commit + push to THAT branch ONLY. ⛔ NEVER commit/push to `dev` or `main` directly. When the story is done + CI-green, the closeout hands the operator a PR (branch → `dev`); the operator merges. A second session that shares files then REBASES on the merged `dev` — the conflict is EXPLICIT + reviewable, not a silent working-tree stomp. (This supersedes the old "sessions don't commit / hand back a `git add` list" model, which assumed one shared tree.)
   - ⚠️⚠️ **A BRANCH ISOLATES COMMITS, NOT THE WORKING TREE — concurrent sessions SHARE ONE CHECKOUT unless a session uses `git worktree` (INC-39 + NF-D19, 2026-08-02, both hit it the SAME day).** Two Claude sessions running against the same repo dir share ONE `.git` working tree, so a parallel session can `git checkout` and switch HEAD OFF your branch mid-task, and its uncommitted in-flight edits to UNRELEASED files sit in YOUR `git status`. The per-session-branch rule prevents a bad *commit*, not a mixed *tree*. **CONSEQUENCES + RULES: (a) re-run `git branch --show-current` IMMEDIATELY BEFORE EVERY COMMIT — HEAD may have moved since you branched; (b) stage ONLY your story's explicit paths (`git add <path> …`), ⛔ NEVER `git add -A`/`git add .` — that sweeps another session's mid-edit into your commit; (c) a test failure in a file you don't recognise is probably another session's mid-edit, NOT your bug — re-run it a minute later before chasing it (INC-39 hit exactly this: 7 core-shard failures in an untracked file that passed on re-run); (d) ⭐ the clean isolation is `git worktree add ../<session-dir> -b <branch>` off `dev` — a SEPARATE working dir so another session's HEAD-switch can't touch yours (NF-D19 did this to isolate from a session mid-task on `mh2.1-wide-window-retrain`); prefer it whenever you know another session is live.**
   - ⚠️ **BUT A FRESH `git worktree` CANNOT RUN THE SLOW GATE — `dbt/target/manifest.json` is gitignored, so it is ABSENT in any new worktree, and `pipeline/__init__.py` reads it AT IMPORT → ~20 sensor tests die at COLLECTION on any fresh-worktree branch (NF-D18, 2026-08-03).** This is a WORKTREE artifact, NOT a code regression — prove it by running the SAME slow gate in a control worktree at plain `dev` (it fails the identical ~20), don't report a phantom regression. The FAST gate is immune by design (it never imports `pipeline` — the E11.23 rule). ⇒ run the SLOW gate from the MAIN checkout (or symlink `dbt/target/manifest.json` into the worktree), and know that "20 sensor tests fail in my worktree" is expected, not your bug. ⭐ And the shared-tree hazard bites from BOTH directions: a CONVENIENCE COMMIT on your branch can SWEEP UP unrelated doc files + untracked artifact dirs belonging to OTHER sessions (NF-D18 swept 4 doc files + an artifact dir) — which is exactly why the `git add <explicit paths>` / never-`git add -A` rule is load-bearing; stage your story's files by name, never the tree.
   - ⭐⭐ **THE MORE DANGEROUS SIBLING — A FRESH WORKTREE CAN PRODUCE A FALSE *PASS*, not just a false failure (pre-draft board regen, 2026-08-03): an ON-DISK artifact-PRECEDENCE bug CANNOT be validated in a worktree, because the gitignored legacy artifact that TRIGGERS it is exactly what a fresh worktree LACKS.** The board exporter's `resolve_board` preferred a legacy `e7_13_prospect_board_comps.csv` (the pre-E8.1 export path) over the freshly-built board; verifying the publish end-to-end in a fresh worktree PASSED (no legacy file there) while PROD — which had a stale Aug-2 copy — silently published a 2-day-old board (moved prod BACKWARDS an hour after a good publish). Same NF-D18 mechanism (worktree lacks the gitignored artifact) facing the OTHER way: there a phantom FAILURE, here a phantom PASS — and the pass is worse because it ships. **RULE: any logic that reads/prefers an ON-DISK artifact (a source-precedence order, a "first existing candidate" pick, a cache/legacy-file fallback) CANNOT be validated in a fresh worktree — the worktree's CLEAN state HIDES the bug. Test it in a checkout that HAS the legacy/artifact files (the MAIN checkout, or copy them in), and always LOG the chosen path + mtime so a wrong pick is visible.** ⭐ SIBLING ROOT-CAUSE: **a "PREFERRED-SOURCE" constant is a TIME BOMB once the build STOPS WRITING that source** — E8.1 made native comps the build output but left the exporter PREFERRING the legacy `BOARD_WITH_COMPS` path, so any checkout that ever ran the old path keeps its stale file forever and the exporter silently prefers it (the "documented ≠ actually served" / stale-preference class). **When a build stops emitting an artifact, grep every CONSUMER's source-preference and repoint it; a preference pointing at a no-longer-written file is a silent-staleness bomb.** (Fixed: prefer a comps-carrying candidate, then NEWEST by mtime; a newer-but-comp-less candidate is passed over with a loud WARNING. Board regen shipped PR #564 org-correction + #566 exporter-fix, both merged; draft ran off the correct 1,459-row board.)
   - 🖥️⭐ **THE MAIN `dev` CHECKOUT IS RESERVED FOR THE OPERATOR + PM-DOC EDITS ONLY — EVERY WORK SESSION RUNS IN ITS OWN OPERATOR-CREATED `git worktree` (2026-08-03, after E8.3 + NF-D17 BOTH worked directly on the shared `dev` tree despite an inline branch FIRST-ACTION).** The failure mode is NOT a bad commit — sessions edit files in whatever checkout they're launched in and do NOT self-isolate, so an inline prompt instruction doesn't hold, and a commit-hook can't catch "editing without committing" (and would block the operator's own `dev` work). ⇒ ISOLATION IS OPERATIONAL, NOT IN-SESSION: the **OPERATOR creates the worktree BEFORE handoff — `git worktree add ../<story> -b <story-branch> dev` — and launches the session in THAT directory**; the session then physically cannot touch the main `dev` tree. The main `dev` checkout is edited ONLY by the operator (pulls/merges) + PM catalog/doc recordings; a session never works in it. This makes a session's "did it branch?" behaviour irrelevant AND protects PM edits on the main checkout from being wiped by a session's `git checkout` (exactly how NF-D17's first draft was lost).
   - ⚠️ **A `git show origin/<branch>` / remote-ref read can RACE the operator's merge and read a STALE ref (INC-39, 2026-08-02).** A session briefly told the operator "the box looks like it's on an un-merged branch" off a `git show origin/main` that raced a merge in flight. Before asserting deployed/branch STATE from a remote ref, `git fetch` first, and treat a single ref read as provisional — a stale read is a false alarm that costs operator time.
2. **LIVE-DATA / SERVING collision → `--publish` + `deploy.sh` ONLY FROM MERGED `dev`, NEVER FROM A WIP BRANCH.** ⚠️ **branches do NOT isolate prod** — there is ONE S3 api-cache bucket + ONE API Lambda, NO staging plane, and `--publish`/`deploy.sh` reach around git entirely. A session may BUILD + verify on its branch, but the actual `export … --publish` to prod S3 and `infrastructure/lambda/deploy.sh` are a DELIBERATE step AFTER the PR merges to `dev` — never fired mid-session off in-flight code. (The NF3.4 near-miss: a WIP re-export stamped the wrong `model_version`, one `--publish` from shipping another story's UNRELEASED board.) If a story's DoD needs a publish/deploy, the closeout LISTS it as an operator step to run POST-MERGE — the session does not do it.
⚠️ **NOT MECHANICALLY ENFORCED (know this):** `dev` has NO branch protection → the PR "gate" is the operator running both CI gates + reading the diff before merge; the branch gives the PLACE to do that, not automatic blocking. And the API-Lambda deploy-skew (backend needs `deploy.sh`; `frontend/` auto-deploys on `main`, NF-C0's lesson) is UNCHANGED — still remember it per feature.

### 🚦 LONG-RUNNING SCRIPTS (>2 min) → HAND TO THE OPERATOR, DO NOT RUN THEM YOURSELF (operator 2026-07-27 — sessions keep ignoring this)
**Any script/command you expect to run longer than ~2 minutes MUST be handed to the operator to execute — do NOT run it in-session.** This includes (non-exhaustive): backfills, `run_w1_lakehouse`/lakehouse rebuilds, full-history ingests, model fits / Optuna bake-offs, dbt `run`/`build` over many models, season-wide serving backfills, any box (`docker compose … exec`) job of real size. A quick smoke run on a TINY slice (1 date / a few rows / `--limit`) to prove the code path is fine and encouraged; the FULL run is the operator's.
**🖥️ DEFAULT A *DATA FIX* TO THE LAPTOP, NOT THE BOX (operator 2026-08-01, INC-37):** a lakehouse rebuild / backfill / re-export / re-score should be handed off as a LAPTOP command unless it needs box-only state. The box is an **r6g.large = 2 vCPU** and `run_w1_lakehouse` sets `SET threads=2` on every heavy build, so DuckDB alone saturates it: the INC-37 remediation tripped the `credence-box-cpu-sustained` CloudWatch alarm (95.5/99.5/90.4% over three 10-min periods) and, worse, **a pinned-CPU box can starve the Dagster daemon → sensors stop ticking (the INC-32 class)** — actively dangerous during an incident whose recovery depends on the lineup-monitor sensor. The laptop is also FASTER (measured: `mart_bookmaker_disagreement` 162.9s on the laptop vs >8 min on the box) and already has everything needed (same S3 via the `credential_chain` secret, AWS keys, `SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY_PATH`) — just export `AWS_DEFAULT_REGION=us-east-2` for DuckDB-S3. ⚠️ Say plainly that a laptop run still writes **PRODUCTION** S3 keys. This does NOT relax the 🟥 RUNTIME GATE: validating a pipeline CODE change still requires the op to run once on the box.
**How to hand it off (mandatory format — the same as the session-closeout run-order rule):** give the operator (a) WHERE to run it — **LAPTOP or the EC2 BOX** (box commands wrapped in `docker compose -f services/dagster/aws/docker-compose.yml exec -T dagster-codeloc …`, `-e AWS_DEFAULT_REGION=us-east-2` for DuckDB-S3), and (b) the FULL, copy-pasteable command (exact flags/paths/env, no placeholders). Never silently kick off a >2-min job and wait on it.
- **🔬 MODELING BAKE-OFF DISCIPLINE (guide §0.5 — non-negotiable):** any story that BUILDS or SELECTS a predictive model runs a **bake-off, not a single architecture** — pre-register ≥3 candidate model classes, Optuna-tune each, feature-ablate, pick on the metric under purged/embargoed CV (per serving tier), guard the search with PBO<0.2/DSR>0. A prescribed structure is **one candidate among several**, always with a direct-learned foil. **A single architecture missing its gate is NOT a trustworthy null** — that's only earned after the whole candidate set, tuned + ablated, fails. Exemplar: E1.9 v6 (6 learner classes → Optuna → gate). Exceptions: pure registration/backfill, cheap sub-model re-eval, or explicit harness/eval stories.
  - **📏 SELECTION-METRIC HYGIENE (E2.1-r, 2026-07-20 — a pre-registered metric can be SILENTLY INVERTED):** ⚠️ **INTERVAL-COVERAGE TARGETS ARE BIASED FOR DISCRETE PREDICTIVES.** E2.1-r pre-registered `|calib_80 − 0.80|` — which LOOKS principled — but **inclusive integer interval bounds inflate a CORRECTLY-specified count model's coverage to ~0.82–0.86**, so the term **REWARDED UNDER-DISPERSION** and briefly ranked a genuinely under-dispersed candidate #1. **RULES: (a) for any DISCRETE/count predictive (runs, strikeouts, points, totals) gate on RANDOMIZED-PIT FLATNESS (e.g. max-decile-deviation), and keep a coverage figure as a FLOOR (`calib_80 ≥ 0.80`), never as a TARGET to minimize distance to; (b) ⭐ SANITY-CHECK EVERY SELECTION METRIC AGAINST AN ORACLE FLOOR** — score a known-correct oracle and assert nothing beats it (a candidate scoring BETTER than the oracle is mathematically impossible = the tell that the metric is inverted). Guard pattern: `test_oracle_is_the_scoring_floor`. Applies to E2.3/E2.4/E5.2 and the NCAAF/NFL totals legs.
    - **⚖️ (c) THE OTHER HALF OF THE ORACLE FLOOR = A DEGENERATE CEILING THAT MUST LOSE (NF-D11, 2026-07-29 — MAE is INVERTED on a zero-heavy cohort).** The oracle-floor catches "nothing should beat a perfect model"; it does NOT catch a metric a NIHILIST wins. NF-D11 selected a return-from-injury availability prior on a cohort where **43% of returners play ZERO games** — MAE is minimised at the conditional median, so it PAYS FOR PESSIMISM: a degenerate "project ZERO games for everyone" arm scored **MAE 15.47, BEATING the best real candidate's 18.96**. A metric a "predict nothing" arm wins cannot select a projection. **RULES: (a) for a zero-inflated / skewed cohort, use a PROPER score (CRPS) as primary — it grades point AND spread jointly, so pessimism doesn't win; (b) score TWO-SIDED anchors every run — the ORACLE FLOOR (knows realized outcomes, nothing beats it) AND a DEGENERATE CEILING (a trivial null — all-zero / all-mean — that MUST lose); a real candidate scoring worse than the degenerate = the metric is inverted.** MAE / RMSE on a zero-heavy or fat-tailed target is the smell.
      - **⭐ REFINEMENT (NF-D14, 2026-07-31 — the pre-registered inversion DID NOT FIRE, and knowing why matters): MAE inverts on the conditional MEDIAN, not on the zero ATOM.** NF-D14 pre-registered the NF-D11 inversion as CERTAIN (rookie QBs are zero-heavy too) — but the all-zero nihilist LOST MAE outright (5.96 vs 3.79). NF-D11's returners: 43% zeros AND **median 1 game** → the conditional median IS ~0 → pessimism wins. Rookie QBs: 31% zeros but **median 4 games** → the conditional median is positive → the nihilist loses. ⇒ **"zero-heavy" is NOT the test for MAE-inversion — the conditional MEDIAN being at/near the floor is. So don't REASON about whether MAE will invert; keep the degenerate ceiling in the field EVERY run and READ its score** (which is exactly why the rule is "score the degenerate," not "check if the cohort looks zero-heavy"). Applies to every zero-heavy/skewed §0.5 leg (E2.x totals/strikeout, NCAAF, prospect).
      - **📉 A DEFLATION STATISTIC COMPUTED OVER A FIELD CONTAINING ITS OWN NULLS MEASURES THE NULLS (NF-D14, 2026-07-31 — the NF1.8 whole-field-spread lesson, one statistic over, now on DSR).** NF-D14's whole-field **DSR 0.069 vs contender-set DSR 0.945** — because `deflated_sharpe`'s expected-max-SR term scales with the trial-Sharpe DISPERSION, and a field that includes its own pre-registered nulls has a huge dispersion that inflates the deflation penalty. **RULE: report BOTH the whole-field and the contender-set deflation stat (DSR/PBO), and pre-register WHICH binds** (NF-D14 bound the whole-field gate and STILL cleared under the generous reading, so nothing turned on the choice — that's the clean shape). Sibling of the NF1.8 "PBO over a field mixing near-clones with far nulls measures the nulls" lesson. Also from NF-D14: a winner can LEGITIMATELY beat a peeking oracle at UNMATCHED n (2.455 vs 2.977, the winner trains on ~500 rows vs the oracle's ~79 held-out) — the `matched_n_candidate` gate (NF1.9 (f)) is what makes that admissible, not a metric inversion; and a two-sided knob on an uncertainty covariate (an availability qreg FEATURE) is free to NARROW even when the widen-only widener is clamped (the NF1.7 (d) hazard on the channel the clamp doesn't cover).
    - **🪤 (d) FOUR WAYS AN ANCHOR SET LIES — the oracle floor / degenerate ceiling need these guards or they pass on nothing (NF1.7, 2026-07-29 — a §0.5 interval selection, all four hit + now guarded).** (1) **AN ANCHOR THAT FAILS TO FIT MAKES ITS CHECK VACUOUSLY TRUE** — NF1.7's per-position oracle refused a fit under 40 rows (a held-out class is ~80 rows / 4 positions → ~20 each), silently returned `None`, and `oracle_respected` passed on NOTHING. RAISE on a missing/failed anchor, never treat `None` as a pass. (2) **THE ORACLE FLOOR MUST BE SAME-FAMILY *AND* SAME-SAMPLE** — a peeking MISSPECIFIED oracle is legitimately beaten by an honest well-specified candidate, and a peeking k-NN's capacity depends on n (k=60 of 62 same-position rows = a constant band an in-fold arm beats). "peeking can only help" holds ONLY at equal family + equal resolution → for the UNIT test use a **PERMUTATION** (fit on truth vs SHUFFLED outcomes), well-posed at any n, not a fitted oracle. (3) **SHARPNESS NEEDS TWO DEGENERATES, NOT ONE** — `zero_width` (maximally sharp; a naive sharpness metric crowns it) AND `max_width` (coverage ~0.98, useless; a coverage-target loves it) must BOTH lose; reporting one leaves the metric gameable from the other side. (4) **A "WIDEN-ONLY" KNOB MUST ACTUALLY BE MONOTONE** — NF1.7's `exp(gain·z)` widener was two-sided → it SHARPENED half the field off a parameter-uncertainty z and cost coverage; clamp `z→max(z,0)` so it can only widen. ⭐ these apply to ANY interval/anchor-gated §0.5 story (E2.1-r/E2.3/E5.2/totals legs + any future NF/prospect interval).
    - **🕳️ (e) A ZERO-ATOM POPULATION MAKES A COVERAGE *TARGET* STRUCTURALLY INVERTED, AND CAN MAKE A CONFORMAL LAYER A MATHEMATICAL NO-OP (NF1.9, 2026-07-29 — the E2.1-r inversion in a NEW costume; ANY skewed/zero-heavy target).** NF1.9's veteran fantasy target has a **31% point mass at exactly 0** and a bound floored at 0, so the left tail is nearly un-missable (83% of lower bounds ARE 0; below-p10 0.025 vs nominal 0.10) → coverage ≈ 1 − P(y>p90) ≈ **0.89 for ANY honest band**, and a **0.80 coverage TARGET is INVERTED — hitting it requires deliberately UNDER-covering the RIGHT tail** (the pre-registered `normal_cov` arm prices that naive fix at +7.2% interval score). The SAME zero atom made NF1.8's *winning* Mondrian conformal layer a **no-op here** (~29% of conformity scores are exactly 0 → the (1−α) quantile pins at 0, all CQR arms score byte-identically to base). ⇒ on a zero-heavy / floored target, keep coverage a FLOOR (E2.1-r) and select on a proper INTERVAL SCORE (Winkler), and don't assume a mechanism that won on one population still ACTS on another — "a mechanism that cannot act is a finding, not an omission." Also: bigger σ was NOT the fix (score- and coverage-fitted normal arms both lost to a neighbourhood quantile that expresses a q10 genuinely 0 across the bottom deciles and positive in the top).
    - **📏 (f) A PEEKING ORACLE IS A FLOOR ONLY AT MATCHED n — OPERATIONALISE IT AS CODE, NOT PROSE (NF1.9).** The NF1.7 (b) lesson ("same-family AND same-sample") shipped as a runnable guard: the winner legitimately BEATS the peeking same-family oracle (160.9 vs 179.8) only because the oracle fits the ~646-row held-out season while the winner trains on ~7,236 — capacity, not leakage. NF1.9 adds a `matched_n_candidate` anchor (the winner's own arm trained on ONE prior season) and gates on the oracle beating THAT (179.8 ≤ 182.9), so the floor is enforced at equal family AND equal resolution. Reusable shape for any oracle-anchored §0.5 story.
    - **🎏 (g) A MATCHED FOIL SEPARATES A NULL FROM A TIE; A LEADERBOARD RANK CANNOT (NF-D10, 2026-07-31 — how a feature-family study earns an ATTRIBUTABLE "no").** NF-D10 tested a coaching-change feature family by registering EACH bundle beside the IDENTICAL bundle MINUS the new feature (`base_system_coach − base_xfp`, `env_coach − base_env`, `kitchen_sink − all_skill`) → the family was negative in 7 of 10 pairings and no position was positive on BOTH its pairs, so the lone positive (RB-sink +0.0229, contradicted by RB's other two pairs) was attributable to a contract/opp interaction, NOT the coach. **A leaderboard rank alone can't do this** — the best coach cell sat inside the top tie band at RB/WR (rank 4–5, ~1% back), which "it didn't win" reads as failure but the matched foil reads correctly as *indistinguishable-from-incumbent, not harmful*. **RULE: to earn an attributable null (or win) for a FEATURE/mechanism, register it as a matched pair (with vs without) and read the PAIRED delta, not the rank** — the rank confuses "my feature is inert" with "my feature is in a tie." (Sibling of the NF1.8 flip-distribution lesson: a rank statistic can't tell a tie from a loss.)
      - **🎏 (g′) A MATCHED FOIL ALSO REFUTES A *WINNER'S STATED MECHANISM*, NOT ONLY A NULL — and a level-vs-per-player claim has a bias SIGNATURE (NF-D15, 2026-08-01).** NF-D14 shipped a real lift and EXPLAINED it as a cold-bias LEVEL correction. NF-D15 registered the identical arm with its **per-player content stripped but the per-position MEAN scale byte-identical** (a matched level-only foil): the foil failed to beat the incumbent AT ALL (RB 87.33 vs 80.43, TE 55.49 vs 53.92) while the real arm beat that foil by 18.9/8.2 PPR (p=0.034/0.022) → the lift is PER-PLAYER, the stated level mechanism is REFUTED. ⭐ The corroborating tell a level story CANNOT produce: **the winner moved pooled bias FURTHER from zero (−20.87→−22.44) while MAE improved** — a genuine level correction moves bias TOWARD zero. **RULE: to attribute a WIN to its claimed mechanism (not just to earn a null), register a matched foil that keeps everything EXCEPT the claimed channel and read the paired delta + the mechanism's own signature (bias direction for a level claim); "my arm won" is not "it won for the reason I said." And a mechanism can be BOTH wrong-as-stated and real-as-a-separate-effect** — a per-position CONSTANT recalibration independently beat the incumbent (p=0.0052), a DIFFERENT effect from the per-player lift, so "the level story is refuted" ≠ "there is no level effect" (→ spun off pre-registered as NF-D16).
      - **📉 (g″) UNDERPOWERED ≠ ABSENT — a real effect can reproduce on the selecting metric, pass PBO, and STILL fail DSR/BH-FDR purely from n; the fix is a SCHEDULED re-run, not a ship, and the record must say so (NF-D15).** NF-D15's lift reproduced (draftable-tier MAE RB 80.43→68.44, TE 53.92→47.33), PBO was clean (0.029/0.057), the matched foil attributed it — yet it failed DSR (0.022/0.008) and the 3-test BH-FDR, so nothing shipped. The trap is recording that as "we tested it, nothing there." Two disciplines make the null honest: **(a) state the margin in the UNIT THAT GROWS, not p-decimals** (NF1.8's "in ROWS" convention, one unit over — here "RB needs 11 draft classes and has 7, TE needs 10, WR needs 29" ⇒ RB/TE are underpowered and re-runnable in 2028/29, WR is a genuine absence); **(b) prove the null doesn't rest on YOUR gate choice** — re-run at DSR≥0.0 and with DSR removed; if it still doesn't ship, the binding constraint is elsewhere (here the per-position FRAMING's multiplicity penalty: pooled single-hypothesis p=0.081 vs the 0.033 BH cutoff), which is a computed finding, not a shrug. **RULE: an effect that is real-but-underpowered gets a SCHEDULED re-validation trigger (a data-availability pointer on the re-validation cadence, NOT folded into a coverage-floor exit code that would corrupt that contract) and a record that reads "blocked by multiplicity at n=7, NOT absent" — distinct in kind from a genuine absence.**
      - **🏓 (g‴) A PEEKING/ORACLE CEILING MUST BE PER-*FORM* WHEN THE CANDIDATE FORMS NEST — one ceiling for the whole field VETOES A LEGITIMATELY-BETTER NESTED FORM as a false metric-inversion (NF-D16, 2026-08-01 — shipped in the first cut, caught by a regression test, the closest that session came to a wrong answer).** NF-D16 floored every recalibration arm on the per-position CONSTANT ceiling — but the slot-varying (`mult_tier`) and affine (`ols_slope`) forms each CONTAIN that constant as a special case, so either can legitimately beat it (the NF1.7 (b) / NF1.9 (f) CAPACITY effect), and the single ceiling would have vetoed the real winner as an inversion. **RULE: floor each arm by the peeking version of its OWN form; the four per-form ceilings then ORDER by capacity (ols 0.778 < tier 0.835 < const 0.909 < offset 0.911) — an internal-consistency signal a single ceiling structurally cannot produce.** Two siblings from the same story: **(1) a PERMUTATION anchor is near-vacuous against a LEVEL/marginal hypothesis** — a within-position shuffle PRESERVES a level (additive form: measured max |Δ| 7.1e-15), so register it as an expected TIE in advance and prove it, don't present the near-tie as a passed test (NF1.9's "a mechanism that cannot act is a finding"); **(2) "zero ordering harm BY CONSTRUCTION" is FALSE for an affine — it is only CONDITIONALLY monotone** (a negative fitted slope inverts a whole position's board), so measured zero rank-movement is a property of THIS run's slopes, not the form — measure it, never assert it. And **(3) a "moves no ranks" risk argument for a level shift is a WITHIN-position claim only** — rookies and veterans share one board, so lifting every rookie reorders them ACROSS positions and can trip a whole-board placement gate (NF-D16's rookie #1 flipped QB→RB); cite it as within-position or it gets mis-applied.
      - **🕳️ (g⁗) "THE CONSTRAINT HELD ON N OF M FOLDS" IS NOT EVIDENCE UNTIL YOU MEASURE HOW MANY FOLDS THE MECHANISM COULD *ACT* ON AT ALL — a STRUCTURALLY-INACTIVE gate produces a clean-looking record in BOTH directions (NF-D20, 2026-08-04 — NF1.9's "a mechanism that cannot act is a finding" + MH2's `INACTIVE` state, applied to a GATE rather than an ARM).** NF-D20 selected a rookie-board shrink IN-FOLD under a per-fold whole-board PLACEMENT constraint → `CONSTRAINT_REFUSED`; the transferable finding is WHY: **the constraint is INACTIVE on 4 of 8 boards** — the best rookie there is a QB (the position the correction may not touch), so no λ moves the rank and every λ is VACUOUSLY admissible. A rule reading prior boards learns "λ=1 was fine," then meets a class whose best rookie is a corrected RB/WR and is caught ⇒ **the per-fold constraint is NOT LEARNABLE from prior boards because its ACTIVITY is a draft-class ACCIDENT, not a stable property.** The TELL that a gate is inactive-not-passing: a BLIND matched foil (zero information) satisfies it out-of-sample on EVERY fold while no informed rule does — the blind sheet is as thin as the rules' failures. **RULE: before crediting "the constraint held N/M," COUNT the folds on which the mechanism could CHANGE the constrained quantity; report the active-fold count beside the pass count; an inactive fold is UNINFORMATIVE, never a pass.** ⭐ TWO SIBLINGS (NF-D20): (1) **a pre-registered anchor that FAILS is left FAILING and DECOMPOSED, never re-labelled** — `over_scale` (λ=2, registered to lose) BEAT every real arm, which is NOT a metric inversion (the do-nothing degenerates lose by a mile) but a refuted MAGNITUDE hypothesis (the metric keeps improving past the registered interval ⇒ the fit UNDER-corrects and the metric-optimum is OPPOSED to the placement constraint with NO interior optimum) — obtainable only because the anchor was SCORED, not reasoned about (NF-D14 (g′)); (2) **a BUNDLED gate flag mixing a metric-SANITY check with a MAGNITUDE hypothesis is a liability** — a future reader reads its `False` as "the measurement is untrustworthy" when the sanity half is fine. And the decision-shape rule: **when a null rests on a REGISTRATION CHOICE (an arm registered non-shippable) rather than a gate LEVEL, say so plainly** — NF-D20's blind constant would have SHIPPED (DSR 0.9999, cap-clear) had it been registered shippable, so the honest record states that one arm's ELIGIBILITY, not any threshold, separates the null from a ship (⛔ re-classifying it post-hoc is the E2.1-r inversion in its most literal form).
    - **🗄️ …AND A NULL CAN BE A STALE-CACHE ARTIFACT — a bake-off over a feature cache written BEFORE the family was registered reports a CLEAN NULL for a family that was never in the matrix (NF-D10).** NF-D10 nearly reported a comparison as "apples-to-apples" with an archived stage, but the rebuilt pool was 6958 vs 6736 because an upstream universe change (NF-D11) had landed ~14h after the archived run and sat UNMATERIALISED in stale caches → the archived "null" was over a different (smaller) universe. `cache_is_current` surfaced it. **RULE: before trusting a NULL from a bake-off, confirm the family/universe was ACTUALLY in the assembled matrix (a `cache_is_current` guard) and check the archived result's `generated_at` against `git log` on the UPSTREAM modules before claiming comparability — a re-run's "same answer" over a stale cache proves nothing.**
  - **🧮 READING A HIGH PBO CORRECTLY (E2.1-r):** when the top configs **genuinely TIE** (E2.1-r: top-12 within ~4%), a **HIGH PBO is the NULL, not evidence of overfitting** — "which tied candidate wins" is noise, which is exactly what a trustworthy learner-null looks like. Do NOT report a tied-field high PBO as a failed search; report it as "no candidate robustly beats the incumbent ⇒ the incumbent's choice is now PROVEN, not assumed." (Conversely a high PBO with a WIDE spread IS overfitting — the spread is the discriminator.)
    - **⚖️ …BUT THE WHOLE-FIELD SPREAD IS THE WRONG DISCRIMINATOR ONCE THE FIELD MIXES NEAR-CLONES WITH KNOWN-BAD NULLS (NF1.8, 2026-07-29 — the rule above would have FORBIDDEN shipping a tie).** NF1.8's 80-config field held ~15 near-clones **within 0.3%** at the top AND deliberately-included honest nulls **27% away** at the bottom. So `PBO 0.514` + `spread 27%` landed squarely in the "genuine overfitting ⇒ do NOT ship" quadrant while the actual contest at the top was a coin flip between two arms 0.94% apart. **A rank statistic cannot distinguish "my pick is unstable" from "my pick is tied", and a spread computed over a field that CONTAINS its own nulls measures the nulls, not the contest.** ⭐ **RULES: report three things beside PBO — (1) the **FLIP DISTRIBUTION** (which arms win the in-sample halves, and how often: mass on two arms a fraction of a percent apart IS a tie; mass spread thinly over a dozen unrelated arms is a search that learnt nothing — this is the cheapest and most informative of the three); (2) **Bailey's PERFORMANCE DEGRADATION** — the median out-of-sample score gap between the in-sample winner and the OS best, which asks the decision-relevant question ("did picking it COST anything?") rather than the rank question (NF1.8: 2.0%); (3) the **CONTENDER spread** (top quartile only), not the whole field. And compute PBO over the **ELIGIBLE set** — the search the selection actually ran — not over every config scored.** Then break a genuine tie on a pre-existing DEFECT metric the program already owns (NF1.8 used per-player-ness — the runner-up quietly reverted 4.9% of rows to the class-level band it exists to remove), **never** on the constraint's own headroom (see the floor rule below).
    - **🚧 A COVERAGE FLOOR IS A CONSTRAINT AND MUST NEVER BECOME A TARGET — AND ITS PROOF IS THAT A DEGENERATE SATISFIES IT (NF1.8).** E2.1-r says a coverage figure is a FLOOR, never a target. NF1.8 adds the operational test: **score the `max_width` degenerate against the floor and report it.** It SATISFIES every per-position coverage floor and still loses the primary metric by a wide margin — which is exactly the right shape (a constraint a degenerate satisfies is fine, because the metric then eliminates it; a *criterion* a degenerate wins is fatal). Two consequences: **(a) never tighten a coverage floor above nominal "for safety"** — every notch above nominal moves the eligible set toward `max_width` and away from an honest interval; **(b) never break a tie on "more headroom above the floor"** — that criterion is MONOTONE IN WIDENING, so the degenerate wins it outright, and re-picking a tie on it is the E2.1-r inversion facing the other way.
    - **📐 A PER-GROUP FLOOR CANNOT BE A MEAN OF PER-CLASS MEANS, AND ITS MARGIN MUST BE STATED IN ROWS (NF1.8).** Two separate traps in evaluating a per-group (per-position / per-book / per-park) coverage floor: **(1)** NF1.7 averaged each metric over held-out CLASSES and required ≥3 rows for a group to enter a class's mean — so a group thin in one class was **silently DROPPED**, and a floor computed that way is evaluated on a quietly different population than the one it names. **Pool over ROWS for any per-group constraint** (and report both conventions when changing one). **(2)** ⭐ **Report the floor margin in ROWS, not in coverage decimals.** NF1.8's QB "0.741 → 0.815" reads like a calibration change; it is **six covered rookie-seasons out of 81, with 1 row of slack** (RB: **0** rows). At n≈81 a hard point-estimate floor at nominal rejects a PERFECTLY-calibrated arm ~50% of the time — **and that false-reject rate barely moves with n; sample size buys the ability to detect a SMALLER true shortfall, not a lower false-reject rate.** So report the exact `P(reject | truly nominal)`, both the binomial AND the class-CLUSTERED SE (rows inside a class are not independent draws), and pre-register a power-derived fallback floor for a structurally-thin group (derived from n ALONE — a design quantity known before any result — or it is a floor reverse-engineered from the answer).
    - **🎚️ PER-GROUP *CALIBRATION* WORKS WHERE PER-GROUP *FITTING* DOES NOT (NF1.8).** Given a per-group coverage floor, the instinct is to fit the model separately per group. NF1.8 pre-registered both: a separate quantile REGRESSION per position **LOST and made the target group WORSE** (QB 0.716 — separate slopes overfit at that group's n), while a **MONDRIAN / group-conditional CONFORMAL layer** (inflate by the (1−α) quantile of the conformity scores *within* the group, at the finite-sample level `⌈(1−α)(n+1)⌉/n`) **won and lifted only the group that was short** (+0.82 PPR at QB, exactly 0.0 elsewhere). Always pre-register the **POOLED conformal FOIL** beside it: on NF1.8 pooled calibration was a numerical **NO-OP**, which is what makes "the per-group conditioning earned it" attributable instead of asserted. ⚠️ And **an in-fold calibration cannot see a NEXT-period gap** — small fitted adjustments are evidence the gap is period-to-period variation rather than fittable miscalibration, not evidence the mechanism failed.
  - **🔭 READING A §0.5 NULL'S POWER — A NULL IS IN ONE OF SEVEN STATES AND A REPORT MUST NAME WHICH (MH2, 2026-08-02 — the diagnostic of the eval; instrument = `betting_ml/utils/cv_power.py`, characterization = `ablation_results/mh2_cv_power_characterization.md`).** Three of the four §0.5 gates have a **stringency that moves with the DESIGN rather than with the evidence**, and the program had been reading them as fixed bars: the fold clause fires on a TRUE lift of zero **49.7% of the time at 3 folds and 27.4% at 11**; PBO is **UNDEFINED (not failed)** below 4 folds; the fold-SIGN floor `2⁻ⁿ` can sit ABOVE the BH cutoff so **no effect of any size could pass** (E7.14); and DSR's bar rises with the **FIELD SIZE**, so the same winner on the same folds clears at 2 arms and fails at 7 (E7.15-H3, reproduced exactly: `bb_pct` 0.607→0.998, `iso` 0.657→0.981). ⇒ **"trustworthy-dead vs underpowered" is a FALSE BINARY.** Classify with `cv_power.classify_null`, in this order: **INACTIVE** (the mechanism has no rows it can move — E7.15's `xwoba_against`, zero within-player transitions; a SCOPE finding, remedy = a different population, and there is **no defect to hunt**) · **UNKNOWN** (the artifact records no fold structure — unread, not underpowered; E7.3's summaries) · **UNDEFINED** (a stat was not COMPUTABLE, or a run was anchor-BLOCKED ⇒ no verdict was reached) · **GENUINE ABSENCE** (the best arm loses ON AVERAGE — no n and no field size rescues a negative point estimate; **do not state a re-test trigger**) · **DSR-UNREACHABLE** (beats the foil but `SR ≤ SR0` in THIS field ⇒ **no `n` ever clears** — `n` enters only through `√(n−1)`, so it scales a positive gap but cannot create one; remedy is a SMALLER field, and reporting it as "needs N more seasons" is actively misleading) · **POWER-LIMITED** (every gate reachable; state the shortfall in the unit that grows) · **TRUSTWORTHY DEAD** (MDE ≤ the pre-registered practically-meaningful effect, and nothing showed). Two axis rules the program did not have: **(a) A FAMILY GETS ITS OWN PRE-REGISTERED FIELD — AND THE RULE IS TWO-SIDED.** `SR0 = √V·z(N)` is taxed through TWO channels, the trial COUNT `N` *and* the cross-trial Sharpe DISPERSION `V` that far-away arms inflate (on E7.15-H3 the DISPERSION channel dominates, `V` falling ~65× vs ~2.5× for `z`) ⇒ bundling unrelated mechanisms OVER-taxes a real finding. ⚠️ **BUT TRIMMING A FIELD AFTER THE FACT UNDER-taxes it, and that is a SECOND layer of the very selection bias DSR exists to deflate — the arm you drop is chosen because it LOST. YOU GET TO PRE-REGISTER A FAMILY; YOU DO NOT GET TO DISCOVER ONE.** Worked example, and it overturns the headline this case was being used for: E7.15-H3's recorded "clears at **0.998** over the 2-arm trajectory family" DROPS `T3_tenure`, a named member of that family in H3's own pre-registration. Restore it and the SAME winner on the SAME folds reaches **0.849** — and scored against its own pre-registered family, **NOTHING in H3 clears** (batter `bb_pct` 0.849 / `iso` 0.759 / `k_pct` 0.748; pitcher `gb_pct` 0.564). Also: the pitcher side's largest H3 lift (`k_pct` +1.713%) belongs to the PLAYER-STRUCTURE family, not to trajectory — carrying "H3's trajectory arms are a real effect" forward without splitting the families mis-attributes a result. ⇒ the lesson is "run a COHERENT, DECLARED family", not "run fewer arms"; **(b) STATE THE MARGIN IN FOLDS/SEASONS/ROWS AND SAY WHETHER IT IS REACHABLE NOW** — a trigger reachable by a WIDER WINDOW or a SMALLER FIELD is a live re-test, only a calendar-bound one is a future note. ⭐ **The worked example, measured: the MLB game model's 3-fold ceiling is a WINDOW CHOICE, not a data limit** — E7.9 ran 2021–2026 (6 seasons ⇒ 3 folds) while the served store holds **2015–2026 / 26,883 rows**, and 2016–2026 sits at ≥0.827 non-null coverage of the served 13-col contract ⇒ **8 folds available TODAY**. Also structural: at **3 observations the maximum attainable DSR is 0.977** (`dsr_ceiling`) against a 0.95 gate — E7.9's `DSR 0.842` is a statement about the design before it is one about the features. ⚠️ And the same gate NAME is computed two ways — `h_harness.dsr_report` uses FOLDS + measured `trial_sharpes`, `e7_9_train_serve_consistency` uses year-MONTH buckets (~19 non-independent obs) and omits `trial_sharpes` (so `V` is the asymptotic `1/n_obs`); **both biases inflate DSR**, so E7.9's figure is if anything generous.
    - **⚠️ THE `DSR-UNREACHABLE` REMEDY "a SMALLER field" IS UNSAFE WHEN THE FIELD IS ALREADY THE DECLARED MINIMUM — and `cv_power.classify_null` EMITS IT BLINDLY (MH2.2, 2026-08-03; → MH2.7 fixes it in the instrument).** The (a) rule above ("you get to PRE-REGISTER a family, you do NOT get to DISCOVER one") applies to the INSTRUMENT'S OWN remedy text: on MH2.2's `bb_pct` (a 3-arm DECLARED family) `classify_null` prescribed "a field of ≤2 arms" — which IS the retired POST-HOC field, i.e. the remedy re-commits the exact selection bias it exists to deflate, inside a badge that READS LIKE A FIX. `classify_null` sees only a TRIAL COUNT and cannot tell a DECLARED narrow family from a DISCOVERED one. **RULE — ✅ NOW ENFORCED IN CODE (MH2.7, PR #791, 2026-08-15): `classify_null` takes `declared_field_size=` and REFUSES to prescribe below it (the ≤N figure survives as arithmetic; the imperative is withheld + replaced by a named selection-bias refusal; machine flag `field_remedy_admissible`; the n_arms=1 "fold shortage" mis-render → PBO INAPPLICABLE, not a fold trigger). ⚠️ BUT it forces an AUDITABLE CLAIM, it does NOT adjudicate the pre-registration — "declared" is a fact about a DOCUMENT not the data, so a caller passing a `declared_field_size` it DISCOVERED post-hoc still gets the admissible badge (the claim lands on `detail['declared_field_size_source']` for a reviewer to check vs the prereg). ⭐ NEW §0.5 stories pass `declared_field_size=` (normally n_arms) + read `field_remedy_admissible`, NOT the prose (guide §0.5.4 rules 5/5b); existing MLB callers stay on the safe default. ⭐ TWO REUSABLE LESSONS: (i) the INSTRUMENT'S OWN REMEDY must obey the rule the instrument enforces — a defect corrected N times downstream (the n_arms=1 render was hand-fixed 4×: NF-W2/D18/W3/W4) is a defect in the INSTRUMENT; (ii) changing a SHARED instrument requires grepping cross-vertical guards that PIN its output (the football suite pinned MLB-instrument behaviour, invisible to a targeted local run — a guard suite can encode a RETIRED world, so re-anchor onto the new implementation, don't weaken or delete).** Also confirmed at MH2.2: the post-hoc DSR jump (0.849→0.998) was bought by cross-trial DISPERSION `V` collapsing (19,938× on `bb_pct`), NOT by the trial COUNT — "we ran fewer arms" badly under-explains a field trim. ⚙️ PROCESS: the ">2 min ⇒ operator run" tag is OVER-applied to §0.5 RE-SCORING — MH2.2 finished 22s/side because the expensive random-intercept arms weren't in the field → run a cheap SMOKE before defaulting to an operator handoff for a re-scoring story.
    - **⛔ AN 8TH NULL STATE THE SEVEN-STATE TAXONOMY DOES NOT CONTAIN — `CONSTRAINT_REFUSED` (NF-D18, 2026-08-03): a null caused by a DETERMINISTIC CONSTRAINT, where MORE DATA CAN NEVER CHANGE THE VERDICT, and `cv_power.classify_null` MISCLASSIFIES it as `POWER_LIMITED` → an actively misleading "re-test with more seasons" trigger.** NF-D18's attenuating arms did NOT lose on the metric (qmap 0.9506 beat the incumbent 1.0738, winning 6/7 classes; 3 of 4 beat it) — they were REMOVED by a whole-board RANK against a fixed placement cap, where NO sampling error accumulates, so no number of additional draft classes can move a board rank. The seven states classify STATISTICAL nulls (power, field size, absence); a deterministic-constraint refusal is a different kind. **RULE: when a null comes from a HARD CONSTRAINT (a rank cap, a monotonicity requirement, a placement bar) rather than from the metric, classify it `CONSTRAINT_REFUSED` — the remedy is a DIFFERENT MECHANISM or a PM DECISION, NEVER more data; do NOT publish a `classify_null` "needs +N seasons" trigger for it (that's the misleading direction MH2's own g″ rule warns against, one class over).** And the degenerate anchor gains a SECOND job once a constraint is in the field (NF1.8 extended): a constraint a degenerate SATISFIES is fine (the metric eliminates it), but a criterion a degenerate WINS is fatal — SCORE the degenerate's placement (NF-D18's `zero_scale` satisfied the cap trivially) to PROVE the constraint wasn't quietly promoted into a selection criterion. ⭐ And the frontier finding that outlives the null: a plain GLOBAL SHRINK of the ratified correction cleared the cap at λ=0.75 retaining 81.5% of the gain — "the constraint was never binding, the SHAPES were" — but ⛔ that λ CANNOT be pre-registered forward (it was fitted to the one board with the answer in view = the E2.1-r inversion in a successor's badge); a legitimate successor SELECTS the shrink IN-FOLD under a per-fold constraint (→ NF-D20). "A null that names where the answer lives" is the strongest kind.
    - **🎲 H8 — A CONSISTENCY CLAUSE'S FALSE-FIRE RATE MUST BE A DESIGN CONSTANT, NOT A SIDE-EFFECT OF `n` (MH2; the E7.12-S5/S6 defect, FIXED).** `fold_win_rate ≥ 0.60` is `P(Bin(n,½) ≥ ⌈0.6n⌉)` under the null — 0.50 at n=3, 0.34 at n=6, 0.27 at n=11 — i.e. **it is a different gate at every fold count and at the low end nearly free** (which is why an E7.12-S5 placebo cleared it 9/11 and S6 measured 49.7% at 3 folds). CURE = `cv_power.fold_consistency_clause`: required WINS = `max(⌈0.6n⌉, smallest k with P(Bin(n,½) ≥ k) ≤ 0.20)`, wired into `h_harness.numeric_gate` via `n_folds=`. It (1) bounds the false-fire rate at every n, (2) declares itself **UNDEFINED rather than passed** when `2⁻ⁿ > α` (n ≤ 2), and (3) is **weakly STRICTER than the legacy clause at every n**, so it can only prevent a false ADD — verified mechanically to re-decide **none** of the 8 recorded ADDs (all sit at 8–10 wins of 11 against a requirement of 8). ⚠️ **The `max(...)` is load-bearing and was caught by its own guard:** a fixed-α sign test asymptotically demands a RATE → 0.50, so past **n ≈ 31** the calibrated count alone would be LOOSER than 60% — the two clauses answer different questions (statistical vs substantive consistency) and the gate wants both. α=0.20 is derived from DESIGN quantities (the tightest end of the legacy clause's own operating range; and it is already `MAX_PBO`), and the α=0.10 sensitivity — which WOULD re-decide 4 of the 8 ADDs — is reported rather than hidden. 🪤 Same story, THIRD defect in `null_analysis`'s DSR extrapolation (after E7.15-H3's two): `np.resize` TILES the skill series, inflating its `ddof=1` Sharpe by ≈`√(n/(n−1))` and non-monotonically, and the single-element `trial_sharpes` made `V` fall back to `1/n_obs` **on the resized length** — so the benchmark melted away as folds were added. Every recorded `folds_needed_DSR` was wrong (E7.15-H3 `bb_pct` 140 → **372**; three were **UNREACHABLE at any n**). Fixed to the closed form; **no verdict changed — only the re-test TRIGGERS.**
    - **🩹 MH2.1 — FOUR §0.5 LESSONS FROM THE FIRST WIDE-WINDOW RE-TEST (2026-08-02; the live re-test MH2 predicted, verdict SHIP_CHALLENGER on total_runs/post_lineup, `best_alpha=0`).** MH2's thesis reproduced exactly (the DSR bar fell 7.28→1.18 as folds went 3→8×28→4 arms and the arm cleared it — the 3-fold ceiling was a WINDOW choice, not a data limit). Four durable defects, all caught by CONTROLS not by eye: **(a) A DIAGNOSTIC ANCHOR IS NEVER A TRIAL — check every anchor against every FIELD it might silently JOIN, not just the leaderboard.** The E2.1-r `oracle_floor` (which SEES the target, per-fold Sharpe ~30) had leaked into the DSR TRIAL field → drove cross-trial dispersion V≈220, SR0≈15.6, making DSR unclearable for a purely ARITHMETIC reason — the anchor that exists to POLICE the metric was silently SETTING the gate's own bar. Exclude diagnostic anchors from V; and a reference arm's identically-ZERO skill series also inflates a small-family V, so measure V over NON-reference arms while keeping n_trials at full field for multiplicity. **(b) ⭐ WHEN A KNOWN-CORRECT REFERENCE EXISTS ANALYTICALLY, ANCHOR THE METRIC ON IT, NEVER ON THE INCUMBENT — an incumbent-relative metric INVERTS whenever the incumbent is the defective one.** A "does the leader differ from the matched foil?" verdict labelled the BETTER-calibrated arm "damaged" because the foil was the WORST arm; the fix anchors on the analytic truth Var(z)=1, not on the incumbent. (Sibling of NF-D10's matched-foil lesson, one turn further: distance-from-the-incumbent can only say "different"; a promotion needs "better".) **(c) REPORT PER-COLUMN ABSENCE, NOT A POOLED COVERAGE MEAN — "missing" and "NEVER EXISTED" are different findings.** A ~0.83 per-season coverage mean read as "uniformly noisier data," but two of the 13 served contract columns are STRUCTURALLY ABSENT in the early folds (Statcast bat-tracking 0.000 through 2022; `home_starter_proj_fip` 0.000 for 2016–19) → only 3 of 8 folds actually test the SERVED contract ⇒ part of a window-widening's added power is COSMETIC. Keep the handicap identical across arms (never post-hoc trim), but bound what the wide window CERTIFIES. **(d) A SCORING STATISTIC WHOSE EXPECTATION GROWS WITH STRATUM COUNT IS NOISE — score it against a permutation/analytic null before trusting it** (a max−min-of-per-stratum-coverage stat posts 0.069 for a PERFECT model at ~320 rows/decile; the positive control returned INSTRUMENT_BLIND). ⭐ And the finding that OUTLIVES the story (→ MH2.5): CRPS is mean-dominated and PIT-KS is marginal, so BOTH are structurally BLIND to a homoscedastic model losing per-game variance — a conditional-calibration check (RMS |Var(z)−1| across σ-deciles) is the instrument built to see it. ⚠️⚠️ **RETRACTED (MH2.1 promotion ROLLED BACK 2026-08-02, PR #514): the specific claim "the served ngboost σ is actively MISCALIBRATED — 0.050 vs 0.158, Var(z)=1.44 in the calmest decile" is WITHDRAWN.** The challenger was promoted on that basis, briefly served, then rolled back — because ⭐ **A CONDITIONAL-CALIBRATION RESULT IS A PROPERTY OF ITS STRATIFIER, and this one's stratifier was UNVALIDATED: it binned by the WORST arm's σ and never checked those bins separate REALIZED dispersion.** Re-scored against a VALIDATED stratifier (the served v6's own σ, where realized SD rises 3.671→4.973 across deciles, ρ≈0.66) the ordering FLIPPED — the incumbent won in every window. ⭐ **THE DURABLE RULE (→ belongs beside E2.1-r / NF1.7 as the metric-inversion class raised ONE LEVEL, from the metric to the PARTITION it's computed over): before reading ANY Var(z)-by-stratum / coverage-by-bucket / calibration-by-decile number, PUBLISH the realized-SD-per-bin table with its rank correlation + per-bin SE and PROVE the bins separate realized dispersion — the usual controls (a σ-CV floor, a matched heteroscedastic foil, a flattened positive control, a permutation null) all ask "can the instrument detect a known defect?" and NONE asks "does the partition mean anything."** ⭐ **AND SPLIT A SAME-SEASON BACKTEST AT THE INCUMBENT'S FIT DATE: a fresh challenger is in-sample over its whole window, so the incumbent's held-out window is the ONLY reading a same-season backtest can support — an incumbent win there is decisive, a challenger win there is confounded.** ⇒ what SURVIVES: CRPS is mean-dominated + PIT-KS is marginal, so both are structurally blind to a per-game-variance defect (the check's REASON to exist stands); the CRPS bake-off itself (+0.0297 OOF, 8/8, PBO 0.010, DSR 0.9998) — it was never the promotion's argued basis. `best_alpha=0` meant NO bet rode on either model; the rollback cost one registry edit. (MH2.5 re-scoped: the honest target is "make per-game σ GENERALIZE + widen its dynamic range," NOT "flatten it"; flat-σ is now a null-to-beat, not a proven improvement.) ⭐ **PROMOTION-MECHANICS LANDMINES the rollback surfaced (any future SINGLE-TARGET model promotion inherits all three): (a) A ONE-TARGET SWAP BREAKS EVERY CONSUMER THAT ASSUMES TARGETS MOVE AS A BUNDLE — `daily_model_predictions.model_version` is stamped from `registry["home_win"]` only, the backfill idempotency key is `(game_pk, model_version, retrain_tag)`, and `mart_clv_labeled_games` hardcodes `model_version='v6'`; promoting one target silently (App shows no change / backfill writes nothing and reports success / "helpfully" re-pinning the CLV mart to the new version matches ZERO rows = the actual outage). Before promoting one target, audit every bundle-assuming consumer; add a per-target version column, parameterise the retrain-tag. (b) SERVE THE OBJECT THAT WAS VALIDATED, NOT A RE-DERIVATION — `predict_today`/backfill call NGBoost's `pred_dist(X).params` verbatim; a point learner (a `Pipeline(StandardScaler, ElasticNet)`) has NO `pred_dist` → AttributeError inside a HALT-tier op. Persist exactly what the bake-off scored (a `HomoscedasticNormalRegressor` = Normal(pred, training-residual-σ̂)); serving the GLM mean beside the NGBoost's σ would re-import the very σ the promotion existed to remove. (c) ⚠️ A MODEL-REGISTRY CHANGE SHIPS WITH THE BOX IMAGE ON MERGE TO `main` (`orchestration_cd.yml` `COPY . .`) — so MERGING THE PROMOTION PR *IS* THE DEPLOY, with NO gate between merge and serve — the OPPOSITE of the API-Lambda deploy-skew rule (which needs an explicit `deploy.sh`). The lineup-monitor then scores that evening's games with the new champion. ⇒ there is currently NO promotion gate; an OPERATOR DECISION (2026-08-02, open) on whether to add one is warranted — it was harmless here only because `best_alpha=0`.** A promotion decomposition rule too: report the LEARNER-SWAP vs FEATURE-BLOCK split — MH2.1's +0.0297 was +0.0175 swap / +0.0122 block, NEITHER clearing the 0.02 noise floor alone, so "plus_eb won" would be false; only their SUM ships.
  - **🎯 PRICING-OPTIMAL ≠ DISCRIMINATION-OPTIMAL (E2.1-r):** NGBoost traded mean SHARPNESS for honest variance (per-side NLL ~2.449 vs ~2.438 for the GBMs). A model picked for CALIBRATION/pricing is not automatically the right pick for EDGE DETECTION — if a later story wants sharp per-side means, re-select on a discrimination metric rather than inheriting the pricing winner.
- **💾 DATA SOURCE = S3/DuckDB, NOT Snowflake (guide §0.5 — HARDENED 2026-06-29 by the E11.1 lakehouse decommission):** ⚠️ **Snowflake is being DECOMMISSIONED to LITERAL-ZERO** (⚠️ CORRECTED 2026-08-19 by E11.24 — there is NO "Cortex-only" carve-out: E11.20 already moved narratives to **AWS Bedrock Nova Micro** (`amazon.nova-micro-v1:0`, 0 `CORTEX.COMPLETE` calls measured in 9 days), so NOTHING is architecturally exempt and literal-zero is a coherent target. ⛔ HALF-ALIVE-PATH TRAP: `scripts/generate_pick_narratives.py` is DEAD but still holds a live `SNOWFLAKE.CORTEX.COMPLETE('mistral-7b')` — the LIVE narrative path (what `generate_pick_narratives_op` runs) is `betting_ml/scripts/generate_pick_narratives.py` (Bedrock); a `grep "is Cortex gone?"` hits the dead script and returns a false "no". W1–W6 done — `mart_pitch_*`, the odds/CLV/team marts, the FanGraphs/posteriors/savant marts, `stg_batter_pitches` etc. now live as **S3 parquet**; W7 drops the remaining Snowflake views + the feature/serving readers). **So every model/data story reads training + feature data from the S3 lakehouse via DuckDB — a Snowflake pull is now a RED FLAG, not a fallback** (the table is at best a thin external-table view today and GONE after W7; there is NO sanctioned PERMANENT Snowflake use — every remaining SF-resident table is pipeline-WRITTEN and on the E11.24 migration path: `prediction_log`→S3 next (P1), then `starter_ip_signals` / the dbt staging CTAS tail / `lineup_state` SCD-2 / `daily_model_predictions` + its 5 consumers, incl. the Bedrock narrative path's read-modify-write. The narrative path still READS/WRITES Snowflake even though Bedrock does the generation). Use the proven lakehouse-read pattern (DuckDB over the S3 parquet, e.g. the `zone_matchup`/W-series scripts, `run_w1_lakehouse` helpers). If you believe a source is genuinely Snowflake-only, STOP and flag it (it's probably an un-migrated straggler → a precursor, not a Snowflake read). Then the cost hygiene still applies: assemble the feature matrix ONCE → parquet, and have every bake-off candidate / ablation / Optuna trial / CV fold read that cached parquet (never re-read per candidate); state the S3 source + parquet path in the handoff.
- **🧬 FEATURE-SELECTION DISCIPLINE (guide §0.5):** feature selection is required but bounded — **pre-register** a hypothesis-driven set of candidate adds/drops (not open subset search), select **in-fold** (never peek at the eval fold), and **count every config toward PBO<0.2/DSR>0** (deflation makes a wide ablation safe). Use the reproducible instruments — `derive_clustered_contract.py` (removal) + `incremental_lift_eval.py` (addition), or Optuna-tuned regularization — not hand-pruning (E1.8's stale-ranking bug). A single contract's miss is **NOT** a trustworthy null; report what was tried + the mechanism.
- **🚂 ~~Railway MCP~~ — LEGACY / DEAD (do NOT use):** Railway was DECOMMISSIONED at INC-16-P2; production runs on **AWS EC2, not Railway**. The Railway MCP is not a live surface — ignore any "use the Railway MCP" advice. (Kept only so the reference isn't silently deleted.)
- **🪪 BOTO3 S3 WRITERS ON EC2 = INSTANCE-ROLE FALLBACK (W7b-1, 2026-06-29 — cost a morning):** the Dagster box authenticates to S3 via its **instance IAM role** (post-INC-16); `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID` is **UNSET** there. **NEVER pass `aws_access_key_id=os.environ.get("AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID")` (or `...secret...`) to `boto3.client`/`Session`** — when the env var is absent you pass `None`, which DISABLES boto3's default credential chain → `AuthorizationHeaderMalformed: a non-empty Access Key (AKID) must be provided`. Pass explicit keys ONLY when present, else omit them so the chain resolves the role (or write via DuckDB `COPY`, which already uses `credential_chain`). This bit all 7 S3 exporters at once; the parity/mirror class of op must also be **mirror-tier (ALERT-continue), NOT HALT**, until its cutover flag flips. (Note: production runs on **AWS EC2, not Railway** — the Railway MCP line above is legacy.) A CI lint now enforces this: `betting_ml/tests/test_boto3_credential_lint.py` (fast gate) FAILS if any `boto3.client`/`Session`/`resource` is constructed with `aws_access_key_id=os.environ.get(...)` (or a possibly-`None` key) — point new writers at `scripts/utils/lakehouse_raw_writer.make_s3_client()` (the shared instance-role-safe helper).

### 🧬 Type-contract guard (INC-19 — the NUMBER↔FLOAT incremental-drift cure) — ENFORCE
The recurring HALT class: a lakehouse dual-branch migration makes an upstream column compute as DuckDB DOUBLE→parquet→Snowflake FLOAT, but a downstream **`incremental` + `on_schema_change='sync_all_columns'`** table still stores it as `NUMBER(38,x)`; Snowflake can't ALTER NUMBER→FLOAT (`002108`) → the op HALTs and an operator must DROP+rebuild. Fired **5×** (INC-15 / W1d / INC-16-P0 / INC-19 / INC-19-recurrence), every victim = `feature_pregame_game_features_raw`.
- **CURE (prevention):** `feature_pregame_game_features_raw` wraps `final` in a generated `-- TYPE-PIN-START … -- TYPE-PIN-END` block that casts **every FLOAT output column to an explicit `::double`** (value-preserving 64-bit; `::float`=32-bit in DuckDB — never use it). The public wrapper `feature_pregame_game_features` casts each `_seasonnorm` column `::double`. So an upstream NUMBER↔FLOAT flip can never change the stored incremental type. The pinned set lives in `dbt/type_contracts/<model>.types.json` (**source of truth**).
- **GUARD (enforcement):** `betting_ml/tests/test_type_contract_guard.py` (fast gate) + `python3 scripts/gen_type_contract.py --check` (wired into the dbt-Build CI gate) go **RED** if a model's TYPE-PIN block drifts from its manifest.
- **🚨 CONVENTION — intended type change ⇒ update the contract in the SAME PR:** when you migrate a model / intend a type change, (1) edit the manifest (move a column in/out of `double_pinned`, or add/remove a column — re-derive truth from `information_schema.columns`), (2) `uv run python scripts/gen_type_contract.py --write` to sync the SQL, (3) commit model+manifest together (guard then passes). If a STORED type actually changed NUMBER↔FLOAT, the operator must **DROP+rebuild** the incremental (`--full-refresh` MERGEs, does NOT DROP — see [dbtf incremental note]). Add a new incremental victim to `CONTRACTS` in `scripts/gen_type_contract.py`. A new numeric column that can ever be FLOAT **must** be `::double`-pinned.
  - **➕ COLUMN ADD ⇒ `--full-refresh`; only a stored TYPE CHANGE ⇒ DROP+rebuild (E7.9, 2026-07-29 — a session called for an unnecessary DROP of two serving-critical incrementals).** For a model that is `delete+insert` / `on_schema_change='sync_all_columns'` (e.g. `feature_pregame_game_features_raw`), simply ADDING a column needs only `--full-refresh` — dbt's `ALTER ADD` already created the new columns at the correct type, no stored type flips, and `--full-refresh` avoids any window where the serving table is absent. **DROP+rebuild is required ONLY when a STORED column's type actually changes NUMBER↔FLOAT** (the INC-19 flip) — that's the sole case `--full-refresh`'s MERGE can't handle. Calling for a DROP on a mere column-add is unnecessary risk on a serving table. (Same session: a `--full-refresh`-less build silently ran only the 7-day incremental window — 116 of 1,637 rows — and went green; a column-add rebuild MUST be `--full-refresh` to touch all history.)

## Pipeline failure-handling contract (E11.7 — ENFORCE on every new op/test)

Three tiers govern how pipeline failures behave. Every new op, cron, sensor, and dbt test is assigned to exactly one tier:

| Tier | Behaviour | When to use |
|------|-----------|-------------|
| **HALT** | raise Exception / exit 1; fails the op/job | Serving-critical path only — `predict_today`, the feature store, serving-mart `dbt run`, the contract-guard, `write_serving_store`, signal freshness gate |
| **WARN-but-continue** | `context.log.warning(...)` + catch, op succeeds | Peripheral data-quality — non-serving ingestion (weather, OAA, bios), `dbt test` suite, user-bet settlement, narrative generation |
| **ALERT-loud-but-continue** | `context.log.warning(...)` or `echo WARNING` to stderr, then exit 0 | Any graceful skip that hides work — missing env var (DBT_RUNNER_URL unset), unreachable runner, no-op ingest; NEVER a silent `print()`/`pass` |

**Rules:**
- No in-process `dbtf` invocations anywhere — all dbt must go through `pipeline/ops/_dbt_exec._run_dbt` (enforces the remote-runner path and hard timeout).
- On `dbt build` days, the serving-critical step is always `dbt run` first (HALT on failure); the test suite is a separate non-blocking step (WARN tier). Never a single `dbt build` that gates predictions on a peripheral test failure (INC-6).
- Any `except` block in ops/sensor/cron files that does NOT call `context.log.warning()` (or write to stderr) is a contract violation.
- `dbt test` severity: serving-critical model contracts stay `error`; peripheral / non-serving data-quality checks use `severity: warn`.

**Op → tier map** (canonical; update when adding ops):

| Op / script | Tier | Reason |
|-------------|------|--------|
| `dbt_daily_build` → run step | HALT | gates feature store & mart rebuilds |
| `dbt_daily_build` → test step | WARN | peripheral data-quality; non-blocking |
| `predict_today_morning` / `lineup_predict` | HALT | primary serving output |
| `write_serving_store_op`, `write_api_cache_op` | HALT | gates **DynamoDB (`credence-prod-serving-cache`) / S3 api-cache** serve (NOT Railway PG — decommissioned INC-16-P2) |
| `signal_freshness_check` | HALT | gates predict on stale inputs |
| `rebuild_sub_model_signals_consumer_op` | HALT (once W8A_LAKEHOUSE_S3=1; else no-op) | INC-25: rebuilds the feature_pregame_sub_model_signals CONSUMER S3 parquet from the fresh W9 stores AFTER export_w9_signals_to_s3_op, BEFORE the freshness gate — post-W8a-cutover the SF consumer reads that parquet |
| `dbt_umpire_feature_rebuild`, `dbt_build_bullpen_posteriors_op` | HALT | rebuild critical feature blocks |
| `ingest_statcast`, `catchup_ingest_statcast`, `catchup_refresh_ext_tables` | HALT | core pitch data; predictions depend. E9.41b: post-W11-E `catchup_ingest_statcast` runs the S3 ingest (`ingest_statcast_to_s3.py`) + `catchup_refresh_ext_tables` (was `catchup_dbt_rebuild`) refreshes the pitch ext tables — both had silently no-op'd since W11-E |
| `ingest_weather`, `ingest_oaa`, `ingest_umpires_early/late` | WARN | non-critical; predictions run without |

INC-38: the op's TIER IS UNCHANGED (a stuck bet must never fail the job), but `_run_settlement` now captures the script's stdout and `_alert_on_stale_pending_bets` PAGES CRITICAL via `send_alert` on `[METRIC] stale_pending_bets>0` — a bet still pending >24h after its game's first pitch. That is the ONLY signal separating "the game isn't over yet" (the benign skip, ~every pass) from "this game's Final will never arrive, so this bet never settles"; the settle loop itself cannot tell them apart, which is how 3 bets sat pending for 2 days behind a clean-looking op. WARN (never silence) on an absent/unevaluable metric — a check that did not run is not a pass (NF1.7 (a)). `betting_ml/monitoring/stale_pending_bets.py` |

Nothing else re-writes a slate's game-detail after its games end — `write_book_odds_op`'s odds-sensor window closes at last first pitch (games freeze mid-'Live'), and the daily serving write is TODAY-only. Terminal leaf after `write_serving_store_op`; failure logs loud, never blocks the daily path |

The archetype flip was a BUG FIX, not a cost lever: its SF table froze 2026-07-05 when the writer went S3-only (W7a), so that entry had printed a false STALE every game day for a month = the FOURTH retired-SF-writer instance. ⚠️ The **Snowflake branch is deliberately RETAINED** (not deleted) as the escape hatch for a future SF-resident feed, with `_DEFAULT_SOURCE="snowflake"` so a source-less entry fails toward the store that certainly exists — but **a single new `source="snowflake"` entry silently re-opens the connection and re-adds this script to the wake queue**, so that is a decision to argue, and it is guarded (`test_e11_24_bundle_freshness_reexports.py`). ⚠️ Each S3 entry is only as fresh as the re-export leaf that feeds it — see the `reexport_*` family row above, and note a fresh deploy needs a one-time mirror PRIME (the weekly `player_profiles_raw` writer would otherwise false-STALE for up to 7 days). `docs/e11_24_check_guard_s3_repoint.md`, `docs/e11_24_bundle_freshness_reexports.md` |

(Limitation: still played-games-only by design → a block NULL *only* on the current unplayed slate is caught by `check_served_prediction_integrity_op`, not here.) **E11.30: pages via `send_alert`** on any DEGRADED block (CRITICAL if a whole-slate date outage is present, else ERROR) — previously log-only in the non-strict default. |

On 2026-08-01 the month-boundary hole built the tail on a July-only universe → weather + public_betting held 0/15 slate games and a front-end panel was blank while the gate read a healthy 0.878 and nothing paged. Runs `scripts/check_w11_tail_coverage.py` (SF-free DuckDB/S3, finite `timeout=` per INC-32) and pages via `send_alert` on the verdict the script already computes: **CRITICAL on BUILD_GAP** (raw feed HAS the slate, built table does not = the INC-37 fingerprint), **WARN on PARTIAL**, **silent on FEED_PENDING/OK/NO_SLATE**, **WARN on unevaluable** (never scored healthy — NF1.7 (a)). ⭐ **TWO RUNS, ONE PER SLATE, and that is the crux:** a BUILD_GAP is a defect only for a block whose feed lands BEFORE the build consuming it. Measured 2026-08-01 UTC — `ingest_action_network` (s4) 12:00 **precedes** the ~12:40 `lakehouse_w11_nightly_op`, but `ingest_weather` (s7) writes `forecast_pregame` at 12:50 and `ingest_umpires.py --date today` (s8/s17) lands 12:0x–16:39, **after** it. Judging all three same-day would page CRITICAL **every morning** on the normal build cadence. Policy lives in `betting_ml/monitoring/w11_tail_coverage.py`; fans out from predict, so a blank panel never withholds a slate. The script's `--strict` stays the OPERATOR's post-rebuild acceptance gate. |
| `check_artifact_freshness_op` (daily) + `artifact_freshness_standalone_op` (off-cycle `artifact_freshness_job`) | ALERT (never HALT — no strict escalation exists) | INC-41: per-artifact freshness SLAs on serving-critical parquet — the guard for the class where a DERIVED artifact stops advancing while its FEED stays healthy, so every source-watching check reads green. On 2026-08-06 `stg_statsapi_lineups_wide` froze for 6.5h; the lineup monitor read it ~40 times reporting "No newly confirmed lineups" and the op returned SUCCESS every 30 min (3 games unscored past first pitch). PR #638 made a *failing* intraday leg page; a build that is SKIPPED / gated off / never scheduled still freezes the artifact silently. Registry + policy in `betting_ml/monitoring/artifact_freshness.py`; reader `scripts/check_artifact_freshness.py` (SF-free DuckDB/S3, finite `timeout=` per INC-32). ⛔ **NEVER an S3 `LastModified`** — `aws s3 ls` prints SHELL-LOCAL time, and #638's atomic server-side copy refreshes the mtime even when the DATA is unchanged, so an mtime check would have read GREEN through INC-41 itself; the timestamp is read from INSIDE the parquet. ⭐ **LAG IS COUNTED ONLY ACROSS EACH WRITER'S DECLARED ACTIVE HOURS** — schedule-capture runs `*/30 14-23` + `0,30 0-3` UTC, a deliberate 10.5h overnight gap in which a "frozen" artifact is healthy (measured 2026-08-07 05:00Z: every schedule-derived artifact sat at 03:30Z, correctly). ⚠️ hour-granularity windows cannot express a 30-min grace: including UTC hour 4 put every artifact at a lag of EXACTLY 90 against a 90 SLA, passing only on a strict `>`. Registered: `stg_statsapi_lineups_wide` (the victim — it carries NO timestamp of its own, so its contract declares an explicit **PROXY**, its co-built sibling `stg_statsapi_lineups`, labelled PROXY in the table/metrics/page rather than silently substituted), `stg_statsapi_games`, `stg_statsapi_probable_pitchers`, `feature_pregame_lineup_features`. ⚠️ `feature_pregame_game_features_raw` was CONSIDERED and REJECTED: its only temporal column `odds_ingestion_ts` measured **40 HOURS** stale on a healthy store (it records when the joined odds were captured, not when the store was built) — a registry entry is a claim about a column's BEHAVIOUR and must be measured, not assumed. Daily op fans out from predict (DOWNSTREAM of the W7b/W8a builds it guards — INC-40); the off-cycle job is what catches an INTRADAY freeze between daily runs. Unevaluable/absent ⇒ WARN, never healthy (NF1.7 (a)) |
| `check_dbt_test_results_op` | ALERT (never HALT — no strict escalation exists) | INC-41 (backend half): pages when a SERVING-CRITICAL dbt test goes red. The daily `dbt test` step is WARN-tier by design (INC-6) — correct, but it means a `not_null` failure on a serving contract surfaces DAYS later in CI to whoever opens the next PR. In INC-41 the test WORKED (went red on the nulled odds price) and nobody was notified: the detection existed, the page did not (E11.30, one layer over — here the detector is a dbt test whose non-zero exit is deliberately swallowed). ⭐ **KEYS ON THE CONFIGURED SEVERITY, which the repo already encodes** (E11.7: serving-critical contracts `severity: error`, peripheral checks `severity: warn` — today 17/69), so it needs no new registry to drift. 🪤 **STATUS IS NOT A SAFE PROXY:** measured on dbt-fusion, a warn-severity failure is `status: "warn"` and an error-severity one is `"fail"` — BUT a test that cannot EXECUTE reports `status: "error"` REGARDLESS of severity, so a status-keyed pager would page CRITICAL every time a PERIPHERAL test broke (the alert-fatigue mode that gets a monitor muted). Severity comes from the manifest (⚠️ fusion stores it UPPERCASE, core lowercase → normalise, never compare verbatim); with no manifest, `fail`→ERROR and `warn`→WARN are inferable but `error` stays UNKNOWN→WARN, never promoted to a page. ⛔ **DOES NOT RE-RUN THE TESTS** — it READS the `run_results.json` the suite already produced; re-running would double the suite AND resume COMPUTE_WH (a new waker; ~80% of SF burn is wake/idle per E11.20-COST, and the E11.24 soak is live). SF-free. ⛔ **DOES NOT GATE PREDICTIONS** — a leaf off `dbt_daily_build` that nothing depends on (pinned on the COMPILED Dagster graph, with a positive control that the probe finds real edges); the test step stays non-blocking, INC-6 is not regressed. 🔌 **THE ARTIFACT IS NOT ON THE OP'S DISK:** `DBT_RUNNER_URL` is always set on the box, so dbt runs in the `dbt-runner` CONTAINER, which shares NO volume with `dagster-codeloc` — `/app/dbt/target/run_results.json` there is a permanently STALE file, not the daily suite's output. Hence the runner's read-only `GET /run_results/{run_id}`, CAPTURED at run time (not read at request time, or a later invocation's overwrite would be served) and 404-ing rather than returning `{}` (which would classify as a clean suite). `_run_dbt` gained an optional `run_ref` OUT-param for the run id — not a return value, because it RAISES on a failed run and a failed run is exactly when the results matter; the other 15 callers are untouched. Provenance is verified before use (`args.command` must be test-bearing; `generated_at` must not predate the invocation) — a `dbt run` artifact holds ZERO test nodes, and "0 failures" from a command that tested nothing is the most dangerous false green (INC-39). ⭐ **`NOT_RUN` ≠ `UNAVAILABLE`:** the full suite runs only on build days, so a no-test day is SILENT (a routine cadence — reporting it as unverified would WARN on most days and train the operator to ignore this); only "the suite ran, results unreadable" is WARN, never healthy (NF1.7 (a)). Policy in `betting_ml/monitoring/dbt_test_results.py` (import-safe per E11.23); fixtures are REAL dbt-fusion output + a committed generator, never hand-written JSON (NF-C0e). NOT in the `check_monitors_healthy` set — that heartbeat watches sensors/schedules that can be STOPPED; this is an op inside the daily job. Docs: `docs/inc41_dbt_test_pager.md` |
| `reexport_player_seq_posteriors_op`, `reexport_team_seq_posteriors_op`, `reexport_matchup_cell_posteriors_op`, `reexport_player_profiles_op` | ALERT (never HALT — no strict escalation exists) | INC-25 mirror-trail family (#693 2026-08-09 + the E11.24 Bundle 2026-08-14). Each re-mirrors ONE table to S3 **downstream of its own writer**, because the daily job mirrors these tables at lk9/lk10 while the sequential writers advance Snowflake ~40 min LATER — so each S3 parquet was permanently one writer-cycle behind (measured: player_seq **38.72h vs a 36h threshold**; team_seq **2.78h / 17 rows**), and `player_profiles_raw` had **no scheduled mirror writer at all** (frozen ~1,133h). ⛔ **The order cannot be swapped** — `update_team_posteriors_op` reads the SF `eb_bullpen_posteriors` copy → needs the ext refresh → runs after lk10 (a genuine cycle, E9.53); the lk9/lk10 mirrors STAY (the `--w8a`/`--w8b` builds read them) and these leaves are ADDITIVE. ⭐ Each is an **unbound FAN-OUT LEAF** — structurally incapable of withholding a slate — wired into **EVERY** job that runs its writer (INC-38: daily_ingestion_job + statcast_catchup_job for the three sequential tables; weekly_player_profiles_job for the profiles one), **ungated** (a flag-gated mirror freezes silently when the flag lapses), with a finite `timeout=900` — INC-32 applies even to a leaf, since `in_process_executor` runs steps ONE AT A TIME topologically, so a hung leaf stalls predict. What they fix is every read between the writer and the next lk9/lk10 — the freshness monitor above all, which is what unblocked `check_data_freshness` going Snowflake-free. ## 🟥 RUNTIME GATE — CI-green is NOT a merge gate for pipeline/serving code (added 2026-06-29 after a string of merged-then-broke runtime bugs)
**The problem this fixes:** CI mocks ALL IO (Snowflake/S3/network) by design → the fast gate + `dbtf compile` CANNOT see the bug class that keeps biting prod — AKID credential-chain, the inline-Snowflake-key gotcha, INC-19 type-drift (only HALTs on a real incremental run), INC-20 OOM, INC-21 (sensor silently not firing), INC-22 (UTC-date). Every one is a RUNTIME / box-environment bug invisible to CI. So "CI green" is necessary-NOT-sufficient and must NOT be treated as "ready to merge" for this code class.
**THE RULE:** for any change touching the **daily pipeline / serving writers / S3 exporters / sensors / ops / date-or-tz logic / boto3 / DuckDB-on-box**, the merge bar = **CI green AND the relevant op actually RAN once on the box** (a scoped run is fine — you do NOT need the full 40-min pipeline; skip the stable W1 pitch rebuild and run only the targeted chain, per the INC-21 recovery). ⚠️ flag-gated dual-branch code is safe merged-OFF (Snowflake arm stays live), but the **un-gated runtime glue runs regardless of any flag** (that's how INC-21/INC-22/AKID hit prod despite cutover flags being off) → that glue is where the real-run gate matters MOST.

### 🧨 RECURRING LANDMINES — READ FIRST before any pipeline/serving/box session (stop re-hitting the same bug across parallel sessions)
