📖 **HOW THE BOX ACTUALLY WORKS → `services/dagster/aws/BOX_OPERATIONS.md`** (docker compose layout + how to exec a script, the inline Snowflake key, host crontab captures, out-of-process dbt-runner, Flaresolverr, deploy.sh/CD + baked-image drift, box facts, box-aware DuckDB memory). Read it instead of re-deriving the mechanics.
- **boto3 on the box:** NEVER `aws_access_key_id=os.environ.get("AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID")` (unset on EC2 → `None` → kills the instance-role chain → `AuthorizationHeaderMalformed`). Use `scripts/utils/lakehouse_raw_writer.make_s3_client()` / DuckDB `COPY` (credential_chain). Lint: `test_boto3_credential_lint.py`. **⚠️ delta-rs/Rust-object_store variant (2026-07-12 box backfill 400):** for delta-rs, even OMITTING the keys from `storage_options` isn't enough — object_store reads the AWS_* env vars ITSELF, and compose `${AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID}` interpolation of an unset host var lands in the container as an EMPTY STRING that it signs with verbatim (empty AKID → 400; botocore skips empty env, object_store doesn't). CURE = `scripts/utils/delta_lake.storage_options()` resolves the botocore chain itself and passes explicit creds; any future Rust-backed S3 client (delta-rs, polars cloud reads, object_store) must do the same.
- **Snowflake auth on the box = INLINE key** (`SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY`, raw/base64), NOT a key-FILE. ✅ FIXED in `snowflake_loader.py`/`data_loader.py`/`ingest_statsapi.py` (INC-22 shared PATH-if-exists→inline→password resolver); use that pattern (or `write_serving_store.py`'s) — never read `SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY_PATH` raw, and stay pure DuckDB/S3 where possible. **🩹 STRAGGLER FOUND 2026-07-03:** `scripts/refresh_w1_external_tables.py` had its OWN file-only resolver (read `SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY_PATH`, else `os.environ["SNOWFLAKE_PASSWORD"]`) → on the box it `KeyError`'d on `SNOWFLAKE_PASSWORD` (both unset). Now delegates to `data_loader.get_snowflake_connection`. ⚠️ If you find ANOTHER script defining a private `get_snowflake_conn`/`_load_private_key`, it's the same straggler — repoint it at the shared resolver. **This fix is NOT live until an `up -d --build` deploy** (baked image); to refresh ext tables on the CURRENT box without a deploy, run `ALTER EXTERNAL TABLE …lakehouse_ext.<t> REFRESH` inline via `data_loader.get_snowflake_connection()` (the Snowflake MCP role CANNOT — DDL `Command` is blocked). Same baked-image caveat hit the coverage guard's new `bullpen_eb` block — the box runs the pre-edit image until rebuilt.
- **💳 STRIPE TEST→LIVE FLIP — FIVE WAYS A BILLING FLIP FAILS SILENTLY, EACH PRESENTING AS A PLAUSIBLE ERROR NOT AN OBVIOUS ONE (E9.8-P2, 2026-08-16, the go-live):** (1) ⭐ **TEST-MODE TRANSACTIONS CONTAMINATE A NEVER-DECREMENTED LIVE COUNTER** — the founding-100 counter read 4 before any real customer, because test-mode Checkouts fired the test-mode webhook which called `increment_founding_slots`, and that counter is deliberately never decremented (a permanent grandfather). The public page already advertised '96 seats left' on 4 test charges. RESET the counter + delete the test customer rows BEFORE the flip; a never-decremented live counter must start at 0. (2) ⭐ **STRIPE CUSTOMER IDS ARE MODE-SCOPED** — a `cus_…` created in test mode exists ONLY in test mode, so a billing-portal click that passes it to a live key 502s; clear test customer ids at the flip. (3) ⭐ **VERIFY THE ENV-VAR NAME THE CODE READS, NOT THE BRIEF'S** — the handler reads `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`; a brief/runbook saying `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SIGNING_SECRET` leaves the REAL var holding the TEST secret → every live webhook fails signature → the handler 503s → Stripe retries for days → cards charged, nobody promoted, first symptom is a customer emailing 'I paid and got nothing'. (4) ⭐⭐ **THE API LAMBDA HAS NO `send_alert` PATH AT ALL, SO A FAILED ENTITLEMENT GRANT IS SILENT** — the Stripe webhook catches every handler exception, logs, and returns 200 (which tells Stripe 'delivered'), so a failed Cognito promotion (IAM/throttle/outage/bug) = customer charged, gets nothing, nobody notified. This is E11.30 ('ALERT-tier quietly meant detected-but-nobody-notified') on the PAYMENT path — detection exists, the page does not. FIX (carded, HIGH): page on a webhook-handler exception + an unresolvable sub, AND a periodic Stripe-active-subs vs Cognito-`subscriber` RECONCILIATION (a drift check catches what an event-time alert can't — the INC-41 artifact-freshness shape on billing). (5) **A LAMBDA ENV FLIP WIPES THE MAP** — `update-function-configuration --environment` REPLACES the whole Variables map and `deploy.sh` (which only calls `update-function-code`) will NOT restore it → one forgotten read wipes every var on the day money starts moving; use a read-modify-write tool (`infrastructure/lambda/set_lambda_env.py`: abort on an empty read, assert every unnamed key survives byte-identical, re-read to prove it landed, keep secrets off argv). ⭐ THE PATTERN across all of these + the mid-flight command bugs (`curl -d` turns a Stripe GET into a POST = the UPDATE endpoint at a live billing object → use `-G`; a `pk_live_` pasted where `sk_live_` belongs; `baseball-access-user` denied `lambda:GetFunctionConfiguration` which `aws lambda wait` calls internally → `deploy.sh` needs the admin profile): every failure produces a PLAUSIBLE symptom (a 401, an `invalid_request_error`, a 200) rather than an obvious one — the G100-D1 'a failure indistinguishable from the healthy state has not been verified' class, on the billing path. Verify each guard by its DENIAL path (the founding counter, the webhook secret, the MFA refusal), never by 'looks enabled'.
- **🚢 THE API LAMBDA HAS NO CI/CD — `git push` DEPLOYS THE FRONTEND AND NOTHING ELSE, so an API RESPONSE-SHAPE CHANGE MUST BE ADDITIVE (NF-C0, 2026-08-01 — a 200 with a blank screen and no error anywhere):** `orchestration_cd.yml` deploys the **Dagster box**; the **API Lambda ships ONLY via a manual `infrastructure/lambda/deploy.sh`**, while `frontend/` auto-deploys on push. ⇒ the two halves of every user-facing feature deploy **independently**, and there is ALWAYS a skew window in both directions. NF-C0 changed `/fantasy/import/sleeper/leagues` from `{user, season, leagues}` to `{season, kind, league}` — **dropping the `leagues` key the deployed client read** → the older frontend called `setLeagues(undefined)`, every render branch skipped, and the user got a **200, no error, and a dead-looking button**. Invisible to CI (both sides pass their own tests in isolation) and to any status-code check. **RULES: (a) a change to a DEPLOYED endpoint's response must be ADDITIVE — add the new key (`kind`), NEVER remove or rename the key the previous client reads, and keep the old key populated in every branch (NF-C0's league branch now returns `leagues: [that league]` so a stale client renders a one-item picker); (b) the CLIENT needs the symmetric guard — `res.leagues ?? []` so a missing key falls through to a VISIBLE empty state instead of rendering nothing; (c) "I pushed" NEVER means the backend shipped — say `./infrastructure/lambda/deploy.sh` explicitly in every handoff that touches `app/backend/`.** Guard: `test_nf_c0_platform_import.py::test_every_branch_carries_leagues_so_an_older_client_still_renders`.
  - **🕳️ …AND THE SKEW BITES THE *REQUEST* SIDE TOO — an un-deployed backend SILENTLY DROPS a newly-added request field (no `extra="forbid"`) → the save round-trips and VANISHES with zero error (E8.6, 2026-08-03).** E8.6's `my_team` picker reverted to "Not set" on save: PR #541 added `my_team` to the `PATCH .../leagues/{id}` request model but the backend wasn't `deploy.sh`'d yet, and the FastAPI/Pydantic request models have NO `extra="forbid"`, so the running Lambda accepted the PATCH, IGNORED the unrecognized field, and returned 200 — the client's optimistic value round-tripped and disappeared with no error anywhere (the NF-C0 skew class, facing the request side instead of the response side). **RULES: (a) adding a request field is the SAME "backend must `deploy.sh` FIRST" hazard as a response change — say it in the handoff; (b) a SAVE surface must show explicit save FEEDBACK (Saving… / ✓ Saved / real error) so a silently-dropped field is VISIBLE, not a phantom revert — E8.6 #544 added exactly this and it's the cheapest guard against the whole silent-save class; (c) this surface (admin Cognito auth) has NO in-session browser click-through, so UI-behavior verification is OPERATOR-manual — CI-green + `tsc`/`next build` do NOT catch a deploy-skew revert.** ⚠️ PROCESS sibling (E8.6): a follow-up commit pushed to an ALREADY-MERGED branch does NOT retroactively join the merged PR — after any merge, re-verify the PR's real state and open a FRESH PR for anything landing post-merge.
  - **🪣 THE FIRST `app/backend` WRITE TO AN S3 BUCKET NEEDS A NEW IAM GRANT — prior grants may be READ-ONLY, the write fails SILENTLY, and CI can't see it (E8.5, 2026-08-03).** E8.5 was the first thing the API Lambda ever WROTE to `baseball-betting-ml-artifacts` — every prior Lambda grant on that bucket (zone overlay, k-projections) was READ-ONLY. Without a new `put-role-policy` grant the write throws, and because the write was advisory-tier (caught + logged, never raised) it failed SILENTLY; CI mocks all IO so it can't catch it either. **RULES: (a) any NEW `app/backend`→S3 WRITE (a bucket/prefix the Lambda only ever read before) needs an explicit IAM `put-role-policy` grant on `credence-prod-lambda-execution-role` — add the exact `aws iam put-role-policy` command to `infrastructure/aws_resources.md` and list it as an operator step in the handoff (same class as the `deploy.sh` skew — an un-granted write is invisible until a live run); (b) an advisory/best-effort write that swallows its exception will hide a missing grant → live-validate the actual write path on the box/prod, never trust CI-green.** ⚠️ Doc bug noted in passing (minor cleanup): `aws_resources.md`'s "Lambda — FastAPI Backend" table mislabels its "Function name" row as the IAM ROLE (`credence-prod-lambda-execution-role`); the real function name (`credence-prod-lambda-api`) only lives in `deploy.sh`.
  - **⏭️ VERCEL SKIPS THE BUILD WHEN `frontend/` IS UNCHANGED — AND ITS EXIT CODES ARE INVERTED FROM INTUITION (2026-08-04):** the worktree-per-session + PR-per-story workflow floods the free tier (every story-branch push = a preview, plus `dev` + `dev→main`), so `frontend/vercel.json` sets an `ignoreCommand` that path-filters on `frontend/`. **Exit `0` = build IGNORED/`Canceled`; exit `1`+ = build PROCEEDS** — backwards from the obvious reading, and inverting it silently freezes every frontend deploy. 🪤 **The Ignored Build Step runs with cwd = the project's ROOT DIRECTORY (`frontend`), NOT the repo root**, so the natural `git diff --quiet HEAD^ HEAD frontend/` resolves to `frontend/frontend/`, matches nothing, and **exits 0 for EVERY commit = a total silent deploy freeze** (measured). Hence the command is repo-root-anchored: `cd "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)" && git diff --quiet HEAD^ HEAD -- frontend/`. ⚠️ **This saves BUILD MINUTES, NOT the deployment COUNT** — Vercel creates the deployment, *then* cancels it (Vercel's monorepo doc: "Canceled builds initiated using the Ignored Build Step **count towards your deployment and concurrent build limits**"). 🔑 **AND A REDEPLOY AFTER AN ENV-VAR CHANGE IS SILENTLY SKIPPED — the trap most likely to bite.** Changing a Vercel env var does NOT change git, so a plain Redeploy re-diffs the SAME commit; if that commit was non-frontend (on `main`, usually) the step exits 0, the redeploy is skipped, and **the new env var never takes effect with no error** — the site just serves the old build. This repo sets `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` / Cognito ids / `NEXT_PUBLIC_ADMIN_EMAILS` / PostHog / Sentry in the dashboard, so it WILL come up. ⇒ on **Redeploy**, untick **"Use project's Ignore Build Step"** (Vercel documents exactly this); same for promoting to prod or any rebuild-without-a-code-change. Rule: **want a build but the commit didn't touch `frontend/`? untick the box.** 🪟 And `HEAD^ HEAD` is a SINGLE-COMMIT window: a MERGE commit diffs against its first parent so `dev`/`main` see the whole PR ✅, but a story branch's preview stops updating on backend-only pushes after its frontend commit — benign, looks like a bug. 🎚️ **The dashboard's native "Skip deployments when there are no changes to the root directory" toggle is ENABLED AND STRUCTURALLY INERT HERE — that's WHY the flood happened despite it looking on.** It requires npm/yarn/pnpm/Bun **workspaces**, and this repo has **no root `package.json` at all**, so per Vercel "changes that are not a part of the workspace definition will be considered **global changes and deploy all applications**" ⇒ every commit deploys. Vercel's own remedy for that case is the Ignored Build Step, so `ignoreCommand` is the documented-correct answer, not a workaround. Leave the toggle on (it errs toward DEPLOYING, never toward skipping, so it can't cause a missed deploy). 💳 **Account moved to Pro 2026-08-04** ⇒ the quota that motivated this (100/day) is gone (6000/day, 450/hr, 500 concurrent) and `git.deploymentEnabled` is MOOT — keep the preview URLs; the remaining benefit is billable Build CPU Minutes. Full rationale + the operator's two-sided live check: `docs/vercel_build_skipping.md`.
- **🔒 A NEW PUBLIC API ROUTE 401s BECAUSE A COGNITO JWT AUTHORIZER SITS PER-ROUTE, OUTSIDE THE REPO IaC (NF3.2, 2026-08-02):** the API Gateway (HTTP API) has a Cognito JWT authorizer applied **PER-ROUTE, configured in the AWS console** — NOT in any `app/backend/` code, `infrastructure/` IaC, or the FastAPI router. So a route that is genuinely public IN CODE (an unauthenticated router with no `require_*` dependency, even guard-tested public) STILL returns **401 at the gateway before the Lambda is ever invoked** — invisible to CI and to a local run. **RULE: any NEW public route must have its authorizer disabled AT THE GATEWAY — `aws apigatewayv2 create-route --api-id <id> --route-key '<METHOD> <path>' --target … --authorization-type NONE` (or set the existing route's authorization-type to NONE).** This RECURS for every public surface (E9.56's public/gated split, NF-C6, any future no-login page) → treat "created a public router" as NOT done until the gateway route is confirmed `NONE`. Route inventory + the fix are in `infrastructure/aws_resources.md`.
  - **🧪 …AND A SECOND *REAL* PAYLOAD FINDS WHAT A SECOND FIXTURE CANNOT (same story):** NF-C0's Sleeper adapter passed 56 tests over a live-verified league, then the operator's OWN league immediately exposed a mapping bug every fixture had missed — Sleeper supports BOTH a coarse `fgm_50p` AND a fine `fgm_50_59`/`fgm_60p` pair, and only the coarse key was mapped, so a league paying 5 for a 50-59 and 6 for a 60+ had **both rules silently dropped to "captured"** despite the catalog having an exact column for each. Fixtures inherit the author's assumptions about which variant a platform emits; a second real account does not. **RULE: for any third-party-payload adapter, validate against ≥2 INDEPENDENTLY-SOURCED real payloads before trusting the field map — a fixture derived from the first payload cannot disconfirm it.** (It also produced the first genuine `exact=False` fold in the whole feature, which is what a real payload is FOR.)
- **📭 A PYDANTIC RESPONSE MODEL SILENTLY DROPS ANY FIELD IT DOESN'T DECLARE (E9.41, 2026-07-19):** `FeaturedYesterday` never declared `status` → FastAPI/Pydantic STRIPPED it on serialize → the recap's Won/Lost COLOR was broken for EVERY settled pick since the field was introduced, even though DynamoDB had it right the whole time. No error — the data is correct in the store, just missing from the API. CI-invisible (mocks IO). **RULE: when a serving surface reads a field the store has but the UI doesn't get, SUSPECT the response model first — a field absent from the Pydantic schema is dropped silently. Any new served field must be added to the response model, not just the writer.**
- **🕳️ AN 'UNMATCHED' / EMPTY STATE THAT RENDERS IDENTICALLY FOR THREE DISTINCT CAUSES MAKES EVERY RECURRENCE RE-INVESTIGATE FROM SCRATCH — and a PROJECTABLE position can VANISH FROM THE PUBLISHED BOARD while every test stays green because the FIXTURES still carry it (NF-C6b / NF-K1, 2026-08-17):** the 2026 published board shipped 795 players QB/RB/WR/TE only — ZERO K, ZERO D/ST — a REGRESSION (the exporter DECLARES `PROJECTABLE=(QB,RB,WR,TE,K,DST)` per NF1.6 + carries a full `_KDST_STAT_KEYS` block; the declaration outran the PRODUCTION, the NF-C0e class). Every test was green because the E2E fixtures still contain 42 K + 32 D/ST → a guard on the code PATH cannot see a position missing from the PUBLISHED artifact. RULES: (a) a PUBLISH-TIME position-coverage guard — REFUSE to publish if any `PROJECTABLE` position has ZERO rows (the NF-D12 rookie-coverage-publish-guard shape; fixtures ≠ production, so guard the ARTIFACT); (b) ⭐ a 'not matched' surface must DISTINGUISH its causes — 'we don't publish a projection for this position right now' vs 'we couldn't resolve this name' vs 'this player genuinely isn't projected' are THREE different facts, and rendering them identically is why the same D/ST symptom cost an investigation TWICE (occ.1 NF-C6P3 = a real join bug, fixed 0/15→15/15; occ.2 = the join is FINE, there are no K/DST rows to match). Making the causes distinguishable converts a multi-hour investigation into a glance. The tell that cracked occ.2: K fails too, and K is a plain name join no D/ST logic touches → check the POSITIONS (does the artifact even contain the rows?) before chasing the JOIN. ⭐ SIBLING (two vacuous gates the RED-proof caught in the SAME story) — **a RANK gate needs a fixture where the two orderings DISAGREE, and an OVERFLOW gate must assert the SCROLLING CONTAINER's `scrollWidth`, not the page's:** 'ranked by best-possible' was VACUOUS because the existing fixture had the same team leading on both readings, so a rank-by-as-set impl passed identically → a `lineupGap` fixture that REVERSES the orderings (same scoring, one team's better roster benched) is what makes the assertion real; and the mobile 'never scrolls sideways' gate pointed at the PAGE-level check, which structurally can't catch a table scrolling inside its own `overflow-x-auto` container (the document stays tidy) → the real assertion is the container's own `scrollWidth`.
- **🔑 THREE WAYS A "DECLARATION OUTRUNS ITS PRODUCTION" — a value the code CLAIMS to serve is silently wrong/absent, and CI can't see it because every mechanism behaves as designed (NF-C0e, 2026-08-04; a live ESPN scoring outage + two post-merge defects, all one shape).** (a) **A WRONG KEY-MAP SHIPS BECAUSE THE TEST RESTATES THE CODE:** an adapter mapped ESPN stat ids onto the WRONG platform keys (`pass_yd` vs canonical `pass_yds`) → every ESPN league scored ZERO yardage since import shipped; unrecognized keys pass through as "captured" by design (no error). It survived because `test_core_scoring_is_applied` asserted `per_stat["pass_yd"]==0.04` — reading the value back under the key THE CODE WROTE. **A test that reads a value back under the key the code writes can NEVER catch a wrong key — it's a restatement of the code, not a test of it (the INC-38/39 vacuous-guard family). Assert the CANONICAL contract key + the verdict of the REAL engine + a cross-adapter agreement guard, never the adapter's own output.** (b) **WIRED ≠ INVOKED:** a projection module was wired into the profile + catalog + both frontend mirrors + the exporter key list — and NOTHING ever CALLED it → it exported null while reporting "captured." **A field is APPLIED when something COMPUTES it, not when its column name appears in the profile/catalog/export map — guard with a CONSUMER_CALLERS registry + an exhaustiveness check that every declared term has a live caller.** (c) **A CACHE KEYED ON THE QUERY *RANGE*, NOT THE QUERY *TEXT*, SERVES A STALE COLUMN SET:** a `team_defense_{lo}_{hi}.parquet` cache written before a SQL change kept returning the old columns → a graduated term published all-NULL. **A stale cache must be impossible to READ, not merely noisy — key it on a SHA of the query text.** ⚠️ (b)/(c) were found ONLY by reading the PUBLISHED ARTIFACT (not code/logs/CI), and the end-to-end verify MISSED (c) because it ran in a FRESH WORKTREE with no cache → rebuilt clean and passed = the board-regen artifact-precedence landmine facing the SHIP direction (to validate an on-disk-artifact/cache fix, COPY THE REAL STALE FILE IN FIRST — a clean checkout cannot reproduce a bug whose trigger is a file it lacks). Also NF-C0e: **`|weight|` is NOT impact — `|weight| × the stat's VOLUME` is** (telemetry ranked `pass_yd` dead-last at 0.04 while it was the most consequential term); and a **degenerate-baseline graduation gate must require BOTH MAE and RMSE** — `fum` won MAE 7/7 but lost RMSE 7/7 (67% zero) = the NF-D11 conditional-median inversion on live data.
- **📥 A RESPONSE MODEL MUST NEVER INHERIT A REQUEST MODEL'S VALIDATORS — a tightened INPUT rule is RETROACTIVE over stored history and 500s the READ path (E9.49, 2026-07-29 — the E9.41 dropped-field class facing the OTHER way).** E9.49 added a `total_line`-required validation to `BetCreate` (the request model); `Bet` (the response model) SUBCLASSED `BetCreate`, so the new create-time rule ran against EVERY stored row on READ → the one legacy no-line bet raised, and because the router built the list in a single comprehension, one bad row **blanked the ENTIRE bet log** (a prod 500 on `GET /bets`). CI-invisible — every fixture was well-formed; no test read prod rows through the response model. **RULES: (a) split the models — a shared `_BetFields` base with NO validators, `BetCreate` owns the WRITE rules, `Bet` (response) owns NONE; a write-time constraint must never gate a read of already-stored data. (b) SERIALIZE ROW-BY-ROW on any list endpoint so one malformed row costs only itself, never the whole collection. (c) add a real malformed stored row as a fixture — a validator tightened on inputs is silently retroactive over history, and only a prod-shaped bad row catches it.** Sibling product rule (same story): **a record without its grading inputs is UNSETTLEABLE FOREVER and looks identical to "not finished yet"** — an O/U bet with no `total_line` sat "Pending" indefinitely; require the grading inputs at WRITE time (API + UI both reject), not at settle time. And: **INC-34 applies to SETTLEMENT, not just serving** — anchoring a user-facing CLOSE on a heavy-daily-rebuilt mart (`mart_starting_pitcher_game_log`) when an intraday-fresh authority exists (`stg_statsapi_games` / the Stats API boxscore) produces a silent multi-day settlement delay no freshness guard sees (the feed is fresh; the derived mart is not) → E9.54.
- **🩹 A "LATEST EVENT WINS" STATE MODEL TURNS ONE MISSED EVENT INTO A PERMANENT WRONG STATE (E9.48, 2026-07-29 — healthy players flagged on the IL for up to FIVE SEASONS):** `feature_pregame_injury_status` calls an interval CURRENT iff `status_end_date is null` — i.e. iff no LATER classified event exists. So a missed CLEARING event is not a one-day blip, it pins the state FOREVER: 62 of the 187 players served as currently-on-the-IL had already PLAYED since their `il_since` (Jesús Luzardo served `il_since=2024-06-19` while starting for PHI on 7/24). Three independent ways the clear went missing, and any ONE of them is enough: **(a) TEXT-PATTERN BRITTLENESS** — the classifier required `'% activated%from the % injured list%'`, but the Stats API very often writes the BARE form ("Tampa Bay Rays activated RHP Drew Rasmussen.") → 21 players; **(b) THE RETURN NEVER USES THE EXPECTED EVENT TYPE** — a player can come off the IL via OPT→CU with no activation `SC` at all (Volpe) → 29; **(c) THE FEED HAS A STRUCTURAL SEASONAL HOLE** — `ingest_transactions.py` runs a 7-day lookback from the IN-SEASON daily job, so the table has **ZERO rows for Nov/Dec/Jan/Feb of EVERY year**, which is exactly when MLB reinstates the 60-day IL en masse (the live API returns 843 IL transactions for 2025-11-01..10 alone) → 12. Invisible to CI (mocks IO), to parity (both branches agreed on the same wrong answer), and to every freshness check (the FEED was fresh; the DERIVED STATE was not). **RULES: (a) never derive a current state from text patterns alone — add a GROUND-TRUTH reconciliation that cannot be reworded away (here: a player who APPEARED IN A GAME is not on the IL; the appearance truncates the injured interval, and closing AT the appearance is the LATEST admissible end so it can only shorten a wrong window, never leak); (b) a "latest event wins" table needs a CONTRADICTION guard, not just a freshness guard — assert the state against reality (`check_injury_status_health.py`: anyone currently-flagged who has since played, tolerance 0), because a stale DERIVED state is invisible to a fresh SOURCE; (c) an ingest whose cadence is seasonal has a SEASONAL HOLE — check `count(*) by month` before trusting any "latest event" over a year boundary.** Sibling: a dual-branch model's ext-table column contract (6 cols here) means a join key added for the fix must NOT reach the final select.
  - **📅 …AND THE UPSTREAM SHIPS TYPO'D DATES THAT POISON BOTH THE ORDERING AND THE GUARD (E9.48-b, 2026-07-29 — found by the off-season backfill):** the MLB Stats API emits transactions whose `effective_date` is a keying error — `2925-11-26` for a `transaction_date=2025-11-26` row; Julio Rodríguez's **2022-10-03 IL PLACEMENT dated 2024-07-23**; a `0205-02-20`. **9 rows in the live table, 7 of them IL PLACEMENTS, and 6 are 2021–2023 IN-SEASON dates — i.e. PRE-EXISTING, not backfill artifacts** (the backfill scan is merely what surfaced them). ⚠️ A first pass looked for years `>2100`/`<1990` and found ZERO, which is how they hid: most typos are a WRONG YEAR that is still plausible (`2021-04-16 → 2023-04-14`), so **screen by the GAP to the sibling date, never by an absolute year range**. One is dated 2 years in the PAST (`2022-08-06 → 2020-08-05`) — hence `abs()`. Two distinct bites: **(a) ORDERING** — the injury model orders events by `event_date`, so a far-future typo sorts LAST, becomes the player's `is_current` interval and pins their status for ~900 years. **Measured on live data: the bound removes 196 net PHANTOM INJURED-DAYS across 7 players — Nick Gordon alone goes 172 injured-days → 1** (his 2021-08-16 placement was dated 2023-05-18, fabricating a ~6-month injured window in a season he was healthy). Those days feed `injured_player_count` / `injury_adj_avg_woba_30d`, so this corrupts TRAINING FEATURES, not just a badge, and it had been doing so since 2021; **(b) IT SILENTLY DISABLES A `max()`-BASED FRESHNESS GUARD** — `max(coalesce(effective_date,transaction_date))` picks the typo, the lag goes hugely NEGATIVE (−328,473d), and the staleness test can never trip, so the check reads **OK on a completely frozen feed**. **RULES: (a) never trust an upstream date as an ORDERING key without a sanity bound — fall back to the sibling date when the gap is absurd (`abs(datediff('day', transaction_date, effective_date)) > 400`), and use `abs()` so far-PAST typos are caught too; (b) a freshness guard must read the newest PLAUSIBLE value, and must treat a FUTURE-dated max as a FAILURE, not a pass — a negative lag means the guard is disabled, which is worse than no guard; (c) bound it rather than hard-failing on the corrupt row, or the guard goes permanently red and nobody reads it.**
  - **🧊 …AND ITS EXT TABLES MAY NEVER HAVE BEEN REFRESHED AT ALL (same story):** `W7B_TABLES` (the injury chain's `lakehouse_ext` tables) was DEFINED in `refresh_w1_external_tables.py` but referenced NOWHERE in the refresh flow — only `W7B_SERVING_TABLES` made the daily set. So `--w7b` rewrote the injury parquet daily while the SF external tables over it (AUTO_REFRESH=FALSE) went stale — the INC-25 "built but never refreshed" shape. **RULE: a `*_TABLES` constant that nothing references is a DEAD refresh, not a configured one — grep for each constant's USE, not its definition.**
- **🦆 DuckDB's DELTA EXTENSION READS S3 CREDS + REGION FROM A `SECRET` ONLY — the deprecated `SET s3_region/s3_access_key_id/…` is an httpfs channel `delta_scan` IGNORES, so it FALLS BACK to ambient env and a REGION mismatch 301s the read to 0 ROWS (INC-45, 2026-08-17; the box K/DST board freeze):** the box's K/DST lake read resolved the RIGHT creds (the botocore chain env→profile→IMDS instance role, `s3io.storage_options`) and handed them to DuckDB through the LEGACY `SET s3_*` settings — a channel `delta_scan` does not read. With nothing on the channel it DOES read, the delta extension resolved region ITSELF from `AWS_DEFAULT_REGION=us-east-1` (the container default), asked us-east-1 for the us-east-2 lakehouse bucket, and got a `301 redirect without LOCATION` on the FIRST `_delta_log/_last_checkpoint` read → 0 rows, dying before a single data byte. The DATA was fine (74 rows since 08-03); the READ channel + REGION were wrong. CURE (ONE owner): `s3io.configure_duckdb_lake_auth`/`duckdb_lake_connection` beside `storage_options()` — a `CREATE OR REPLACE SECRET (TYPE S3, …)` carrying botocore creds (fallback `PROVIDER credential_chain`) AND the region on the secret. This is the pattern `sports_dbt/profiles.yml` ALREADY used to `delta_scan` the SAME bucket on the SAME box every build — 6 call sites had FORKED away from it. ⛔ the tempting one-line fix (`AWS_DEFAULT_REGION=us-east-2` on the container) ALSO breaks paging — SNS defaults to us-east-1, so a global region env puts the SNS client on the wrong region; pin region PER-RESOURCE on the secret, never globally. ⭐ DIAGNOSTIC HEURISTIC (this class recurs): when a reader 'works locally and returns NOTHING elsewhere,' do NOT re-audit whether the CREDENTIALS are correct — ask WHICH CHANNEL the reader actually reads, and find a reader of the SAME store PROVEN in the target environment to diff against. It was structurally UNreproducible off the box (the laptop's ambient region is also us-east-1 but a different object_store build resolves the redirect → 'works on my machine at the same region' was true AND meaningless) and CI-invisible (mocks IO) → the trigger stayed INFERRED until the operator ran a LIVE box capture, which had to happen BEFORE the deploy or the evidence is gone. ⚠️ SIBLING (swallow-and-recompute HIDES a broken read): 3 of the 6 forked call sites (`defense_source`/`contract_source`/`coaching_source`) SWALLOW a read failure + silently RECOMPUTE → the broken channel produced ZERO symptom there, likely for MONTHS; the fix means those reads now succeed for the FIRST time in prod → WATCH the next couple runs for a material projection shift with no data explanation. ⭐ SIBLING (monitoring) — **do NOT add an INC-41 freshness SLA to a DELIBERATELY-STATIC artifact:** the fix added NO scheduled K/DST rebuild + NO SLA — the partition was never missing, K/DST is a base preseason fit that shouldn't change day-to-day, and an SLA on an artifact that SHOULDN'T advance pages daily on a healthy file (the muted-monitor pattern); NF-K1's publish guard (CONSUMABILITY at the moment it matters) is strictly STRONGER than a freshness check on a static file — it froze the board visibly rather than shipping a gap, which is how this was found.
- **🦆 A DuckDB-IN-LAMBDA LAKEHOUSE READ HAS TWO SILENT-FAILURE MODES THAT PRESENT IDENTICALLY AS `degraded:[]`, AND THE FIX THAT UNBLOCKS BOTH IS SURFACING THE ERROR STRING (E5.10, 2026-08-15 — an API-WIDE outage across ~33 `lakehouse_read` sites):** both prop endpoints (and picks/perf/fantasy/parlay/`GET /bets` auto-void) returned `degraded:true, []`, UNDATEABLE because `lakehouse_query` catches everything → `[]` (the E9.26b silent class). Two INDEPENDENT stacked causes: **(1)** the Lambda runtime leaves `$HOME` EMPTY, and DuckDB resolves its extension dir under home → `INSTALL httpfs` raises `IOException: Can't find the home directory at ''` BEFORE any download → cure `SET home_directory='/tmp'` (reproduce locally with `HOME=''`). **(2)** the IAM role had NEVER been granted lakehouse read — a DuckDB `**/*.parquet` GLOB issues a `ListObjectsV2`, a **bucket-level** action, but every prior grant on the bucket was `s3:GetObject` on ONE narrow serving prefix → a 403 the moment httpfs finally installed. ⇒ the first lakehouse GLOB read from the Lambda needs `s3:ListBucket` on the bucket + `GetObject` on the lakehouse prefix (the E8.5 first-S3-access-needs-a-grant family, READ side + the ListBucket subtlety). ⭐ **THE DEADLOCK-BREAKER was the smallest change in the whole story: `lakehouse_query_reason` returns the failure STRING to an authenticated caller** — it named cause 1 in one round trip and cause 2 in the next; before it, the only signal was 'empty' (⇒ a swallowed error must be SURFACEABLE on demand, or an outage is indistinguishable from a quiet day). ⭐ **SIBLING — a SERVING POPULATION must not be driven by a VOLATILE/OVERWRITTEN feed:** the TB batter list was driven by the live props feed (`--mode live` OVERWRITES hourly), so a game that STARTED dropped out of the feed and took all its batters with it = the 'can't see started games' symptom; rebuild to draw the population from a STABLE source (posted lineups ∪ EB posteriors) with the volatile feed as optional ENRICHMENT. ⭐ **SIBLING — a table with NO SCHEDULED WRITER is a silent-staleness bomb: `stg_ref_players`** was a one-shot MANUAL export (referenced by no op/schedule/workflow), 53 days stale, holding ZERO 2026 players → a writer that named batters from it skipped 34; grep every CONSUMER (~11 here) and either schedule the writer + an INC-41 freshness SLA or repoint consumers to a live source (INC-27 / NF-INFRA1 gitignored-read family).
- **🐘 A WIDE-TABLE LAKEHOUSE READ SILENTLY FAILS INSIDE THE API LAMBDA → `lakehouse_query` swallows → `[]` → the page zeroes (E9.26b, 2026-07-18):** the Model-Skill query `LEFT JOIN`ed the 94-col `daily_model_predictions`; that wide read reliably FAILS in the API Lambda (works on the exact DuckDB 1.2.2/256MB/real-S3 OUTSIDE it) and `lakehouse_query` CATCHES-and-returns-`[]` → the panel was empty since ship day, un-reproducible locally, no CloudWatch on the swallowed error. **RULE: compute a serving tally from the NARROWEST mart that answers it (`mart_clv_labeled_games`, not the 94-col predictions table); make any wide/fragile read a SEPARATE best-effort refinement that can't zero the base result; run the fragile read AFTER the reliable one (a failed read on the shared DuckDB singleton can take down a later query).** A `lakehouse_query` returning `[]` in a serving endpoint = the silent-empty class → treat an empty result as SUSPECT, never cache/serve it (the anti-freeze guard).
- **🪶 LEAN CAPTURE IMAGES MUST BE SELF-CONTAINED — do NOT delegate their SF resolver to `betting_ml` (2026-07-05, odds-freshness CRITICAL):** the `services/*_capture/` images (odds/weather/schedule/derivative) are built *without* `betting_ml`/`pandas`/dbt on purpose — each COPYs only 1–2 "self-contained" scripts. So the INLINE-key straggler fix above has a **critical exception here**: a script that runs in a lean capture image must resolve Snowflake auth **inline** (its own PATH-if-exists→inline `SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY`→password via `cryptography`, mirroring `data_loader._load_private_key`), NOT `from betting_ml.utils.data_loader import get_snowflake_connection` — that `ImportError`s every cron fire (no `betting_ml`; and `data_loader` imports pandas) → the capture silently stalls → stale odds/weather/derivative. A blanket straggler-sweep did exactly this to 3 of the 4 images. Reference cure: `scripts/odds_api_ingestion.py::get_snowflake_connection` (+ `_load_private_key_der`). **Guard:** `betting_ml/tests/test_lean_capture_images_selfcontained.py` (fast gate) FAILS if any script a `*_capture/Dockerfile` COPYs imports `betting_ml`. ⚠️ **Same class — a shared env leaking into a lean image it can't honor:** `LAKEHOUSE_RAW_WRITE_MODE=both` (in `.env`, for the full-container writers) reaches odds-capture via `env_file` and made it attempt an S3 mirror it *couldn't* do (the image installed no boto3/pyarrow, copied no `scripts/utils/`) → `No module named 'utils'` every run. **Resolution (2026-07-05, the Snowflake-decommission-correct one): make odds capture S3-NATIVE, not SF-only** — the decommission goal is to REDUCE the SF footprint, and pinning it SF-only would keep `oddsapi.mlb_odds_raw` alive purely to be re-mirrored. So: added boto3+pyarrow + `COPY scripts/utils/lakehouse_raw_writer.py` to `services/odds_capture/Dockerfile`, set `LAKEHOUSE_RAW_WRITE_MODE: s3` + `AWS_DEFAULT_REGION: us-east-2` on the odds-capture service, and **RETIRED the `export_odds_raw_to_s3.py` mlb_odds_raw cron** (with the SF write dropped, that bridge would re-mirror the now-FROZEN SF over the fresh S3 = clobber). Safe because the serving path already reads `lakehouse_raw/mlb_odds_raw/` (stg_oddsapi_odds, mart_bookmaker_disagreement, the W12 freshness sensor); also removed the SF-table `mlb_odds_raw` entry from `check_data_freshness.py` (would false-warn on the frozen table). **General rule: when a shared write-mode env reaches a lean capture image, either make the image able to honor it (boto3+pyarrow+writer) — preferred, S3-forward — or override it per-service; NEVER leave it attempting an op the image can't do.** (derivative/weather-capture were already S3-capable, so they stay `both`.)
- **🩺 HEALTHCHECK PROBES CHECK REACHABILITY, NOT A VENDOR RESPONSE STRING (2026-07-05):** `services/dagster/aws/healthcheck.sh` probed flaresolverr with `curl :8191/ | grep -qi flaresolverr` — when Byparr replaced EOL FlareSolverr (INC-26) it stopped echoing the literal "flaresolverr" (and its `/` may not 200), so the probe FALSE-PAGED CRITICAL every run while Byparr was `Up (healthy)` + pulling rows. **A liveness probe must assert a 2xx on a health endpoint (`/health`, fall back to `/`), never grep a vendor-specific response body** — the body changes when you swap the backing image. Fixed to `curl -fsS :8191/health || curl -fsS :8191/`.
  - **🕳️ …AND THAT EXACT CURE CAUSED A 7-DAY SILENT FANGRAPHS OUTAGE — `curl -f` DOES NOT ASSERT A 2xx, IT ONLY FAILS ON ≥400, SO A REDIRECT PASSES IT (2026-08-03; the entry above SAYS "assert a 2xx" and the code it shipped never did).** Byparr's Camoufox browser stopped launching (`BrowserType.launch: Connection closed while reading from the driver`, raised inside FastAPI's `solve_dependencies`→`get_camoufox` — i.e. during DEPENDENCY SETUP, before the route handler ever runs, hence a bare `Internal Server Error` body rather than a structured solver error). `/health` began returning 500 — but the `||` fell through to `/`, which Byparr answers **`301 Moved Permanently`**, and `curl -f` exits **0** on a 3xx ⇒ the healthcheck reported GREEN for SEVEN DAYS while every FanGraphs ingest 500'd. **THREE independent layers each failed to surface it and each needed its own fix: (a) the probe (above); (b) the running-set check treats an `unhealthy` container as fine — it IS still "running"; (c) `restart: unless-stopped` reacts ONLY to a process EXIT, and the FastAPI server never exited, so the container sat `Up 3 weeks (unhealthy)` with `restarts=0`. Docker KNEW the whole time and nothing asked it.** CURES (all shipped): the probe now compares the real status code and requires `2??`; a health-status loop reads `.State.Health.Status` per core service (`starting` is NOT a failure; a service with no HEALTHCHECK reports `none` and is SKIPPED, never scored healthy — NF1.7 (a)); and a label-scoped `autoheal` container restarts anything labelled `autoheal=true` (byparr ONLY, so it can never bounce Dagster/Postgres mid-job) — with `autoheal` itself in `CORE_SERVICES`, because a watchdog nobody watches reproduces the outage it exists to prevent. Guards: `betting_ml/tests/test_byparr_solver_hardening.py`, RED-proven against the pre-fix source, incl. a fixture proving a COMMENT claiming "2xx" cannot satisfy the guard (the INC-38 prose-cannot-satisfy lesson).
    - **🔎 TRIAGE IN THIS ORDER — the first two steps eliminate whole families and are the ones a session gets wrong.** (1) ⭐ **a direct laptop `curl` of the FanGraphs API returning `403` + `cf-mitigated: challenge` + "Just a moment…" is the HEALTHY, EXPECTED state** (it is precisely what the solver exists to get past) ⇒ if you see that, FanGraphs is FINE and the fault is ours. (2) **`scripts/utils/fangraphs_client.py` names WHICH SIDE failed BY LINE**: a traceback at `r.raise_for_status()` (~line 208) = **Byparr itself** 500'd; a FanGraphs-ORIGIN 5xx instead surfaces from the `solution.status` check (~line 218) as "upstream returned HTTP N". Different lines, different causes — never chase Cloudflare on a line-208 failure. (3) `docker compose logs flaresolverr` names the exception. (4) `docker restart flaresolverr` cures the browser-death class **instantly** — it had run clean for 16 days first, so this is IN-CONTAINER STATE DEGRADATION on a **~2-3 WEEK CLOCK; EXPECT RECURRENCE** (ruled out at the time: disk 81% with 5.9G free, `dmesg` clean of OOM, `/dev/shm` 0% used). (5) Verify by **CAPABILITY, never reachability**: `docker compose … exec -T dagster-codeloc python services/dagster/aws/validate_flaresolverr.py` → "OK: Cloudflare clearance obtained — pulled N rows". ⚠️ that validator lives at **`services/dagster/aws/`**, NOT `scripts/` as the compose comment claims.
    - **🧪 TWO MEASUREMENT TRAPS WHEN VERIFYING A WATCHDOG — BOTH PRODUCED A CONFIDENT, WRONG "IT'S BROKEN" VERDICT (2026-08-03).** (1) **`RestartCount` does NOT increment on an API/manual restart** — it counts RESTART-POLICY restarts only, so `restarts=0` proves nothing about a watchdog that calls `POST /containers/<id>/restart`; **use `.State.StartedAt`** (it advanced 01:56:56→02:00:05, which is what finally proved autoheal works). (2) **autoheal computes its `$DATE` ONCE per loop iteration**, so identical timestamps on "found to be unhealthy" and "…failed" do NOT mean instant failure — 30s had actually elapsed. ⭐ And the real defect those traps hid: **`CURL_TIMEOUT` MUST EXCEED `AUTOHEAL_DEFAULT_STOP_TIMEOUT`** — autoheal runs `curl --max-time $CURL_TIMEOUT … -f -X POST …/restart?t=$STOP_TIMEOUT` and the image DEFAULTS `CURL_TIMEOUT=30`, EQUAL to our stop timeout, so a container that ignores SIGTERM (normal — **a container's PID 1 gets NO default signal handling**) burns the full window, curl aborts, and autoheal logs "failed" **while the daemon completes the restart anyway**. The tell is the attempt cadence: a steady **90s** apart = `INTERVAL 60` + the 30s timeout. Left unfixed it makes a SUCCESS and a genuine FAILURE indistinguishable in the log — the same non-discriminating-output class as the `curl -f`/301 bug above. ⚠️ Both third-party box images are DIGEST-pinned (a version tag is re-pushable), and **`willfarrell/autoheal` 1.2.0 publishes arm64 while 1.0.0 / v0.7.0 are amd64-ONLY** — the box is Graviton (`r6g.large`, aarch64), so "roll back to an older tag" is NOT a safe recovery.
    - **🔕 WHAT ACTUALLY CAUGHT IT WAS A FRESHNESS SLA ON THE DERIVED DATA, NOT ANY LIVENESS PROBE.** Every infra check was green for the whole week; the outage surfaced only because E7.6's `scripts/check_milb_coverage_sla.py` reported `the_board` + `fg_leaderboards` at **7d stale against a 3d SLA** on its first real run. ⇒ for any WARN-tier feed (a failure that is correctly non-fatal and therefore scrolls past unread), the durable detector is a **per-feed staleness check on the LANDED DATA** — a probe asking "is the service up" structurally cannot see a service that is up and producing nothing.
- **dbt-runner HTTP call needs the auth header:** a manual `POST {DBT_RUNNER_URL}/run` from `dagster-codeloc` must send `Authorization: Bearer $DBT_RUNNER_AUTH_TOKEN` (both on `/run` and `/status/{id}`) or it 401s — `pipeline/ops/_dbt_exec._run_dbt_remote` is the reference. Body: `{"args": [...], "env": {}, "use_state": false}`.
- **DuckDB S3 region = `us-east-2`** for `baseball-betting-ml-artifacts` (explicit; boto3 is region-less but DuckDB needs it).
- **🕐 `aws s3 ls` PRINTS `LastModified` IN THE SHELL's LOCAL TIME, NOT UTC (E11.20 phase-2a, 2026-07-25 — cost a session a false alarm):** the box/laptop shell runs in local time (e.g. CDT), so an `s3 ls` mtime compared against `date -u`/UTC looks ~5–6h STALE → a false "the intraday build FROZE at 18:00" alarm when the parquet was actually fresh. **RULE: never diff an `aws s3 ls` LastModified against a UTC clock to judge freshness — verify the parquet CONTENT (max game_date / row counts / a data timestamp column), not the `ls` mtime.** (Use `aws s3 ls --recursive` + read the object, or `date` in the same TZ, if you must compare timestamps.)
- **TZ/date = the US baseball-day, NOT UTC** (the box runs UTC → UTC "today" rolls to tomorrow after ~00:00 UTC = evening US time = INC-22, a silent empty-serve). **Use `betting_ml/utils/game_day.py::current_game_date()` (`America/Los_Angeles`, injectable clock) / `current_game_date_iso()`** — the canonical helper routed through ~22 serving/predict/ops/router sites; never `date.today()`/`utcnow().date()` in a serving path.
- **🕰️ `LTZ::timestamp_ntz` SILENTLY SHIFTS BY THE SESSION OFFSET — comparing it against a UTC-stored `TIMESTAMP_NTZ` costs you 7 HOURS, and the display column still looks right (E11.20 phase-2b, 2026-07-29 — SIX DAYS of slate-wide false abstains, ZERO actionable picks shipped 7/24→7/29):** `predict_today`'s serving-freshness gate compared `information_schema.tables.last_altered` (**`TIMESTAMP_LTZ`**) against `stg_statsapi_probable_pitchers.ingestion_ts` (**`TIMESTAMP_NTZ` storing UTC**). Casting the LTZ side `::timestamp_ntz` renders it as session-local wall time (`America/Los_Angeles`) and *then* takes the epoch → the PT↔UTC offset is silently subtracted → `lag_min` = true lag **+ 420 min** (measured: 463.95 as-coded vs 43.95 true) → the 3h staleness threshold trips on a **perfectly fresh** feature store, and the gate abstained the actionable edge on every game, every day. ⚠️ **`to_varchar(max(last_altered)::timestamp_ntz,…)` renders a completely plausible timestamp — the display column CANNOT reveal this.** CI-blind (mocks IO) and parity-blind (the S3 twin was correct the whole time). **RULES: (a) NEVER mix `TIMESTAMP_LTZ` and UTC-stored `TIMESTAMP_NTZ` in one arithmetic comparison — take the epoch of the LTZ value DIRECTLY (`date_part(epoch_second, last_altered)`, already a true UTC epoch); a `::timestamp_ntz` cast on an LTZ column is a tz CONVERSION, not a type tidy-up. (b) When a gate has two branches (SF + S3), assert the branches AGREE on the same slate — a single-branch unit test cannot catch a SQL tz bug. (c) A boolean serving GATE needs a live two-sided proof: score a known-fresh input and assert PASS, not only that a known-stale input FAILS.** 🪞 **The trigger was a fix un-masking the bug:** the 7/23 retired-source repoint of `stg_statsapi_probable_pitchers` to `lakehouse_ext` un-froze `ingestion_ts` **and moved it to UTC**; while it was frozen the lag was hugely negative and the gate passed. ⇒ **repointing a staging model to the lakehouse can change a timestamp's TIMEZONE as well as its freshness — audit every downstream comparison on that column, not just its values** (sibling of INC-23/INC-34: a cutover invalidates an ANCHOR, not the data).
  - **📅 …AND A `>=` DATE-STRING BOUNDARY ON AN LTZ COLUMN PRUNES ~7h SHORT — the 4TH LTZ/NTZ bug, tripped while AUDITING the 3rd (E11.20 close-out, 2026-07-31):** a wake census got two different counts from the IDENTICAL filter — `where convert_timezone('UTC', timestamp) >= '2026-07-20'` returned 100 resumes when the range started 7/20 but 125 when it started earlier. Cause: `warehouse_events_history.timestamp` is `TIMESTAMP_LTZ`, so the predicate is PARTITION-PRUNED against the RAW column in the session tz → the boundary lands at 07:00 UTC, not 00:00, silently dropping UTC hours 0–4 of the first day (exactly the 25 missing events). ⭐ **Only the RANGE'S FIRST DAY is affected**, so a multi-day census is fine but **any single-day wake/credit reading taken with a `>=` date-string boundary on an LTZ column is ~7h short — which reads as a FAKE IMPROVEMENT.** RULES: filter LTZ on the RAW column (or cast the boundary to LTZ), OR bound the range WIDER than the days you actually report, and never let a boundary day be a reported day. ⚠️ This codebase has now produced FOUR distinct LTZ/NTZ confusion bugs — once you've found one +7h, every 7-ish number looks like the same bug (a false-positive already reverted once, `check_data_freshness.py`), so confirm the column's tz at the WRITER before "fixing."
  - **🧮 A STORED RUN-LEVEL CLI ARGUMENT MASQUERADES AS A PER-GAME FLAG → a monitor built on it can NEVER reach 100% (E11.20 close-out, 2026-07-31):** `daily_model_predictions.lineup_confirmed` is the RUN-LEVEL `--lineups-confirmed` CLI arg, stamped IDENTICALLY on every game in the run, while the posterior is gated by the PER-GAME `_lineups_confirmed` check. So `lineup_confirmed=true` does NOT mean THAT game's lineup posted, the "eligible" denominator built on it is OVER-INCLUSIVE, and `with_edge == eligible` is structurally unreachable (a partial-lineup game sits in the denominator with a NULL posterior forever). RULE: for a serving monitor, discriminate on the PER-GAME state (is the NULL slate-wide or per-game?), never on a stored run-level column; and know which suppressor you're measuring — a freshness abstain is SLATE-WIDE (computed once per run, outside the per-game loop → can only ever be `with_edge=0`), a lineup-readiness null is PER-GAME. Also: `h2h_edge` is a PRESENCE flag (identically `0.0` on every row every day because `best_alpha=0`) — read `is not null`, NEVER the value.
- **🕒 SNOWFLAKE MISREADS BINARY PARQUET TIMESTAMPS (W8a 24h outage — WILL recur in any feature/mart S3 migration):** a Snowflake external table reads an INT64 binary parquet `TIMESTAMP` at the wrong scale PER-ROW → a 2026 micros value materializes as year ~56,000,000 → connector overflow (`252005`) on fetch/CTAS/dbt-materialize. ⚠️ `min/max(year())` + `to_varchar(min())` read from parquet COLUMN STATS → look FINE → the bug only shows on a real ROW. CURE: store every `TIMESTAMP*` as ISO **VARCHAR** in the DuckDB build (`run_w1_lakehouse._string_timestamp_wrap`) + emit the ext-table col as `<COL> TIMESTAMP_NTZ AS (VALUE:col::TIMESTAMP_NTZ)` (string parse). `DATE` (INT32) reads fine.
- **🔁 THE VARCHAR-TIMESTAMP CURE HAS A DOWNSTREAM BITE (INC-23):** because the cure stores every `TIMESTAMP*` as ISO VARCHAR in parquet, any DOWNSTREAM model doing a date-fn / interval-math / timestamptz-COMPARISON on that column hits the DuckDB binder (`year(VARCHAR)`, `VARCHAR < timestamptz`). **CURE = cast AT THE USE-SITE: `::date` for date math/`year()`, `::timestamptz` for comparisons (NOT `::date` if you need first-pitch time — that collapses to midnight + breaks CLV).** Output stays VARCHAR → ext-table contract unchanged. `_string_timestamp_wrap` now **HALTs (raises) on a DESCRIBE failure** instead of silently shipping unwrapped binary ts (guard: `test_timestamp_wrap_guard.py`). ⚠️ 3 SF-native edge models (`feature_edge_book_market_era_quality`/`_sharp_anchor_backtest`/`feature_pregame_edge_market`) carry the same bare date-compare pattern — fix when they migrate to DuckDB.
- **🔀 EXT-TABLE `TIMESTAMP_NTZ` ≠ THE DuckDB TYPE — the `--s3` serving path fails SILENTLY (INC-23 generalization, 2026-07-05):** in the S3 lakehouse views, SOME timestamp cols are string-wrapped (→ VARCHAR in DuckDB, per the binary-ts cure) and SOME are real parquet TIMESTAMP. Any date-fn / comparison MIXING the two HALTs **only in `--s3` mode** — and `write_serving_store --s3` CATCHES-and-CONTINUES, so 2 whole sections (game-detail drilldown + teams page) were silently empty on every daily run. Invisible to CI AND to Snowflake-native runs AND to parity-over-parquet. **CURE: before repointing ANY `--s3` consumer, audit EVERY timestamp/date comparison vs `information_schema` parquet types + cast the VARCHAR ones at the use-site (`::date`/`::timestamp`).** The only known VARCHAR ts cols today: `stg_statsapi_games.game_date` + `mart_odds_outcomes.commence_time` + `mart_game_odds_bridge.game_date` / `mart_game_spine.game_date` / `mart_odds_line_movement.game_date` (all their comparisons now cast). A silent caught-and-continue in a serving writer is the tell.
  - **🎰 …AND AN EQUALITY COMPARE DOESN'T EVEN RAISE — IT SILENTLY MATCHES NOTHING (E9.52, 2026-07-29; the TARGET BOOK's price 100% NULL for 5 days):** the INC-23 note above is about comparisons that **HALT**. The worse variant is **VARCHAR = VARCHAR**, which is perfectly legal: `predict_today._BOVADA_ML_QUERY` filtered `where game_date = %(d)s` on `mart_game_odds_bridge`, whose `game_date` is a string-wrapped TIMESTAMP → `'2026-07-25 00:00:00' = '2026-07-25'` → **0 rows, no error**, so the graceful `except` never fired and `layer4_h2h_bovada_ml_home/away` (the price every actionable H2H pick is graded at) wrote NULL for the whole slate. **It kept working on Snowflake** (that ext table declares GAME_DATE `TIMESTAMP_NTZ`, which coerces the literal), so the break landed the day each serving tier flipped to `--s3` — `morning` at the `W7B_LAKEHOUSE_S3` flip, `post_lineup` on 7/25–7/26 at `W7B_INTRADAY_S3` = the observed 7/24→7/25 boundary. Invisible to CI (mocks IO), to parity (never goes through the ext table), and to every freshness/coverage guard: raw capture, `mart_odds_outcomes`, the bridge attach (`has_odds`) and `h2h_market_implied_prob` were **all 100% healthy**. **RULES: (a) an un-cast `=` against a wrapped-timestamp column is a SILENT EMPTY, not a HALT — treat "0 rows and no error" as the tell, and grep for `game_date = ` (equality!) as well as date-fn/`<` comparisons when repointing a consumer; (b) a per-BOOK price needs its OWN coverage guard — a bridge-attach guard proves an event was linked, NOT that the target book priced it; (c) SELECT THE SLATE AND LEFT JOIN THE PRICE so "the book didn't price it" is distinguishable from "the predicate matched nothing" (an INNER JOIN makes the two identical).** Guards: `betting_ml/tests/test_target_book_coverage_guard.py` (source-inspection + a DuckDB proof the pre-fix predicate matches nothing) + `check_served_prediction_integrity.py` check #5 (ALERT-tier target-book coverage on the served slate).
    - **🃏 SECOND DEFECT IN THE SAME READ — `GROUP BY` COLLAPSES THE GROUP, SO A TRAILING `QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY <the group key>)` IS A NO-OP (E9.52):** the read's `MAX(case when is_home_outcome ...)` was `GROUP BY event_id` with a `QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY event_id ORDER BY MAX(ingestion_ts) DESC) = 1` that **looks like "take the latest snapshot" but selects row 1 of 1** → each side carried the **most favourable price ever posted**, mixed across snapshots and including IN-PLAY quotes (game 823601: home **+900** / away **+100** — both positive = no single real quote; the true last pre-game pair was −130/+100). That INFLATES kill-criterion ROI, which is worse than NULL because it looks like data — 2,101 stored rows (662 impossible pairs) since the live capture densified in 2026-05. **RULE: to take "the latest price" you need a snapshot-ALIGNED read — the latest `ingestion_ts` HAVING both outcomes present, plus a `ingestion_ts < commence_time` pre-game bound (`_BOOK_ODDS_BATCH` in `write_serving_store.py` is the reference). A both-positive / both-negative American pair is the mixed-snapshot smell; assert it can't happen.**
- **🔑 INLINE-KEY LINT MUST CHECK THE SCRIPT PARSES THE KEY, NOT JUST MENTIONS IT (INC-22 hardening, 2026-07-05):** the first `test_snowflake_resolver_lint.py` only checked a script *references* `SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY` — but `settle_user_bets.py` mentioned it yet its own PEM parser never unescaped `\n` → `InvalidByte(0,92)` crash on the box anyway. **The blessed inline-key parser is `data_loader.get_snowflake_connection` — scripts must DELEGATE, not re-implement.** The tightened lint flags any script calling `load_pem_private_key` itself without delegating OR unescaping `\n`; it caught 3 more latent time-bombs (`write_api_cache.py` HALT-tier, `generate_pick_narratives.py`, `migrate_placed_bets_to_dynamo.py`). Rolling your own SF auth = a box time-bomb; delegate.
- **💸 SNOWFLAKE COST = WAREHOUSE WAKE/IDLE, NOT QUERY COMPUTE (E11.20-COST, 2026-07-16 — overturned the whole "reads are the burn" premise):** on an X-Small warehouse, of ~4.5 metered credits/day only ~0.8 was actual query compute — **~80% was wake/idle burn from tick chains keeping the warehouse awake 24/7** (even zero-game days). ⇒ **to attribute SF cost, measure WAKE FREQUENCY (which chains resume the warehouse how often), NOT elapsed-seconds** — the elapsed-seconds profile made the pregame feature family LOOK like the credit target when it bills ~0.03 cr/day (it's a RUNTIME target, not a credit one). Cost fixes = stop the WAKERS (dedupe double-firing ticks, horizon-gate 24/7 subprocesses so they skip when no game is near, `--s3` any serving read still hitting SF), not migrate the heaviest-elapsed query. Any "this mart is the SF-credit target" claim must be backed by a `query_history`/metering WAKE attribution, not elapsed-seconds seeding.
  - **⏱️ …BUT A LEVER THAT REMOVES A 24/7 EVENLY-SPREAD POLLER IS JUDGED ON ACTIVE-MINUTES, NOT RESUMES (E11.24 stage-1, 2026-07-31 — the refinement that keeps you from under-crediting the levers this story is built on):** killing an hourly weather/ELO poller deleted `COMPUTE_WH` awake-MINUTES 167→141 (−16%) while RESUMES stayed flat (44→43) — because the remaining BURSTY pipeline work re-wakes the warehouse anyway, so the poller's contribution was never in the resume COUNT, it was in the awake-TIME between bursts. ⇒ **report BOTH wake-frequency AND distinct active-minutes: a WAKER (bursty tick chain) shows in resumes; a POLLER (evenly-spread 24/7 query) shows in active-minutes. Judging a poller-removal on resumes alone credits it ZERO.** Sum-of-elapsed-seconds is STILL the wrong instrument (it moved the WRONG way, 62.9→79.9 min, on a lower query count). Verify a gated lever by a QUERY-SHAPE that stops in `query_history` (proves image-carries-code + flag-set + path-taken at once — stronger than `env | grep`), cross-checked against total executions so "shape went to zero" isn't confused with "job died"; normalize whitespace in the classifier (a raw `query_text` match across a newline gives a false zero).
    - **🔥 …AND A NO-OP-REFIRE GATE IS JUDGED ON THE *FIRE COUNT*, BECAUSE AN OUTAGE FAKES EVERY VOLUME METRIC (E11.24 lever 1b, 2026-08-01 — verified on an INC-37 day, which is exactly why it needed a third instrument).** 1b gates a chain that re-fires hourly until upstream data lands. Its 08–13 UTC band showed resumes 22→11, waits 24→15, active-min 86→37, executions 2,568→877 — but **8/1's total `COMPUTE_WH` executions were ~1,820 against a typical 4,000–6,000 because INC-37 had broken the day**, so every VOLUME metric fell regardless and a volume reading would have credited the gate with an outage's work. **The statistic an outage cannot fake is "in how many distinct hours did the chain run at all" — 5–6 every morning for nine consecutive days → 2.** ⇒ **for a gate that suppresses REPEATED no-op invocations, count INVOCATIONS (distinct fire windows), not queries/waits/minutes; and check whether the measurement day was structurally normal before crediting any volume delta** (the sibling of "a shape going to zero is also what a dead job looks like", one level up: a whole DAY can be the dead job). Also from that reading: **a BURST-removing lever moves RESUMES while a POLLER-removing lever moves ACTIVE-MINUTES** — 1b was the first lever in E11.24 to move resumes at all, so reporting a single instrument systematically under-credits one lever class or the other.
    - **🔎 THREE WAYS A query_history WAKER-BOARD MEASUREMENT LIES — SCHEMA IS THE SESSION SCHEMA NOT THE RELATION, UN-NORMALIZED LITERALS FRAGMENT A FAMILY INTO `other`, AND A VIEW-PR RUN IS ALL NULL-SCHEMA (E11.24 t4+6, 2026-08-17):** (1) ⭐ `query_history.schema_name` is the statement's SESSION schema, NOT the relation it touched — dbt-fusion issues its test queries with NO session schema, so they land `schema_name=NULL` while naming `baseball_data.ci_betting…` only in the SQL TEXT. A schema-keyed filter/guard therefore misses ~78% of dbt statements (caught 14 of 64 CI billable) — and it FALSE-PASSES on the COMMONEST run shape: a `state:modified+` PR touching a VIEW model materializes nothing billable (a `CREATE VIEW` is cloud-services-only), so every billable statement in that run is a NULL-schema dbt test → a repoint verifier keyed on schema said ✅ while the run could be leaking to the wrong warehouse. Disqualify on schema OR a FULLY-QUALIFIED relation reference (`baseball_data.<schema>.`) in the text — 64/64. ⚠️ the qualification is LOAD-BEARING: a bare `%ci_betting%` also matches the monitoring script's OWN SQL (which contains the literal) → the check disqualifies itself and can NEVER return PROVEN; `baseball_data.` can't appear in a query_history predicate but always appears in a real dbt statement. (2) ⭐ NORMALIZE date/number literals before ranking a waker board — a date-parameterized family (`DELETE … WHERE prediction_date = <date>`) fragments in an un-normalized `GROUP BY query_text` into a dozen 1-wait rows that scatter into `other`; normalized, the `prediction_log` DELETE was 8.0 waits/day (a CO-#1 waker) and had been STRUCTURALLY INVISIBLE to the prior census. (3) the `LIKE '%…%'` `%` collides with Python `%`-interpolation (`unsupported format character`) → use `.replace()`, NOT `%%` escaping (which re-arms the trap); and anchor a family pattern at the statement's TARGET RELATION, not a loose `LIKE` (a loose `%transient table%team_features%` matched OTHER models' CTAS that merely REFERENCE team_features, inflating the count — the aggregate-lies landmine, self-inflicted while measuring). ⭐ SIBLING (framing) — **'LITERAL ZERO OVERNIGHT' MAY BE MIS-SPECIFIED: the 00–07 UTC 'zero-game band' is 7 PM–2 AM CDT = PEAK US evening game time**, so the remaining overnight COMPUTE_WH mass (dbt data-tests + starter_ip + lineup_state) is LEGITIMATE live-game pipeline work, not zombies — confirm a 'band definition' against the actual TZ before carding more 'overnight' cost work; the achievable goal is 'quiet when NO games are running,' a different window. The success metric for the remaining targets is distinct ACTIVE-MINUTES / suspension windows, NOT waits removed (wake is a queue, #679 — the `prediction_log` DELETE INHERITED the waker role the instant #675/#662 merged 08-10, a 3rd confirmation); ⛔ CI's overnight mass is dev-activity-dependent, never annualise it.
- **🧟 AN AGGREGATE WAKE-FAMILY COUNT CANNOT DISTINGUISH "STILL FIRING" FROM "PRE-FLIP RESIDUE" WHEN THE CENSUS WINDOW STRADDLES THE FLIP DATE — verify EVERY lever PER-DAY, never off the 10-day aggregate (E11.24 target-1/zombie, 2026-08-03; it bit in BOTH directions in one session).** The `report_e11_24_wake_census.py` aggregate family table labeled a statement **"6 tick CTAS (dead 7/25)"** — it was NOT dead; statement-level attribution showed `create or replace transient table …stg_statsapi_lineups_wide` was the **top waiting statement in the whole account** (49 waits/10d, firing overnight on a zero-game warehouse). Simultaneously the aggregate showed `compute_elo` with 27 waits — which the PER-DAY cut proved were **entirely pre-flip residue** (executions hold, waits → 0 from 7/30 = the gate already fired, target-1 needs NO work). **RULE: credit or condemn a lever only on the PER-DAY series (executions HOLDING while waits → 0 = a working gate; both collapsing = a dead job/outage, the 1b lesson) — an aggregate over a window containing the flip measures the residue, not the current state. Instrument it: a census cut is not trustworthy for a straddling window until it has a per-DAY × per-family breakdown.** ⭐ And the cheap-fix lesson: **a metadata-only DDL is FREE; a CTAS is not — `create or replace view` never resumes the warehouse (the sibling staging views recorded ZERO waits), so where a Snowflake staging model has no SF-side consumer needing materialization, flipping `table→view` removes the waker for EVERY caller at once with a strictly SMALLER blast radius than editing a serving job graph** (no soak on the serving path; every reader preserved; freshness unchanged when it's the same pivot over the same `ref()`). ⚠️ but a view RE-EVALUATES on read — measure ACTIVE-MINUTES after the flip, not just resumes (a full-history scan by a non-filtering reader could climb; serving readers that filter by key/date are unaffected). 🧰 Session mechanic: `dbtf` is a SHELL ALIAS to `~/.local/bin/dbt`, invisible to `exec` inside `scripts/dbt_state.sh` → invoke the binary directly: `dbt build --select state:modified+ --state dbt/state --defer --project-dir dbt --profiles-dir dbt`. ⭐ VALIDATED LIVE 2026-08-03: the census session initially read target-3 (metering) as STILL-FIRING off the OLD band-aggregate and the per-day cut OVERTURNED it — i.e. the session hit the very landmine the instrument closes, which is the strongest argument for the per-day cut. 🪤 Attribution-hygiene sub-lesson from the same build: **classify statements over the SAME text window the classifier's CASE uses (it truncated `query_text` to 110 chars before family-matching → dumped 30 weather-slate waits into `other`, INVENTING a phantom waker) — and CROSS-CHECK the per-family sums against the grand total to catch exactly that** (a family bucket that doesn't reconcile is a mis-classification, not a finding); and give the attribution query a finite `STATEMENT_TIMEOUT_IN_SECONDS` + compute the family once in a CTE (a double-evaluated `FAMILY_CASE` over an unfiltered scan timed out at 300s).
  - **♻️ A `--backfill` THAT LOADS THE EXISTING STATE AS ITS PRIOR THEN REPLAYS EVERY DATE IS NON-IDEMPOTENT — a second run DOUBLES the state, silently, for months (E11.24 census find, 2026-07-31; serving-critical):** `update_team_posteriors.py --backfill` `_load_current_seq` reads the current posterior as the prior, then replays all dates on top → `team_sequential_posteriors` held **2.72–2.75× the games actually played** (PHI "151 wins in 109 games"). `win_prob` absorbs exactly one observation per team per game, so **`n_cumulative == games_played` is an IDENTITY** — its violation is the tell. Mean survives but `sigma2 ∝ 1/(a+b)` → the served posterior is **~2.7× OVERCONFIDENT**, feeding `feature_pregame_game_features_raw`. Onset was the 6/04 re-run on an already-populated table (undetected 2 months); dating the onset off SCD-2 history is what separated "a backfill did it" from "which backfill." **RULES: (a) any state-accumulating backfill needs a `--reset`/idempotency guard (truncate-then-load, or detect-and-refuse a populated table) — replaying onto existing state is the default failure; (b) add the conservation IDENTITY as a monitor (`n_cumulative == games_played`), not just a freshness check — an inflated state is invisible to freshness; (c) date the ONSET before accepting/rejecting a cause ("my suspect didn't do it THIS time" ≠ "it's innocent" — here the suspect mechanism was right, a different instance of it was the cause); (d) establish the MECHANISM before saying "confirmed" — correct arithmetic ≠ correct attribution. Sibling writers `update_player_posteriors.py` / `update_matchup_cell_posteriors.py` share the shape.**
    - **🧨 A `--reset` GUARD THAT DELETES *BEFORE* IT READS ITS SOURCE MOVES THE FAILURE TO A WORSE PLACE — load THEN delete (E11.24 target-6 handoff, 2026-07-31).** The fix for the non-idempotent backfill above was a `guard_or_reset_backfill` that DELETEs the season before loading — but a hand-run repair that omitted `--s3` hit a source (`mart_pitch_play_event`) E11.20 phase-1.5 had dropped from Snowflake → the DELETE ran, the read raised, and the store was **EMPTY for ~20 min** (an empty table breaks the serving JOIN outright — strictly worse than the silent inflation the guard prevents). CURE: `require_source_before_reset()` — LOAD-then-delete, refuse on a zero-date source, and name BOTH missing preconditions in the error. **RULE: a destructive guard must verify its source is READABLE before it destroys the current state; a guard that is safe against failure-mode A but unsafe against failure-mode B has just relocated the outage.** ⚠️ two operational siblings from the same handoff: (i) **a HAND-RUN backfill does NOT inherit the daily op's argv** — the player/matchup writers need BOTH `--s3` AND `LAKEHOUSE_DELTA_W1=cutover` (without the latter, `lakehouse_view_sql` silently falls back to the parquet glob phase-1.5 deleted); (ii) **adding a flag is ONE edit across FOUR owners — `env.required` + `.env.example` + `BOX_OPERATIONS §10` + ⭐ the BOX's LIVE `.env` (`${APP_DIR}/services/dagster/aws/.env`, the one `deploy.sh` validates — NOT `~/app/.env`).** Registering it in one but not its pair fails Orchestration-CI / env-parity, and — the 3rd-instance recurrence E11.30 hit 2026-07-31 — **a `git pull` NEVER touches the box's uncommitted live `.env`, so a newly-`env.required` flag makes the NEXT deploy FAIL until the operator adds the key to the box `.env` by hand.** ⇒ any story that adds a required flag MUST list "set `<FLAG>=<default>` in the box's `services/dagster/aws/.env`" as an explicit operator-handoff step. The repo's recurring one-logical-thing-many-owners motif, cf. INC-30 crontab / INC-36 concurrency. (Related, same E11.30 handoff: **SNS defaults to `us-east-1` — do NOT pass the S3-lakehouse `AWS_DEFAULT_REGION=us-east-2` to an SNS client** → a misleading `InvalidParameter: TopicArn`; only the S3 lakehouse bucket needs us-east-2.)
    - **✅ FIXED + IMPACT SCOPED (E9.53, 2026-07-31).** GUARD: `sequential_bayes/catchup.guard_or_reset_backfill` refuses a backfill onto a populated season and `--reset` does the DELETE itself; wired into ALL THREE writers (a parametrized test asserts every writer calls it, so a 4th can't ship unguarded). MONITOR: `dbt/tests/assert_team_sequential_no_double_apply.sql` (tolerance 1.02). Season 2026 repaired via `--backfill --season 2026 --reset` → ratio **1.0000 on all 30 teams**; 2021-2025 were never inflated (ratio 1.000). ⚠️ **BUT SCOPE THE IMPACT BEFORE ESCALATING — the "~2.7× overconfident" is TRUE OF THE STORE AND FALSE OF SERVING: `grep` for `posterior_sigma2|prior_sigma2|param_a|param_b|n_cumulative` outside the writer returns NOTHING.** Both consumers read only the MEAN (`feature_pregame_game_features_raw` → `prior_mu`; `build_bullpen_state_dataset` → `posterior_mu`), so the corrupted second moment was never served. And the mean barely moved because the Beta prior is only **8 games** (`a0=b0=4`): reconstructing the 3× state from the repaired parameters gives a win_prob mean delta of just **0.002 (max 0.006)** — which is precisely WHY it hid for two months. ⭐ **THE GENERALISABLE PAIR: (1) a replay that corrupts only the SECOND moment is invisible to every value/mean check ⇒ assert on a COUNT identity; (2) a weak prior makes the mean nearly replay-invariant ⇒ the same weakness that hides the bug also BOUNDS its serving harm — so quantify the consumed quantity before calling a store defect a serving defect.** (Measured contrast, same slate: the E9.53 per-metric resolution fix moved the SAME feature by 0.049 mean / 0.126 max — ~96% of the observed change. Attributing all of it to the inflation would have been wrong in both directions.)
- **🪪 "DOCUMENTED CUT-OVER" ≠ "ACTUALLY SET ON THE BOX" — audit LIVE container env, never trust the docs (E11.20-COST, 2026-07-16; the 3rd instance of this class):** `W7B_LAKEHOUSE_S3` was documented as cut-over but was NEVER SET on the box → the daily predict/serving reads hit Snowflake for WEEKS, found only because the cost audit checked the live container env instead of the intended-state docs. Same class as the `aws_resources.md` subscriptions-table-documented-but-never-created + the two-owner crontab. **CURE (now standard): any flag whose documented state is load-bearing goes in `env.required` (a missing/non-`1` value FAILS the deploy) + the `check_monitors_healthy_op` heartbeat (pages on drift)** — W7A/W7B added there. When a session says "X is cut over," verify against `docker compose … exec` live env, not the roadmap.
  - **🪞 …AND "VERIFIED LIVE IN-CONTAINER" IS ONLY TRUE IF IT TARGETED THE PERSISTENT JOB-EXECUTING CONTAINER, NOT A THROWAWAY EXEC (FU-1, 2026-08-03 — the subtler variant: the flag WAS set, just not where it ran).** 6a's flip was "verified live in-container" at 05:29 UTC, but that check hit a throwaway/one-off container; the persistent `dagster-codeloc` container that `DefaultRunLauncher` actually runs job subprocesses in was NOT recreated until 20:52 UTC (an incidental redeploy) → `E11_24_UMPIRE_REBUILD_GATE` evaluated **False** in the container that ran all 7 of the 8/2 `lineup_monitor_job` rebuilds, so the gate never fired the whole slate despite reading `=1` on a later `exec`. **RULES: (a) a "gate is armed" claim must be proven in the SAME container Dagster executes jobs in — an env flip only takes effect after the executing container is RECREATED (not restarted; `up -d --build`/a redeploy), so a mid-day `printenv` on a fresh exec proves nothing about the container that ran this morning's jobs; (b) ⭐ the RESTART-PROOF verification is the Postgres EVENT LOG, not `docker compose logs` — raw stdout is `LocalComputeLogManager` = ephemeral disk WIPED on container recreation, but `context.log.info(...)` persists indefinitely in Postgres; query `DagsterInstance.all_logs(run_id)` inside the container and grep for the gate's marker to prove per-run whether it fired. (c) a same-day env flip does not retroactively arm already-run jobs — to observe the gate you need a job that RUNS AFTER the executing container was recreated with the flag.**
- **🕒 HOST CRONTAB INSTALLED UNDER TWO USERS = EVERY CRON DOUBLE-FIRES → dbt-runner `409` self-collisions (INC-30, 2026-07-05 — the lineup-monitor manual-kick bug; config was CLEAN):** `deploy.sh`-via-SSM installs the crontab as **root**; a manual run / the setup doc installed it as **ec2-user** → crond runs BOTH tables → every capture line fires twice → schedule-capture ticks 409-collide with each other, silently dropping the `stg_statsapi_lineups_wide` rebuild → stale lineup feed → sensor has nothing to fire. COMPOUNDED by `trigger_dbt.py` treating the single-tenant runner's **`409` (busy) as a HARD failure** instead of a retry. CURES: `trigger_dbt.py` 409→backoff-retry (bounded); `deploy.sh` clears the other user's stray crontab (single-owner). **RULE: the box crontab must live under exactly ONE owner; a `409` from the dbt-runner means BUSY→retry, never fail.**
- **⏳ AN UN-TIMED-OUT SUBPROCESS IN A SENSOR EVAL OR THE dbt-RUNNER WEDGES THE WHOLE ORCHESTRATOR (INC-32, 2026-07-18 — the run-stall / sensor-stop / silent-mirror cluster):** two sibling wedges, same shape — a subprocess with NO timeout on a shared-serialized resource hangs forever and takes the orchestrator with it. **(A)** `_evaluate_lineup_monitor` ran `lineup_monitor.py` (its state read is still Snowflake) via `subprocess.run` with **no `timeout=`** → a hung monitor blocked the Dagster **sensor-daemon worker thread** forever → **ALL sensor evals STOPPED mid-slate** (7/17 ~21:30Z; 7/15 games never got post_lineup). This is the "silently not running" mode `default_status=RUNNING` (E11.23) does NOT cover — the sensor is still RUNNING, it just never ticks. **(B)** the dbt-runner (`services/dbt_runner/server.py:_run_cmd`) ran `dbtf` with **no timeout** → a wedged dbtf (documented 2026-06-15, "the CLI just stopped exiting") hung the worker thread, and because the single-tenant lock is `any run with status=="running"`, that dead entry held the lock **PERMANENTLY → every `/run` 409'd forever** until a manual container restart (→ daily fired ~1.2h late, schedule-capture staging dropped = stale lineups, the manual lineup run "stalled" for hours). **CURES (all shipped): every subprocess on a daemon/serialized path has a finite `timeout=` + kills the child on expiry;** the dbt-runner ALSO marks a timed-out/crashed run `failed` (frees the lock) + a `_reap_stale_runs` reaper clears a dead-thread "running" entry; a tick-**STALENESS** heartbeat in `check_monitors_healthy_op` (`SENSOR_TICK_STALE_SECONDS`, default 60 min) pages when a still-RUNNING critical sensor stops ticking; and the mirror-tier `lineup_intraday_s3_feature_rebuild` except now **`send_alert` CRITICAL + records the failing step** (a mirror-tier `log.warning` nobody watches lets a transient S3/SF/OOM degrade post_lineup coverage unseen — the op itself verified healthy on the box, so the 7/17 0.833 was the sensor-stop + dbt-runner wedge, but the swallow would hide a real one). **RULE: any `subprocess.run` on a sensor-eval / daemon / single-tenant-lock path MUST pass a finite `timeout=`; a serialized resource (the dbt-runner) MUST have a server-side timeout + a stale-lock reaper so a wedge can never hold the lock forever; and a mirror-tier (ALERT-continue) op must genuinely PAGE (`send_alert`), not just `log.warning`.** Guards: `test_inc32_sensor_daemon_timeout.py`, `test_dbt_runner.py` (timeout/reaper), `test_monitor_health_guard.py` (stale-tick), `test_lineup_intraday_s3_rebuild.py` (send_alert). Runtime-gate class — CI mocks all IO, so this only manifests on a live slate.
- **🧬 A RE-SCORE THAT FIRES ON A PARTIALLY-POSTED LINEUP FREEZES THE GAME ONE-AND-DONE (INC-32 coverage-race, 2026-07-19 — post_lineup coverage stuck 0.811, lineup block dead on 14/15 games):** MLB posts a batting order slot-by-slot, and the capture→flatten→`stg_statsapi_lineups_wide`→SCD-2 chain can surface a PARTIAL order (e.g. slots 1–4) minutes before the full 9. The old `lineup_monitor.py` detection only required `COUNT(DISTINCT home_away)=2` (BOTH sides have *some* lineup — the model keeps a per-side row as soon as slot_1 is non-null), so a re-score fired on that partial state → the SCD-2/aggregator rebuilt from an incomplete lineup → the served lineup block (`avg_eb_woba` etc.) computed dead. **The freeze is the real defect: `post_lineup` is ONE-AND-DONE** — once a game has a `post_lineup` row it's never re-triggered (Step 2b only re-fires games *missing* one), so the first degraded attempt is frozen forever, no retry. Not a rebuild-op failure (those verified healthy) and CI-blind (only shows on a live slate). **CURE = a PER-GAME READINESS GATE in `lineup_monitor.py` (`select_ready_games`): a game is eligible to score only once BOTH sides carry a COMPLETE 9-slot order (`min_slots_filled=9`); a partial game is HELD (not triggered, not recorded in `lineup_monitor_state`, no `post_lineup` row) so the sensor's next ~10-min tick RETRIES it once the full order lands. SLA safety valve: a still-incomplete lineup within 40 min of first pitch scores best-effort (loud `[ALERT]`) so the gate can never blow the Epic A1 30-min SLA on a never-completes game (first pitch read Snowflake-free from the S3 lakehouse, postponed rows excluded per the 2026-07-19 DH landmine).** **RULE: any "confirmed lineup" trigger must gate on lineup COMPLETENESS, not mere presence — a per-game readiness check + retry, because a one-and-done serving write on incomplete input is frozen, not self-healing.** Guard: `test_lineup_readiness_gate.py`.
