- **♻️ THE LINEUP MONITOR MUST TRIGGER ONLY PRE-GAME GAMES, AND ONLY ON A *REAL* STARTER CHANGE (INC-32 823523 loop, 2026-07-19 — one Live game re-triggered `lineup_monitor_job` every ~10 min for 4h):** the Dagit `--steps`/run-list diagnostic (`scripts/ops/dagster_runs.py lineup_monitor_job 20`) showed game 823523 in the trigger set of nearly every tick 14:10→18:11 — incl. ~10 fires AFTER its 16:35Z first pitch — though it was a normal Live game with a COMPLETE lineup and an existing post_lineup row. TWO independent bugs in `lineup_monitor.py`: **(1) no pre-game gate** — a post_lineup re-score of a Live/Final game is pointless (bet off the board) + past the Epic A1 SLA, but the monitor read `stg_statsapi_lineups_wide` with NO game-state filter; and **(2) a pitcher-change FLIP-FLOP** — the re-trigger fired whenever `stored_starter != current_probable`, and a transient NULL current probable (a LEFT JOIN gap in `stg_statsapi_probable_pitchers`) or a stored-INT-vs-Decimal/str type read makes that `!=` true EVERY tick, so the UPDATE→re-read never converges. **CURES: (1) restrict candidates to `abstract_game_state='Preview'` games (Snowflake-free lakehouse read, `_pregame_first_pitch`; fail-open on read error so the monitor never goes dark; also excludes postponed) and (2) `is_real_pitcher_change()` — a change counts only when BOTH stored and current are fully-known and differ after int-normalization (a NULL current = data gap, not a scratch).** **RULE: a re-score trigger must be gated to PRE-GAME state, and any "did X change" churn-driver must be null-safe + type-normalized — a verbatim `!=` across a nullable/typed boundary re-fires forever.** This was the actual 4h-loop culprit, NOT a daily run-stall — the 7/19 daily verified CLEAN via `--steps` (predict→serve 4 min; the story's "serve 15:16Z" was a normal intraday serving write, not the daily tail). Guards: `test_lineup_readiness_gate.py` (`is_real_pitcher_change`).
- **💥 ZERO-ARG `{{ ref() }}`/`{{ source() }}` IN A SQL `--` COMMENT PANICS dbt-fusion codegen (W11-C/D, crashed BOTH waves):** fusion's lexer evaluates `{{ }}` BEFORE comments are stripped → an empty/documentary Jinja call in a comment → `index out of bounds`, whole-project `parse`/`compile` crash. **NEVER put a zero-arg Jinja call in a comment — write "a Jinja ref call" in prose instead.**
- **🔢 NULLABLE-INT → DOUBLE MIRROR POISONING (2026-07-15, the INC-17-class avg_eb_woba outage — lineup block NULL on every served slate 07-03→07-15):** a `SELECT *`→pandas→parquet mirror turns any SF NUMBER(38,0) column with NULLs into float64 → parquet DOUBLE (`SLOT_6..9_PLAYER_ID`; slots 1–5 stayed BIGINT). A downstream UNION of mixed slots coerced the whole `batter_id` to DOUBLE → `::varchar` rendered `'664983.0'` → the VARCHAR join to eb_batter_posteriors_raw matched 0 rows → avg_eb_woba NULL for EVERY game incl. history. **Numeric joins (BIGINT=DOUBLE) survive; `::varchar`-cast joins die silently.** Parity/CI-blind. CURE (writer-side, one place heals all consumers): `export_w8b_precursors_to_s3` pins every FIXED scale-0 cursor column to pandas nullable `Int64` pre-write + RAISES if a pinned column would write non-int (guard: `test_export_mirror_int_pin.py`). ⚠️ 8 sibling `scripts/export_*_to_s3.py` share the raw-cursor→`pd.DataFrame` pattern = latent same-class bugs — apply the same pin when touching one (or when a `::varchar` join reads its mirror).
- **🔢 `dtype=str` CSV WRITER → VARCHAR raw mirror → downstream numeric ops HALT (W11 Tier A):** a raw writer that reads its source with `pd.read_csv(dtype=str)` (e.g. `ingest_sprint_speed`) stores numeric cols as VARCHAR in the S3 mirror (the old Snowflake snapshot was typed because SF coerced on INSERT) → a downstream `where competitive_runs > 0` hits `Binder Error: VARCHAR vs INTEGER_LITERAL`. CURE: `try_cast` at the use-site (INC-23 pattern). ⚠️ parity-over-parquet is BLIND to it — only a real `--wN` DuckDB build surfaces it → any future typed-CSV migration needs the same cast.
- **📌 UNPINNED DOCKER ML DEPS = every rebuild floats sklearn/ngboost/lightgbm → serving pickles one rebuild from breaking (2026-07-03).** The Dockerfile `pip install`ed ML libs unpinned + IGNORED `uv.lock` → a rebuild floated sklearn → `No module named '_loss'` on pickle load (train≠serve). FIXED: pin all pickle-fragile libs (Dockerfile + pyproject + lock) + a build-time version guard + re-fit the champion pickles under the pinned version. **NEVER leave the serving image's ML libs unpinned; a champion pickle must be re-fit whenever its lib pins move.**
- **🌧️ POSTPONED GAMES ARE `abstractGameState='Final'` + MLB REUSES THE gamePk FOR THE MAKEUP (2026-07-19):** a rained-out game carries `detailedState='Postponed'` but `abstractGameState='Final'` — so the INC-22 state-rank dedup (`stg_statsapi_games`, "game state only advances") keeps the STALE postponed row over the makeup's `'Preview'`, AND the makeup reuses the SAME `gamePk` (e.g. a rescheduled DH game 1). Two live bites: the app showed the game at the RAINED-OUT slot, and the Statcast SLA sensor's `MIN(first_pitch)` read the past postponed instant → instant false-CRITICAL "deadline breached." **RULE: any dedup or "first pitch"/MIN(commence) logic MUST special-case `detailedState='Postponed'` — demote it to rank 1 (ties Preview; postponed-last tiebreak when a makeup coexists) + EXCLUDE postponed rows from first-pitch/SLA reads.** Guard: `test_postponed_dh_dedup_guard.py`.
- **🕒 THE INC-23 VARCHAR BITE ALSO HITS SENSORS, not just marts (2026-07-03):** the lakehouse returns `game_date` as an ISO VARCHAR → a sensor doing `game_date.astimezone()`/`.tzinfo` throws `'str' has no attribute …` EVERY tick → the sensor silently dies (3-sensor / 3-day odds-serving outage, no alert). CURE: route every sensor's date handling through the shared `to_utc_datetime` coercion helper. ✅ **ALL sensors now routed (E11.23) + a source-inspection guard test makes "any sensor reading a first-pitch `game_date` MUST use `to_utc_datetime`" a mechanical invariant** — keep new sensors on the helper or the guard fails.
- **🚦 SENSORS/SCHEDULES SILENTLY DON'T RUN if they boot STOPPED (E11.23 cure):** a Dagster sensor/schedule shipped without `default_status=RUNNING` boots STOPPED and silently never fires (and reverts to STOPPED on any Dagster-DB reset / re-host = the INC-16 class) → the "silently never runs" outage class. CURE: serving-critical sensors + the daily-job schedule declare `default_status=RUNNING` (self-start + survive a DB reset); intraday-capture/public-betting/weekly schedules stay default-STOPPED ON PURPOSE (operator-gated: double-ingest / paid-capture). A standalone `check_monitors_healthy_op` heartbeat (ALERT-tier, never HALTs) pages if a serving-critical sensor/schedule is manually STOPPED or a permanently-on intraday flag is unset. Intended-state table = `BOX_OPERATIONS.md §10`; the 4 required flags are in `env.required` (deploy-time gate).
- **🔕 "ALERT-TIER" HAD QUIETLY MEANT "DETECTED, NOBODY NOTIFIED" (E11.30, 2026-07-31 — the reason the E11.27 blind spot survived DAYS despite an existing detector):** the E11.7 contract defines ALERT-tier as "page loud, continue" — but of the 6 `check_*_op`/monitor family members, only `check_monitors_healthy_op` (E11.23) and `update_archetype_posteriors_op` (E11.22) actually called `send_alert`; `check_served_prediction_integrity_op`, `check_feature_block_coverage_op`, `check_odds_coverage_op`, and `check_injury_status_health_op` only ever reached `context.log.warning` in their (default) non-strict path — a Dagster step-log line nobody was watching. `check_served_prediction_integrity_op` had ALREADY had a `feature_store_frac<0.80` fallback detector when the E11.24 §8 intraday_fallback blind spot persisted for days — the detection logic existed the whole time; the page never fired. **CURE: all four now page via `send_alert`, keyed on the SAME discriminating signal each script already computed for its log banner** (a new `[METRIC] …_freeze=`/`…_degraded_count=` line where one didn't already exist — `served_integrity_problem_count` and `injury_status_ok` already were), so paging adds zero new false-positive surface: `check_odds_coverage_op` CRITICAL on a current-slate FREEZE only (never NO_ODDS_YET/PARTIAL); `check_feature_block_coverage_op` CRITICAL/ERROR on any DEGRADED block (never SKIPPED/coverage-gapped); `check_served_prediction_integrity_op` CRITICAL on any integrity problem; `check_injury_status_health_op` CRITICAL on `IMPLAUSIBLE` (the E9.48 bug signature)/WARN on `UNKNOWN`, but deliberately STAYS log-only on a `feed_freshness`-only failure (the documented, already-understood Nov–Feb off-season ingest hole — paging on it daily for ~4 months would be the "over-paging monitor gets muted" failure mode this story explicitly warns against). **RULE: an op tagged ALERT-tier must actually call `send_alert` somewhere on its real-condition path — a tier label enforced only by a docstring/table row is not enforced at all.** `check_prediction_coverage` was found UNDOCUMENTED in the tier table entirely: it has no `try/except` (unlike every sibling check op), so it unconditionally fails the op → fails `daily_ingestion_job` → already pages CRITICAL via the pre-existing `run_failure_alert_sensor` (job-failure-based paging is a valid, already-covered notify path — HALT-tier ops don't need a redundant direct `send_alert` call). RUNTIME-GATE: `send_alert` hits SNS, so CI (mocks all IO) can't prove a page fires — trust the mocked-SNS unit tests for the *decision logic* (`test_check_ops_alerting.py`) but a live-box smoke (a deliberately-tripped FREEZE/DEGRADED/IMPLAUSIBLE condition, confirm the email lands) is still required before trusting the page path in prod.
  - **🚬 …AND THAT MANDATED SMOKE MUST BE LABELLED AND MUST NOT OCCUPY THE REAL DEDUP SLOT — an unlabelled smoke both costs an incident response AND SILENTLY SUPPRESSES THE GENUINE PAGE FOR AN HOUR (INC-39, 2026-08-02).** E11.30 requires a live-box smoke for every ALERT-tier monitor; before this, such a smoke went out through the ordinary path with the **production severity, subject and `dedup_key`**, so it was indistinguishable from a real incident. A synthetic `public_betting BUILD_GAP 0/15` smoke of `check_w11_tail_coverage_op` on 2026-08-02 read as a genuine serving defect and opened a P2 — on a slate whose built table actually held **15/15** (the op's real path over the real lakehouse returns `severity=None`; ground truth is gathered by running the check OUTSIDE the op, never by trusting the monitor's own claim). ⚠️ **The second harm is the serious one and it applies to all 66 `send_alert` call sites:** rate-limiting is keyed on `dedup_key` with a **1-hour TTL**, so a smoke firing the PRODUCTION key parks the real page for the next hour — smoke-testing a monitor can BLIND it exactly while an operator is poking at the box. **CURE: `send_alert(..., smoke=True)` — `[SMOKE TEST]` leads the subject, a banner leads the body, and the key is namespaced `smoke:` so a self-test can never occupy a real alert's slot. Deliberately a KEYWORD ARG WITH NO ENV-VAR BACKDOOR: a left-set `ALERT_SMOKE_TEST=1` would label every REAL page a smoke — the mirror-image failure, strictly worse, and this repo's documented-but-never-set flag class (cf. `W7B_LAKEHOUSE_S3`) facing the dangerous way.** 🧭 **SIBLING RULE: a monitor that parses a subprocess's `[METRIC]` lines must be able to verify WHICH SLATE they describe** — replayed/stale/synthetic output parses byte-identically to a live read, and numbers that are individually real can still be about the wrong day. The check now stamps `[METRIC] w11_tail_date=<ISO>` on EVERY exit path (incl. the read-failure path, so the cross-check can't be vacuously satisfied by an absent line — NF1.7 (a)) and `classify` demotes a wrong-slate leg to UNVERIFIED/WARN, never CRITICAL and never healthy. 🪤 **AND THE GUARD GAP THAT LET IT SHIP: the op was covered from BOTH ENDS with an unexercised middle** — the script's classifier and the paging policy each had suites, and the op-execution test monkeypatched `_run_script` AWAY, so every assertion ran against a stdout string a TEST AUTHOR wrote and the suite would have stayed green if the script's print format and the monitor's regex had drifted apart. ⇒ **for any op that shells out, ONE test must exercise the REAL subprocess leg** (real script `main()` with only its IO stubbed → real stdout → real parsers → real `classify` → real op in a Dagster job); `betting_ml/tests/test_inc39_w11_tail_daily_invocation.py` is the reference. ⚠️ **Two of those tests first passed FOR THE WRONG REASON: `_run_script` runs with `cwd=/app` (the BOX layout), so off-box every subprocess dies before exec and the op reports UNVERIFIED/WARN** — which is what one test asserted. ⇒ **an off-box test of a subprocess leg MUST monkeypatch `APP_DIR` and then PROVE the subprocess really ran and parsed (`parse_date`/`parse_evaluated`), or "the op stayed silent" is indistinguishable from "the subprocess never started"** (NF1.7 (a) again). Every guard here was proven to go RED on deliberately-broken source (4 breaks → 3/2/2/1 RED) before being trusted. Postmortem: `docs/inc39_w11_tail_false_critical.md`.
  - **🔗 A GUARD ON AN AND-COMPOSED RULE IS VACUOUS UNLESS ITS FIXTURE SATISFIES EVERY *OTHER* CLAUSE — the guard stays GREEN when you delete the clause it names, because a SECOND clause is already refusing the fixture (NF-D17, 2026-08-03; generalises to EVERY `and`-gate in the repo).** NF-D17's most important guard — "a bigger point estimate ALONE can never earn a public-claim change" — stayed green when its clause was DELETED from the source, because a different clause in the same `and`-gate already rejected the test fixture, so the deletion changed nothing observable. It was caught ONLY by deliberately breaking the source and noticing the guard didn't go red. **RULE: to prove a guard on `A and B and C` actually tests clause A, its fixture must SATISFY B and C (so only A can flip the result) — write ONE isolating fixture PER clause, and RED-prove each by deleting its own clause; a single fixture that trips multiple clauses proves none of them.** (Sibling of the INC-38/INC-39 "a guard that cannot FAIL is worse than none" — one level finer: a guard that fails for the WRONG reason is equally vacuous.) NF-D17 also reconfirmed two benchmark-comparison rules worth carrying: a ONE-SIDED top-N truncation MANUFACTURES an arbitrary Δρ (measured 0.20 wide — state which side defined the N or the comparison is uninterpretable), and a "matched-population" fix does NOTHING when one population is a SUBSET of the other (the intersection count is a design quantity — read the overlap FIRST).
  - **🔭 A MONITOR POSITIONED UPSTREAM OF ITS OWN PRODUCER MANUFACTURES A RECURRING FALSE OUTAGE — and the tell is that the flagged date is NOT the dead date (INC-40, 2026-08-03; the INC-25 build-ordering rule applied to a MONITOR, not a consumer).** `check_feature_block_coverage_op` sat ~13 ops UPSTREAM of `dbt_umpire_feature_rebuild` — the op that BUILDS the very blocks it asserts on (`mart_bullpen_effectiveness → feature_pregame_team_features → …game_features_raw` = `bullpen_eb`/`umpire`) — so it read a store ONE FOLD BEHIND and paged CRITICAL on a date the SAME RUN healed a few ops later (flagged 07-30 @ 0% on the 08-01 run; both read 100% by the time a human looked). The genuinely-dead date was the NEWEST one, which the guard EXEMPTS by design. **RULES: (a) a guard asserting on a table MUST fan off (sit downstream of) EVERY op that writes the columns it checks, AND stay upstream of the consumer it protects (here `predict_today`) so strict mode keeps its teeth; (b) ⭐ THE TELL of a mis-placed monitor: the date it NAMES reads healthy by the time you look while a DIFFERENT, more-recent date is dead — that is a measurement-POINT bug, not an outage; (c) ⛔ NEVER silence it by WIDENING its exemption (`_DATE_OUTAGE_SKIP_NEWEST` 1→2) — that hides the symptom AND blinds the detector an extra day; fix the measurement point; (d) ⭐ SF-vs-S3 AGREEMENT is a ONE-QUERY discriminator that eliminates the ENTIRE ext-table read-bug family (INC-31 / VALUE:-case / glob-dup) before any deeper hunt — that class shows S3 healthy + SF NULL, NEVER both dead, so run it FIRST; (e) an alert's own SUGGESTED-CAUSE banner is diagnostic ANCHORING — INC-40's banner named "likely an ext-table VALUE:-case mismatch" and cost the first hour chasing INC-31; keep suggested-cause text HEDGED or omit it.** ⚠️ RUNTIME-GATE class: CI cannot validate a job-graph rewiring (in_process_executor runs topologically, so a source-line-order test is VACUOUS — pin dependency EDGES + strip comments per INC-38), so the fix is UNVERIFIED until the next real daily run is observed. Guard: `test_feature_block_guard_ordering.py`. 🧰 Session mechanic: a fresh `git worktree` has NO `.env` (gitignored) → every Snowflake/AWS script hangs/fails opaquely until you `cp` it in; and `ssm:SendCommand`/`ec2:DescribeInstances` are DENIED for `baseball-access-user`, so reading box Dagster logs is always an OPERATOR step.
  - **⏱️ "A SENSOR HASN'T FIRED TODAY" IS EVIDENCE OF A WEDGE ONLY AFTER NORMALIZING AGAINST THE SLATE'S FIRST-PITCH TIMES + THE SENSOR'S TRIGGER PRECONDITIONS — comparing run-counts to "the same clock hour yesterday" is INVALID across slates of different size/start time (INC-40 amendment, 2026-08-03; a retracted false INC-32 alarm).** A session flagged `lineup_monitor_job` as an INC-32-class sensor stop because it had fewer runs than the same wall-clock hour on 08-02 — but 08-02 was a 15-game early-start slate and 08-03 was 8 games all Preview with earliest first pitch 22:40 UTC and ZERO lineups posted. The lineup monitor fires ~5h before first pitch and gates on complete 9-slot orders on BOTH sides (the INC-32 readiness gate), so at ~7h out with no lineups, SILENCE IS CORRECT — a late slate looks identical to a wedged sensor until you check the first-pitch distribution. **RULE: before calling a quiet sensor a wedge, normalize by HOURS-BEFORE-FIRST-PITCH and confirm its trigger preconditions are even met — never by clock time. Cheap read-only LAPTOP discriminator: read `stg_statsapi_games` (today's game states + first-pitch range) and `stg_statsapi_lineups_wide` (lineup completeness) — ⛔ NEVER run `lineup_monitor.py` to check, it WRITES audit + state rows.** Confirm no serving-critical instigator is actually STOPPED (`check_monitors_healthy` / `stopped_critical_instigators`) as the real wedge signal.
- **🪓 DROPPING A TABLE IN A DECOMMISSION — the dbt DAG is NOT the full consumer list (INC-27, 2026-07-04, P1 serving-degrade):** W11-E dropped `betting.stg_batter_pitches` after concluding "only consumer = `mart_pa_outcome_substrate` (duckdb-only)" — but that was a dbt-GRAPH check, and ~6+ **Python** scripts embed the table name as a raw SQL STRING (invisible to the DAG), incl. serving-critical `write_serving_store.py` (HALT-tier box-score) + live-backend `picks.py` (box-score endpoint 500s) + the team/bullpen posterior state-writers. **RULE: before dropping ANY table/view, `grep -rIn "<schema>\.<table>"` the WHOLE repo (`.py`, `.sql`, string literals), not just `dbt ls`/the manifest — a raw-SQL-string consumer in a `.py` state-writer or a FastAPI router is a REAL consumer the DAG can't see.** Reversible mitigation for a bad drop: recreate the object as a Snowflake external table/view over the S3 parquet the exporter already writes (no consumer code change), then repoint consumers to DuckDB as the durable finish. (Ref: `generate_matchup_signals.py` already does the durable pattern — `.replace("baseball_data.betting.stg_batter_pitches","stg_batter_pitches")` → DuckDB.) **RESOLVED (INC-27):** `betting.stg_batter_pitches` is recreated as `lakehouse_ext.stg_batter_pitches` (external table over the S3 parquet) + a `betting.stg_batter_pitches` view — the standard decommission pattern — via `scripts/ddl/generate_stg_batter_pitches_external_table.py` (`--apply` runs the DDL on the box); its daily REFRESH is wired into `refresh_w1_external_tables.py` REQUIRED tier so the view never goes stale. NOTE the incident's consumer list was partly stale: `picks.py` + the DAILY `write_serving_store.py --s3` path already read S3 via `lakehouse_query`/`--s3`; the real breaks were the intraday serving path + the hybrid posterior writers (SF-read + SF-write) which the view fixes with zero consumer code change. **GUARD:** `betting_ml/tests/test_decommission_orphan_guard.py` (fast gate) keeps a registry of decommissioned-but-raw-SQL-referenced tables and FAILS if any lacks its daily lakehouse_ext refresh backstop — add a table there whenever you drop one with raw-SQL consumers.
- **🗂️ DELETING A STORAGE *LAYOUT* — grep for readers of the **PATH**, not the table NAME (E11.20 phase-1.5, 2026-07-20, 🚨 P0 ZERO-PREDICTION SLATE; the INC-27 sibling):** step-6's documented `s3 rm` of the SF-compat mirror looked safe — the pre-drop **zero-reader check ran over Snowflake `access_history`**, which showed no consumers. But the §6 straggler repoint had pointed several readers at **`read_parquet('lakehouse/<table>/**/*.parquet')` — the legacy/compat PATH, not `delta_scan`** — so deleting the compat parquet left them reading NOTHING: `generate_matchup_signals_op` raised → **the daily job stopped BEFORE `predict_today` → the slate served ZERO predictions** (`update_player_posteriors` shared the helper and was next in line). **RULE: a Snowflake `access_history` zero-reader check CANNOT SEE DuckDB/S3 PATH readers — before deleting/moving any S3 layout, `grep -rIn` the repo for the PATH STRING (the prefix/glob), not just the table name.** A table can have zero SQL consumers and many path consumers. **CURE (central, shipped):** `register_lakehouse_views` / `lakehouse_view_sql` / `ensure_delta_extension` added to BOTH byte-identical registry homes, routing per table (Delta vs legacy); all four reader modules delegate; guard `test_lakehouse_reader_delta_routing.py` FAILS any new hardcoded W1 glob. Verified live (7,855,232 rows via `delta_scan`). ⚠️ **Same rule applies to every multi-sport lakehouse replay.** Recovered same-day via re-execute-from-failure.
- **🧊 A STAGING MODEL WHOSE SNOWFLAKE BRANCH READS A RETIRED NATIVE `source()` SERVES FROZEN DATA (2026-07-23, the INC-27/E11.20-phase-1.5 mirror-image; P1 missing-predictions):** when a raw capture flips **S3-native** (its Snowflake writer retired) or a native table is dropped, any dbt model whose **Snowflake-executable branch** still reads it via `{{ source('schema','table') }}` silently serves a FROZEN snapshot — no error, no HALT. `statsapi.monthly_schedule`'s writer was retired 2026-07-20 when schedule-capture went S3-native; `stg_statsapi_games` had been repointed to `lakehouse_ext.stg_statsapi_games`, but sibling `stg_statsapi_probable_pitchers` + `stg_statsapi_starter_snapshots` were left reading the native VARIANT → froze at 7/20 17:00 → games whose starters MLB announced after 7/20 served both-NULL probables → the pregame starter block couldn't build → **NO prediction** (824247, 823042 on 7/23). CI-BLIND (mocks IO) and PARITY-BLIND (the S3 `--w8b` served path was fresh the whole time; only the SF-native staging tables were stale). **RULE: a model whose feed lives in the S3 lakehouse must read `baseball_data.lakehouse_ext.<model>` on the Snowflake target, NOT re-flatten the retired native `source()`. When you retire a capture's SF writer, repoint EVERY sibling staging model that flattened it in the SAME change — a `dbt ls`/DAG check won't flag it (both branches parse); grep every `{{ source('<retired>') }}` reader.** A model intentionally dead on SF must mark its Snowflake branch `enabled=false`. **GUARD:** `betting_ml/tests/test_retired_source_guard.py` (fast gate) keeps a registry of retired native sources (`statsapi.monthly_schedule`, `oddsapi.mlb_odds_raw`, `savant.batter_pitches`) and FAILS if any is read via `{{ source() }}` in a Snowflake-executable branch — **add a `(schema,table)` there the moment its writer is retired / table is dropped.** (Sibling still-open follow-up: the 2 SF-native edge/backtest models on the frozen `oddsapi.odds_snapshots_historical` — non-serving, migrate when the edge program revisits them.)
- **🧪 FAST-GATE TESTS MUST NOT IMPORT `pipeline` (E11.23):** `pipeline/__init__.py` reads the dbt manifest, ABSENT in the fast gate → any fast-gate test importing `pipeline` crashes at COLLECTION (not a clean skip). CURE: put import-safe pure logic in `betting_ml/` (e.g. `betting_ml/monitoring/monitor_health.py`) and import from there; guard any genuinely pipeline-importing test to skip when the manifest is absent. RULE: fast-gate tests inspect source or import from `betting_ml`, never `pipeline`.
- **⏱️ REPOINTING A MONITOR TO AN S3 MIRROR requires the mirror to be WRITTEN on the SAME cadence the monitor CHECKS (W12/W9 finding):** W12 repointed the odds-freshness sensor (checks 24/7) to the S3 `mlb_odds_raw` mirror — but the box writes that mirror only during GAME-HOURS (the `W6_LAKEHOUSE_INTRADAY`-gated intraday op; the lean odds-capture image can't write S3 at all) → the 24/7 monitor reads a game-hours-only mirror → false-stale off-hours. **Before repointing any monitor to an S3 mirror, confirm the mirror-write cadence matches the check cadence.** Fix = a 24/7 `export_*_to_s3.py` cron in the full container (or flip the writer S3-native so the mirror is written whenever capture runs — the W11-E odds-writer flip).
- **🔗 Repointing a consumer to `lakehouse_raw` UNIONs two typings** — the SF-typed bridge backfill + the writer-typed live parquet. Before repointing: verify the type reconciliation (esp. TIMESTAMP↔VARCHAR via `union_by_name` common-supertypes) AND that the dedup `ORDER BY` columns exist on the live rows.
- **⏭️ CUTTING A CONSUMER OVER TO AN S3 PARQUET requires that parquet to be BUILT AFTER its source stores are refreshed IN THE SAME RUN (INC-25, P0 serving-down):** the W8a cutover repointed `feature_pregame_sub_model_signals` (SF) to `select * from lakehouse_ext.<it>` = an S3 parquet built by `run_w1_lakehouse_op --w8a` at daily-job START — but the signal generators write their stores and `export_w9_signals_to_s3_op` mirrors them to S3 LATER in the same job. So the consumer parquet lagged the stores by a full slate → the SCD-2 groups (run_env/bullpen/matchup/env/defense) read empty on the freshest completed slate → `signal_freshness_check` HALTed the whole daily job (starter_ip was the lone survivor only because its store parquet happened to carry the prior slate). **The stores + the generators were FINE** — pure build-ORDERING. CURE: the daily job now runs generators → `export_w9_signals_to_s3_op` (fan-in of all 8 gens; stores→S3) → `rebuild_sub_model_signals_consumer_op` (`run_w1_lakehouse.py --sub-model-signals-only` rebuilds ONLY the consumer parquet from the fresh stores + `refresh_w1_external_tables.py --sub-model-signals`) → `dbt_sub_model_signals_rebuild` (SF materialize) → gate. GENERAL RULE: when you cut a serving consumer over to an S3-parquet mirror of upstream stores, the parquet MUST be (re)built downstream of the store refresh in the SAME run, not at job start. `export_w9_signals_to_s3.py` also now emits an at-the-SOURCE empty-slate coverage ALERT so a truly-empty generator warns before the downstream HALT gate.
- **🗓️ A CUTOVER CAN MAKE A "NON-SERVING" UPSTREAM S3 PARQUET SERVING-CRITICAL WITHOUT WIRING ITS DAILY REBUILD (2026-07-02, silent serving degrade — INC-25 sibling):** the W11 migration froze the W4/W5 mart S3 parquet on the premise those marts "have NO request-time read — they feed the dbt feature build, which retains its Snowflake-native path" (so `--w5` was left OUT of the daily job, gated behind default-OFF `W11_W4W5_NIGHTLY`). The **W8b cutover invalidated that premise**: the served pregame feature aggregator (`feature_pregame_game_features_raw` → `feature_pregame_game_features`) now reads `mart_game_spine` (a W5 Group-A model) from S3 as a precursor view. With `--w5` not in the daily build, the spine **froze at its last manual build** → the pregame feature store lost the current slate (max game_date stuck) → `load_todays_features` failed its coverage gate → `predict_today` **silently** fell back to the intraday Stats-API assembly (degraded, patchy `post_lineup`). No HALT, no alarm — just `data_source='intraday_fallback'` in `daily_model_predictions`. **The `--w8b` build was fine; the game universe upstream was stale.** CURE: rebuild the spine (`run_w1_lakehouse.py --w5-only --w5-group-a-only` — Group A only; W5b reads the W8a EB posteriors so it stays in the post-`--w8a` slot) in `run_w1_lakehouse_op` **BEFORE** `--w8a/--w8b`, at the W8a mirror tier (HALT once cut over). `_build_w5` now emits a **spine-staleness ALERT at the source** (`_alert_stale_game_spine`: WARN to stderr if the built spine's scheduled universe doesn't reach `current_date`). GENERAL RULE: when a feature/mart cutover starts reading an S3 parquet that a prior wave decided not to rebuild daily, that parquet is now on the serving path — wire its daily rebuild (correctly ordered, before its consumers) or serving silently rots to a fallback.
- **📅 A MONTH-SCOPED INGEST HAS A MONTH-BOUNDARY HOLE, AND A CONSUMER ORDERED *BEFORE* ITS OWN INGEST ONLY SHOWS IT ON THE 1st (INC-37, 2026-08-01, P1 — the whole morning tier served on an amputated feature vector; it had ALREADY recurred silently on 06-01 and 07-01):** `ingest_statsapi.py schedule` fetches WHOLE calendar months (`iter_months` expands any range to month boundaries), so the last capture of July (`2026-07-31T23:30Z`) carried `2026-07-01..07-31` and **ZERO games for 08-01**. Meanwhile `daily_ingestion_job` ran the ENTIRE S3 lakehouse chain (`lakehouse_schedule_export_op`→W1→W2→W3→W3pre flatten→W6→W7b→spine/bridge→**W8a**→W8b, ops lk1..lk10) **BEFORE** `ingest_statsapi_schedule` (which sat at s6) → the build flattened a July-only schedule → `mart_game_spine`, every W1–W6 mart, the odds bridge and the **whole W8a feature layer** stopped at 07-31 → the served store had 15 rows for 08-01 with **5 of its 6 coverage blocks at 0%** (mean 0.178 vs the 0.70 gate) → `load_todays_features` fell to `intraday_fallback` on **all 15 games**, outputs went FLAT, and the Bovada target-book price wrote NULL slate-wide (a CONSEQUENCE — the E9.52 `::date` cast is fine; `mart_game_odds_bridge` simply had no 08-01 rows yet). ⭐ **THE SHAPE: mis-ordering a consumer ahead of its own ingest is INVISIBLE on 363 days a year** (yesterday's capture already covers today) **and DELETES THE WHOLE SLATE on the 1st** — deterministic, not a race, which is why it recurred monthly unnoticed. **RULES: (a) a consumer reading an S3 mirror must be rebuilt DOWNSTREAM of the refresh that feeds it IN THE SAME RUN (the INC-25 rule — now applied to the schedule; pinned by `test_schedule_build_ordering_guard.py`); (b) an ingest whose SCOPE is a calendar period has a BOUNDARY HOLE exactly as an ingest whose CADENCE is seasonal does (E9.48 (c)) — give it a forward LOOKAHEAD (`--lookahead-days 3`, so the last captures of a month also fetch the next) rather than relying on ordering alone; (c) ⚠️ `check_feature_block_coverage_op` CANNOT see this — it excludes the anchor date and asserts only over dates already in the store, so it is a store-HISTORY guard, never a TODAY guard.** 🔕 `_alert_stale_game_spine` DETECTED it correctly all three times and only ever wrote a stderr WARNING — the E11.30 finding verbatim; it now emits `[METRIC] spine_covers_today=` and `lakehouse_spine_odds_bridge_op` PAGES on it (`betting_ml/monitoring/spine_horizon.py`; an unevaluable/absent check is WARN, never scored healthy). ✅ All three monitors DID page via `send_alert` — the first real-incident validation of E11.30, and the only reason 08-01 was caught when 06-01/07-01 were not. ⚠️ **THREE downstream victims, not one — anything reading the game universe in that window scoped itself to July: (1) the feature build (above); (2) `ingest_weather`, which found no 08-01 games and NEVER captured a `forecast_pregame` observation for the slate (0 rows vs 9 on 07-30 / 14 on 07-31 — the intraday capture kept working but writes `forecast_intraday`, which `stg_weather_raw_snapshots` rejects by design), PARTLY UNRECOVERABLE once first pitch passes — the INC-34 shape applied to an INGEST, not a build; (3) the W11 serving tail (W11b umpire / W11c weather / W11d public betting) built on the July universe → ZERO 08-01 rows → blank front-end panels.** 🕳️ **VICTIM 3 IS INVISIBLE TO THE COVERAGE GATE — `_FEATURE_STORE_COVERAGE_BLOCKS` covers only lineup/starter/team_rolling/bullpen_eb/sequential/odds, so the gate read a healthy 0.878 while umpire+weather were 100% NULL and a serving table had 0/15 rows. ⇒ AFTER ANY TARGETED LAKEHOUSE REBUILD, EXPLICITLY VERIFY THE W11 TAIL — "the gate is green" is not "the slate is complete", and a targeted chain built around the gate's blocks will omit exactly what the gate cannot see.** ✅ **NOW AUTOMATED: `scripts/check_w11_tail_coverage.py` (manual/acceptance mode, `--strict`) + `check_w11_tail_coverage_op` (daily, ALERT-tier, pages via `send_alert` — see the op→tier table).** ⭐ **AND THE WIRING FOUND A SECOND, SEPARATE CADENCE DEFECT: two of the three W11 feeds land AFTER the build that consumes them** (measured UTC — `ingest_action_network` s4 @12:00 precedes the ~12:40 `lakehouse_w11_nightly_op`, but `ingest_weather` s7 writes `forecast_pregame` @12:50 and `ingest_umpires.py --date today` s8/s17 lands 12:0x–16:39), so **umpire and weather populate the current slate one build cycle late BY DESIGN** and only `public_betting` is a valid same-day assertion. A monitor that paged on all three same-day would have fired CRITICAL EVERY MORNING. **RULE (generalizes past this op): before paging on "the built table has not caught up with its feed", check whether that feed even lands before the build in the same run — and in a two-sided raw-vs-built read, the RAW predicate must match what the build actually CONSUMES** (the weather block counted `forecast_intraday` rows that `stg_weather_raw_snapshots` rejects by design → a permanent false PARTIAL). ⏳ **AND THE LINEUP SENSOR WRITES ONE-AND-DONE ROWS BEHIND YOU WHILE YOU REMEDIATE** (824405 was scored mid-fix at 19:44, 2 min before the weather rebuild landed, and `post_lineup` never re-fires a game that HAS a row) ⇒ re-check for NEW post_lineup rows after EVERY stage, and compare each row's `inserted_at` against the feature store's actual rebuild time (S3 `LastModified` via **boto3** = true UTC; never `aws s3 ls`), not merely "is pre-game and has a row". Full postmortem incl. the 3 refuted hypotheses for the `mart_derivative_closes` `UnicodeDecodeError`: `docs/inc37_month_boundary_schedule_hole.md`.
  - **🔙 …AND THE LOOKAHEAD IS ONLY HALF THE CURE — THE SAME BOUNDARY STRANDS THE *PREVIOUS* MONTH'S LATE GAMES FOREVER, AND ONE CALLER CARRYING THE FLAG IS NOT ENOUGH (INC-38, 2026-08-02).** INC-37's `--lookahead-days 3` is FORWARD-only: it fixes "the last capture of July holds no games for 08-01" and does nothing about "no capture ever revisits July after 08-01". Any game that first-pitches AFTER 00:00 UTC on the 1st — **every West-coast night game on the last day of a month** — is still Pre-Game/In-Progress in the final same-month capture and **never gets its Final + score written**. Measured live: **14 of 15 games on 07-31 were frozen non-final** in `stg_statsapi_games` (7 'In Progress', 7 'Pre-Game'), and the ONLY non-terminal historical games in the whole 2026 season sat on **06-30 (4) and 07-31 (14)** — the month boundaries and nowhere else, which is the signature. `settle_user_bets` grades h2h/totals only on `status_code in ('F','O')`, so **3 user bets on two of those games sat PENDING forever** — no error, no page, no self-heal (the E9.48 permanent-wrong-state class); the operator found it by reading the bet log two days later. ⚠️ **THE NEAR-MISS THAT MAKES THE STATUS GATE LOAD-BEARING: the frozen rows carry NON-NULL PARTIAL scores** (824486 sat at 5-3 In Progress) — a score-presence check alone would have settled live bets off a mid-game score. **RULES: (a) an ingest scoped to a calendar period needs a lookback as well as a lookahead (`--lookback-days 3`) — a boundary has two sides; (b) ⭐ THE FLAG MUST BE ON *EVERY* CALLER. The daily op has passed `--start-date <yesterday>` since 2026-07-15 for exactly this symptom and it DID NOT HOLD, because the raw writer uses `mode='overwrite_partition'` keyed on the FIRE DATE: each fire replaces the whole `dt=<today>` partition with only the months IT pulled, so the month-only INTRADAY tick minutes later CLOBBERS the daily's wider fetch. A "one logical job, two execution owners" bug (cf. INC-30 crontab / INC-36 concurrency) wearing a retention-policy costume. ⭐⭐ **AND THE CALLER SET IS FOUR, NOT TWO — INC-37 FLAGGED ONLY THE TWO DAGSTER OPS**, leaving `services/schedule_capture/entrypoint.sh` and `sensor_ops.lineup_ingest_schedule` with NO month-boundary flags at all. ⚠️ **AND ESTABLISH WHICH CALLER IS LIVE FROM THE DEPLOYED CONFIG, NOT FROM A DOCSTRING:** `sensor_ops` still says "the schedule_capture cron handles statsapi schedule ingestion every 30 min off Dagster's bill" — that is the pre-AWS **RAILWAY** arrangement. On the AWS box `schedule-capture` IS defined in `services/dagster/aws/docker-compose.yml` but is **NOT in `capture.crontab`**; the live 30-min capture is the Dagster `intraday_schedule_capture_{daytime,overnight}` schedule (`*/30 14-23` + `0,30 0-3` UTC — matching the observed 23:30:15Z / 03:30:13Z fires exactly). A session that trusts that docstring fixes the DORMANT image and calls it done. Cure: flag EVERY caller regardless of which is live today — which one is live is a DEPLOY-CONFIG fact that drifts underneath the code (the `W7B_LAKEHOUSE_S3` documented-but-never-set class). ⇒ **when a fix is a PER-CALLER FLAG, enumerate callers by grepping the SCRIPT NAME across `.sh`/`Dockerfile`/crontab as well as `.py` — a Dagster-op-only sweep systematically misses the lean capture images, which are usually the live writers — and pin the registry itself in a test** (`test_the_caller_registry_is_still_exhaustive`), because a per-caller flag fails exactly where the registry is incomplete. Include human-facing REMEDIATION STRINGS in that registry: `schedule_freshness_alert_sensor` prescribed a bare `ingest_statsapi.py schedule`, and the likeliest moment a human runs it is a boundary morning — i.e. the prescription re-opened the hole it was paging about; (c) DO NOT SETTLE A USER-VISIBLE RECORD OFF A TABLE THAT IS ONLY AS FRESH AS THE BUILD THAT WROTE IT — `settle_user_bets` now asks the Stats API directly when our own table reports no final (E9.54's theme, and the same independent-terminal-confirmation discipline E9.49 already applied to props), so a schedule-capture hole can never strand a bet again; (d) a "not final yet" skip is indistinguishable from "never will be" — add the AGE check (`stale_pending_bets`, >24h past first pitch) or the failure is structurally invisible.** ⚠️ **KNOWN RESIDUAL (verified, not speculated): `prune_same_month_partitions` is scoped to the FIRE's calendar month, so the re-fetched July blob lives in an AUGUST `dt=` partition and is deleted once the 3-day lookback window closes, at which point the flatten reverts to the stale `dt=2026-07-31` snapshot (confirmed still present at 1/15 final). ⇒ the lookback heals the LIVE window every daily consumer reads, but does NOT permanently heal history; a durable fix needs content-aware retention (keep the latest partition per CONTENT month, which needs a month column on the raw mirror). ⭐ **BUT MEASURE THE BLAST RADIUS BEFORE TREATING IT AS URGENT — it is far smaller than it sounds: `mart_game_results` (Statcast-derived, an INDEPENDENT pipeline) carries 15/15 SCORED for 07-31, so the RESULTS substrate was never affected; the reversion costs only `stg_statsapi_games.status_code` on 14 games (07-31) + 4 (06-30). Nothing serving-critical reads finality there after the fact — the signal generators use it for the game UNIVERSE not the status, `run_env` reads `mart_game_results` (the INC-34 correction), and settlement no longer reads it for finality at all. So this is a historical status inconsistency in ONE staging table, NOT a serving defect; schedule the retention fix, don't scramble for it.** Bets are immune regardless — which is exactly why decoupling settlement, not the lookback, is the primary cure.** ⚠️ **AND A SOURCE-INSPECTION GUARD IS VACUOUS IF PROSE CAN SATISFY IT (caught two-sided, in this story):** the first cut matched the flag ANYWHERE in the file, so the explanatory COMMENT written above each fixed command made the guard PASS on source with the flag DELETED from the actual command. **RULE: match a real ARGV form (`"--flag", "3"`) or strip comment lines first, and prove the guard FAILS on deliberately-broken source before trusting it** — a source-inspection test that cannot fail is the NF1.7 (a) vacuous-anchor class in a new costume. Guards: `betting_ml/tests/test_inc38_month_boundary_settlement.py` (pins ALL FOUR callers + the prescription string + the registry's own completeness, the terminal-status gate, a prose-cannot-satisfy check, and a two-sided stale check). Postmortem: `docs/inc38_month_boundary_settlement_hole.md`.
- **🏟️ SOURCE A GENERATOR'S GAME UNIVERSE FROM THE INTRADAY-FRESH TABLE, NOT A HEAVY-DAILY-REBUILT MART (INC-34, 2026-07-21, HALT — the INC-25 build-cadence sibling):** the offense/starter/run_env floor signal generators pulled their GAME UNIVERSE from the completed-only `mart_game_results`, whose S3 parquet is rebuilt ONLY by the heavy daily `--w5-group-a` step → it LAGS the intraday-fresh feature tables. A generator run during that lag (or a re-execute-from-a-later-step, common during a soak) drops the NEWEST completed slate → 0 offense coverage → `signal_freshness_check` HALTs the whole daily job. Proof: `offense_v2_signals` written 06:04 UTC vs `mart_game_results` 07:38 UTC (the generator ran before the mart rebuilt). **The generators + stores were FINE — pure source-table CADENCE.** CURE: all 4 floor generators now read the game universe from **`stg_statsapi_games`** (matching `starter_ip`, which already did) — it populates the moment a game goes Final; `home_score+away_score` is EXACT-parity with `mart_game_results` finals (verified every completed slate: identical game sets + identical total_runs), so `run_env` (which needs realized `total_runs`) works off `home_score IS NOT NULL` + `official_date` (DATE — dodges the INC-23 VARCHAR `game_date`). Guard test pins all four. **GENERAL RULE: a serving/signal-critical op's game universe / freshness anchor MUST come from an INTRADAY-fresh source (`stg_statsapi_games`), never a mart rebuilt only by a heavy daily step — else the op races the rebuild and drops the newest slate (a false HALT, or silent under-coverage).**
  - **⚠️ CORRECTION for `run_env` specifically — a GENERATOR THAT CONSUMES REALIZED SCORES MUST MATCH THE GATE'S SOURCE, NOT "the intraday-fresh one" (2026-07-24, run_env 0/10 HALT — the INVERSE of INC-34):** the INC-34 cure over-generalized. `signal_freshness_check` anchors its reference slate + coverage denominator on **`mart_game_results`** (`max(game_date) … where game_type='R' and home_final_score is not null`), whose scores come from **Statcast** (`max(post_pitch_home_score)` off `stg_batter_pitches`, rebuilt in-job by `--w1`/`--w5-group-a`). `stg_statsapi_games.home_score` is a **SEPARATE pipeline** (the StatsAPI monthly_schedule capture/flatten) — INC-34 verified VALUE parity but NOT TIMING parity, and on 7/24 the two disagreed: mart_game_results had the 7/23 finals while the schedule flatten still lagged → run_env (moved to stg_statsapi_games by INC-34) emitted 0/10 → false HALT (`W11_W3PRE_DAILY=1`, so NOT a skipped-flatten cause — purely the two independent completion pipelines racing). **CURE: `run_env` now sources its universe + `total_runs` from `mart_game_results` — the gate's OWN table — so a false HALT is structurally impossible in EITHER lag direction (the gate can only demand games that are in mart_game_results, which run_env now reads).** offense/starter/starter_ip STAY on `stg_statsapi_games` (they read no result column → their universe is a SUPERSET of the completed slate → always covers). **REFINED RULE: match a generator's completion/universe source to the FRESHNESS GATE's reference source — for score-consuming completed-only generators that means the gate's mart, NOT the intraday schedule feed.** Guard: `test_signal_generator_game_universe.py::TestRunEnvIsAlignedToTheGateSource` (INVERTED vs the other three).
- **🧯 DuckDB `filter_pushdown` mis-binds a BOOLEAN predicate pushed into a parquet-scan view** (`where is_current = true` over a parquet view → `UBIGINT != BOOLEAN` HALT; W8b). DESCRIBE doesn't trip it — only the full COPY does. CURE: MATERIALIZE that table as a physical DuckDB table (`W8B_MATERIALIZE_TABLES`) so there's no scan to push into.
- **🔠 Snowflake `VALUE:<key>` is CASE-SENSITIVE** — must match the parquet's stored field name EXACTLY. SF-mirrored upstreams (`SELECT *`→parquet) yield UPPERCASE cols; a hard `.lower()` in the generator → that column reads ALL-NULL through the ext table (SILENT). Emit exact described case.
  - **🧟 A RETIRED `SELECT *` MIRROR THAT STILL WRITES A CUT-OVER KEY CLOBBERS THE NATIVE LOWERCASE PARQUET (INC-31, 2026-07-09 — the F2 umpire-null recurrence + weather + public_betting):** after a feature/mart cuts over to a native DuckDB `--wN` build (lowercase parquet, lowercase ext DDL), any leftover `export_*_to_s3.py` `SELECT * FROM <sf model>` mirror that STILL writes the SAME `<key>/data.parquet` (preserving Snowflake's UPPERCASE case — the "do NOT force lower" comment) and runs LATER in the daily cycle OVERWRITES the native lowercase parquet with an UPPERCASE one → `GET(VALUE,'game_pk')` returns NULL for EVERY column incl. `game_pk` → the whole served block materializes 100% NULL on the CURRENT slate (historical rows survive: the served aggregator is an incremental MERGE). PARITY/CI-BLIND (never goes through the SF ext table). **RULE: when a model cuts over to a native `--wN` build, RETIRE it from every `SELECT *` export-mirror in the SAME change** — the native build must be the SOLE writer of that S3 key. Fix here: emptied `export_features_to_s3.FEATURE_TABLES` (umpire/weather/public_betting were the last 3). Detection: `OBJECT_KEYS(VALUE)` on the ext table shows UPPERCASE keys + a nested `VALUE` field (the mirror-of-a-mirror tell).
- **🗂️ Glob-dup:** an ext table's `**/*.parquet` glob unions a stale `part-0.parquet` + the new `data.parquet` → 2× rows. Before a wave builds a model another export still mirrors, remove it from that export's TABLES dict + `aws s3 rm` the stray.
- **🧪 PARITY (DuckDB-over-parquet) IS NECESSARY-NOT-SUFFICIENT:** it is BLIND to the entire Snowflake-ext-table read-bug class (binary-ts→garbage, VALUE:case→NULL, glob-dup) because it never goes through the SF ext table. **The cutover gate for any feature/mart migration = a per-ROW fetch through the actual `lakehouse_ext.*` table** (+ `predict_today` green on the box), not just parity.
- **🧠 DuckDB `memory_limit` must be BOX-AWARE, never hardcoded > physical RAM** (a hardcoded 11 GB on a 4 GB box never spilled → kernel OOM-killed the HOST incl. Dagster = INC-22 #4) → `clamp(0.6×RAM, 2, 11)`. (Box is now `r6g.large` 16 GB.)
- **INC-19 type-drift:** a dual-branch migration flips NUMBER→FLOAT → a downstream `incremental` table HALTs. Intended type change ⇒ edit the manifest + `gen_type_contract.py --write` in the SAME PR; a real flip ⇒ operator DROP+rebuild (`--full-refresh` MERGEs, doesn't DROP).
- **Mirror/parity-only ops = mirror-tier (ALERT-continue), NOT HALT,** until the cutover flag flips.
- **`run_w1_lakehouse` full-rebuilds history (~10 min) every run** → for DOWNSTREAM debugging (feature/serving), SKIP it; rebuild only the targeted chain.
- **🔁 A GITHUB `concurrency` GROUP DOES NOT SERIALIZE AN ASYNC SSM COMMAND — TWO `deploy.sh` RACED AND TOOK THE DAGSTER DAEMON DOWN (INC-36, 2026-07-29):** `orchestration_cd.yml` declares `concurrency: group: orchestration-cd, cancel-in-progress: false`, which reads like "deploys can never race." It is not sufficient: the job *sends* an SSM command and polls it, and the poll budget was **120×10s = 20 min against SSM `executionTimeout=1800` (30 min)**. Causal chain: a **HUNG `intraday_schedule_job` (>1h)** parked deploy.sh's drain loop for its full `DRAIN_TIMEOUT` (600s) → the deploy ran past 20 min → the poll loop exited **non-terminally** → the job failed → **the concurrency group RELEASED while the command was STILL RUNNING on the box** → the queued run launched a **second `deploy.sh` into a live one** → two concurrent `docker compose up` → `Error response from daemon: removal of container … is already in progress` → auto-rollback. **Then the rollback made it worse: it ran `up -d --no-build`, ignored the exit status, and never verified the box came back — leaving `dagster-daemon` GONE for ~10 min, so NO schedule and NO sensor ticked** (the E11.23 "silently never runs" class, and a dead daemon cannot page about itself). ⚠️ **The tell that a concurrent build happened: `COPY . . → CACHED` on a commit that changed 10 files** — impossible on a first build, so something else had already built that context. **RULES: (a) any poll budget MUST exceed the remote command's own timeout, else the orchestration lock releases while the work is live — and cancel the command if you abandon it; (b) a "deploy lock" that is only `touch`+trap is a SIGNAL, not a mutex — exclusivity needs an owner pid + `kill -0` liveness + a bounded stale reclaim, and the EXIT trap must be armed ONLY AFTER acquiring (else a refused deploy deletes the holder's lock); (c) a drain/health probe must never end in `|| echo 0` — that makes "unreachable" indistinguishable from "clear" (INC-32 class); (d) ⭐ AN AUTO-ROLLBACK MUST VERIFY THE SERVICES CAME BACK — a rollback that leaves a service down is a worse outcome than the failed deploy it reacted to.** Guards: `betting_ml/tests/test_deploy_concurrency_guard.py` (14 tests; **all 11 invariants verified to FIRE on the pre-fix source**). Sibling of INC-30 (crontab under two owners → every cron double-fires): the repo's recurring shape is **one logical job with two execution owners**.
- **🔓 A GUARDRAIL'S DENIAL PATH CAN BE STRUCTURALLY UNOBSERVABLE, AND THE FALSE PASS IS CONVINCING — the vacuous-guard class (NF1.7(a)/INC-38/E11.30) now shows up in OPERATOR RUNBOOKS + AWS CONSOLE STATE, where the repo's guard-TESTS cannot reach it (G100-D1 launch cost guardrails, 2026-08-08):** three separate launch checks each returned a HEALTHY-LOOKING answer while measuring NOTHING. **(A) ⭐ the COST kill-switch's DENIAL half cannot be verified anonymously** — a token-free `curl` of a non-floor route returns **`401` FROM THE GATEWAY AUTHORIZER (NF3.2) BEFORE the Lambda runs**, degrade ON or OFF, so a "blocked-looking" status proves nothing about the flag. This ALREADY caused a live false inference: an E9.46 P1 diagnosis eliminated the kill switch with "degrade is OFF (a non-floor public path returns 200)" — there is NO such path (all 13 `--authorization-type NONE` routes are also degrade-allowlisted BY DESIGN, so no anonymous request is ever refused). ⇒ **to establish a flag's state, READ THE FLAG (`aws lambda get-function-configuration --query 'Environment.Variables.COST_DEGRADE_MODE'`), never infer it from an endpoint's status.** **(B) ⭐ a CloudWatch billing alarm HID THREE STACKED DEFECTS, each masking the next, all presenting as a healthy alarm:** the metric didn't exist (account billing-alerts pref OFF) → `--treat-missing-data notBreaching` masked the absent metric as `OK` → after fixing both, CloudWatch LEFT THE STALE `OK` in place ("updating an alarm leaves its state unchanged"). **CloudWatch dimension matching is EXACT, not subset** — an account publishes `{ServiceName,Currency}`, `{Currency,LinkedAccount}` and bare `{Currency}` as DIFFERENT metrics, so a non-empty `list-metrics` is NOT proof; only `get-metric-statistics` on the alarm's EXACT dimensions settles it, and a billing alarm needs `--treat-missing-data missing`. **(C) an ALLOWLIST kill-switch entry taken from the router MOUNT name instead of the route DECORATOR silently DENIED the real pricing path** (`/subscription/public-pricing`, not the guessed `/stripe/public`) — an allowlist's characteristic failure is that a wrong entry doesn't error, it silently denies, surfacing only the once you flip the switch → resolve degrade allowlists against the app's REAL route table + test it. **RULE for every launch guardrail/alarm: answer "what would this look like if it were BROKEN?" before trusting a green — in all three here, broken and working were VISUALLY IDENTICAL, and a check whose failure state is indistinguishable from its healthy state has not been verified.** (The threat model also inverted the intuition: organic traffic is cheap — 100k visitors ≈ +$21/mo — the guardrails earn out entirely on ABUSE/scraping, ~$2,936/mo averted; a "Vercel-Pro spike" is a scraper problem, not a growth problem.)
- **🖼️ A SERVED PAYLOAD CAN CARRY AN ASSET URL WHOSE HOST MUST BE IN THE CSP — AND A GREP-OF-SOURCE GUARD MISSES IT BECAUSE THE HOST LIVES IN DATA, NOT CODE (E9.46, 2026-08-08):** NFL headshots were blocked by our OWN `img-src` CSP (it allowed the ESPN + MLB CDNs, never `static.www.nfl.com`) and had likely NEVER rendered. ⚠️ **A CSP refusal has NO server-side signal, and `player-page.tsx` falls back to INITIALS on image error → every blocked headshot presented as "this player has no photo," product-wide + invisible** (it surfaced only because the home card rendered one WITHOUT a fallback). **The host lives in the served PAYLOAD (a URL column), never in `frontend/` source — so a `grep frontend/` guard finds the code-built ESPN host and MISSES the data-carried NFL one entirely.** RULES: (a) when a served payload carries asset URLs, the CSP `img-src` must include those hosts, and the guard must read the PUBLISHED FIXTURES (the data), not grep the source; (b) an image component that falls back to initials makes a CSP/network block LOOK like missing data — the silent-failure class, in the browser.
- **🧾 A NEW PUBLIC LANDING-PAGE SURFACE MUST JOIN G100-D1'S THREE REGISTRIES OR IT SILENTLY UNDOES THE COST GUARDRAILS (E9.46, 2026-08-08):** G100-D1 moved the featured pick OFF the API Lambda onto the CDN; a new fantasy card added directly ABOVE it was still calling the Lambda PER VIEW → would have put one API-GW request + one Lambda invocation back on EVERY landing-page view, undoing half of G100-D1. RULE: any new public/anonymous landing surface joins ALL THREE — the CDN allowlist, the degrade floor, and the public cache rules — or it re-Lambdas the hot path. Rebasing onto a cost-guardrail change is exactly when this bites.
- **🪟 A VIEW-FLIP (or S3-repoint) OF A `merge`-INCREMENTAL IS GATED BY WHETHER A READER SPANS THE ACCUMULATED HISTORY, NOT BY THE READER *COUNT* (E11.24 #675, 2026-08-08):** a `merge` incremental NEVER deletes → the Snowflake table is a permanently accumulating SUPERSET of an S3 full-rebuild parquet (ghost rows of superseded snapshots; measured +3/+32 on the EB posteriors). So before flipping such a model to a view or repointing its readers to S3, the question is NOT "how many readers" but "does any reader consume ACROSS THE WHOLE HISTORY" — a date-scoped reader (`= current_date()`) structurally cannot see a ghost, a season-first-appearance / whole-history reader can. Repoint + parity-check the HISTORY-SPANNING readers (the parity gate's `only_in_sf` bucket = the consequential one: those keys lose their seed and get skipped). Two corollaries: (a) tables whose reads are UNIONed into one DERIVED MAP must move together (a derived map needs a single vintage, or a row's value depends on which side carried a ghost); (b) ⛔ `enabled=false` is NOT the cleaner end state until EVERY reader is repointed — a disabled model makes `ref()` unresolvable while DROPping nothing, so the relation lingers as a TABLE NOBODY REBUILDS (the silent-staleness bomb); a VIEW is the safe shape until then.
- **🏷️ A `served_*`-NAMED COLUMN ON A RESEARCH PANEL DOES NOT TRACK THE SERVING ARTIFACT — the "documented ≠ actually served" class, one level down (NF1.9-R, 2026-08-08):** NF-RECAL1's C3 gate read "the incumbent band" from a veteran panel's `served_p10`/`served_p90` columns — which `VET_PANEL_COLS` documents as "the LITERAL emitted PRE-NF1.9 band" (frozen so NF1.9's incumbent arm stays reproducible; they never tracked what shipped AFTER). So a gate named its bar off a column called `served_*` that is NOT the served band → C3 refused every level-recal arm against a `min(0.80, 0.50)` bar built on the WRONG band, when the band actually on the wire covers **0.845** on the tier. RULE: any gate needing "the incumbent/served band" must REFIT the current served config through the model path and PROVE which band it holds by reproducing a recorded SERVED figure — never trust a `served_*` COLUMN NAME on a research/panel table. Consequence carried forward: NF-RECAL1's + NF-D21's C3 refusals were computed against the wrong band ⇒ a PM re-read is owed (the direction says the corrected bar is STRICTER, so the refusal most plausibly stands — but that's a reading, not a measurement).
- **🚰 A VIEW-FLIP MOVES WAKE, IT DOESN'T DELETE IT — WAKE IS A QUEUE, NOT A SUM (E11.24 #679, 2026-08-08):** on an auto-suspending warehouse only the FIRST occupying statement after a suspend pays the resume; making it metadata-only (a `table`→`view` flip) PROMOTES the next statement in the same run to pay the resume instead. Measured: flipping the umpire/lineup CTAS moved 5–7 waits/day onto EB-posterior merges that had run for six days at ZERO waits, executions unchanged, total resumes FELL. ⇒ (a) a single flip in a build chain does NOT reduce the bill by itself — reaching literal-zero needs the WHOLE chain flipped, one-per-soak, and the wake WALKS DOWN THE LIST as you go (budget for N soaks); (b) size a flip's payoff by the wake it MOVES vs the wake that DISAPPEARS — only a flip whose successor is ALSO metadata-only (or lets the warehouse suspend) actually deletes wake. Verify a wake reduction by the warehouse SUSPENDING on a zero-game window, not by one statement's waits hitting 0.
- **🕳️ `warehouse_size IS NULL` MEANS THE QUERY NEVER OCCUPIED THE WAREHOUSE — awake-TIME attribution MUST filter it out; WAITS are immune (E11.24 #679, 2026-08-08):** cloud-services-billed statements — `SHOW`, `ALTER SESSION SET QUERY_TAG`, `ALTER EXTERNAL TABLE … REFRESH`, `CALL SYSTEM$…`, and EVERY `create or replace view` — can neither RESUME nor KEEP the warehouse awake, yet are 40–138% of a naive awake-minutes figure (a first cut ranked a Snowsight browser poll as the account's largest awake-time consumer = a phantom that would send a fix session at nothing). Filter awake-time on `warehouse_size IS NOT NULL`; a provisioning-WAIT is safe (a wait implies a real occupation). And the recurring companion: the AGGREGATE lies across a flip date (a 6-day window put an already-flipped view at the top of the wait board; per-day showed it 0) — only the PER-DAY cut is quotable.
- **🧪 A RED-PROOF HARNESS MUST ASSERT ITS MUTATION ACTUALLY LANDED BEFORE RUNNING THE TEST — a RED-proof that can silently NO-OP its own break reports a FALSE "the guard caught it", and it reads as a finding (E11.24 #682, 2026-08-08 — the vacuous-guard class one level up):** a first-cut RED-proof shelled out to apply deliberate source breaks and reported 2 guards "green on broken source" (read as a real defect) — but a shell-quoting bug meant the MUTATIONS NEVER LANDED, so the test passed because the source was UNCHANGED. RULE: a RED-proof must apply its mutation IN-PROCESS (or diff the file) and ASSERT THE SOURCE ACTUALLY CHANGED before invoking pytest — otherwise "the deliberate break went red" and "the break never happened" are indistinguishable, and the latter reads as the scarier (false) finding. (INC-38's prose-cannot-satisfy / NF1.7(a) vacuous-anchor, applied to the test-of-the-test.)
- **🎭 WHEN YOU GATE/NARROW A TIER, ENUMERATE THE RENDERERS, NOT THE ENDPOINTS — and a REDACTED value must not be ARITHMETIC-DERIVABLE from visible ones (freemium tier, 2026-08-08):** the served `projections.json` carries the paid scorings (`fpStd`/`fpHalf`) for EVERYONE by design — no server gate can help, because the gate is entirely WHICH COMPONENT PRINTS them (the board's format picker, the Projections reference-scoring picker, the player-page tiles). #681 gated ONE of those three and looked complete. ⇒ for any "gate/narrow tier X" story, the FIRST deliverable is an ENUMERATED list of every surface that RENDERS X, confirmed against the RUNNING app — derive scope from the renderers, not the brief's prose or the endpoint list (the NF-C0e "wired ≠ invoked" class, on the display side). AND a redacted value must not be recoverable by ARITHMETIC from visible ones: `half = full − 0.5·rec`, `standard = full − 1.0·rec`, so a visible RECEPTION COUNT hands over both withheld totals by subtraction (#684's third leak was arithmetic, not a column). ⛔ do NOT defend a paid stat-line as "anti-scraping" — the free board is scrapeable by design (accepted); a guard forbids the copy from drifting into that claim. Also: a disabled `<option>` is presentation, a state variable is not a gate — read a DERIVED effective-scoring value. ⭐ **AND ENUMERATE BOTH AXES — what RENDERS the gated thing AND what CREATES it (G100-C1, 2026-08-08):** "every personalized renderer gated" enumerated the DISPLAY surfaces but not the two league-CREATE paths, and one shipped ungated (the importer ignored the quota the manual editor enforced) — #681's lesson one layer over. For any tier/gate/quota story the FIRST deliverable is BOTH lists (renderers + creators), confirmed against the RUNNING app.
- **🎯 A DSR / DEFLATION FAILURE MUST BE READ FOR ITS MECHANISM BEFORE IT IS FILED AS POWER_LIMITED — a LOSE-BY-CONSTRUCTION arm in a declared field can veto a REAL finding through the bar alone (NF-W6b-C, 2026-08-15):** NF-W6b filed RB|rushing_tds as POWER_LIMITED at DSR 0.21; the winner (kNN, +13.0% CRPS, 8/8 folds) was real and BYTE-IDENTICAL to the successor — the null was an artifact of a pre-registered LINEAR-residual arm (Sharpe −9.2 on an 86%-zero cell) losing so hugely it inflated the cross-trial Sharpe DISPERSION that `SR0` deflates against (7.32 > the winner's own 6.47). ⛔ The fix is NEITHER ignoring DSR NOR trimming the field after the fact (MH2.2) — it is a FRESH REGISTRATION with a COHERENT family declared up front on mechanistic grounds (here atom-aware forms only, no linear arm) → same winner, same effect, `SR0` 1.33, DSR 1.0. This is the SECOND consecutive DSR/CONSTRAINT-refusal → fresh-registered-successor → SHIP conversion in the vertical (after MARGIN2→3 and W7→W7b) ⇒ a DSR failure over a HETEROGENEOUS declared field is a HYPOTHESIS ABOUT THE FIELD, not a verdict on the effect; read it for its mechanism first. ⚠️⚠️ **BUT IT IS A HYPOTHESIS, NOT A DIAGNOSIS — MEASURE THE 2×2 BEFORE PRESCRIBING A COHERENT RE-REGISTRATION. NF-W7f (2026-08-17) is the COUNTEREXAMPLE:** by then four cases (this one, MARGIN2→3, W7→W7b, NCAAF-S1) had made 'the field did it' the reflex, and a session applied it confidently and was WRONG — dropping the far-out arm cut cross-trial dispersion **V 8.8× (27.15→3.07)** and DSR still reached only **0.174** against a 0.95 bar. `classify_null`'s own `DSR_UNREACHABLE` text ('even a 2-arm field does not clear at this fold count and dispersion; the only lever left is a lower-variance design') was RIGHT and the field instinct was wrong, because the binding quantity was PER-FOLD NOISE in the delta (SR 1.013 at 8 folds), not multiplicity. ⇒ **compute DSR under the coherent field as a labelled DIAGNOSTIC before naming the remedy; if V falls hard and DSR barely moves, the remedy is VARIANCE (draws / a lower-noise estimator), and prescribing a re-registration would burn a story on the wrong lever.** ⛔ Either way, never a post-hoc trim (MH2.2). ⭐ SIBLING (the matched-n control, refined): a per-form peeking oracle's matched-n control must be sized to the PEEK'S EFFECTIVE n — under K-fold cross-fit the peek trains on only ⅔ of the block, so a full-block-size control is same-family but NOT same-sample (NF1.7 (b)) and reads a FALSE near-tie; size the control to the peek's n. And keep one oracle ceiling PER FORM (NF-D16 g‴) — a single field-wide ceiling falsely vetoes a legitimately-better nested form.
- **🧮 TWO INDIVIDUALLY-CORRECT §0.5 RULES JOINTLY MAKE DSR UNCLEARABLE — a pre-registered DEGENERATE in the TRIAL FIELD inflates DSR's `V` exactly like a huge winner (MH2.5, 2026-08-08; affects EVERY leg on the harness):** NF1.8 says "include degenerates to prove the metric is two-sided" and MH2 says "keep the full declared field in `n_trials`" — TOGETHER, a lose-by-construction degenerate that loses hugely + consistently inflates the CROSS-TRIAL Sharpe DISPERSION `V` (the `SR0 = √V·z(N)` term) exactly as a huge winner would, so the WHOLE-FIELD DSR becomes unclearable for a REAL winner. Same mechanism as NF1.8's "a deflation stat over a field containing its own nulls measures the nulls" + NF-D14's whole-field-vs-contender split, now traced to a specific rule INTERACTION (and it is why NF-B3's clean level_fix died only on whole-field DSR). `dsr_gate` already keeps the incumbent in `n_trials` while EXCLUDING it from `V`; whether pre-registered DEGENERATES get the same treatment is a STANDING CONVENTION decision that MUST be pre-registered FORWARD (⛔ adopting it after a failed gate is laundering — E2.1-r). `classify_null`'s remedy text inherits the same inflated `V` (the 3rd instance of that family). ✅ **SHIPPED DSR-CONV (2026-08-08, PR #689/#690): degenerate ∈ n_trials, ∉ V in `dsr_gate`, FORWARD-ONLY + INERT until a story opts in** (both figures reported, degenerate-excluded binds; MH2.5/NF-B3 nulls STAND). ⚠️ **SCOPE — reaches ONLY 2 MLB legs (`e7_9`, `mh2_5`):** the FANTASY vertical calls `deflated_sharpe` DIRECTLY (14/15 NF runners + NF-B3, UNTOUCHED); MiLB/prospect goes via `h_harness.dsr_report` (UNTOUCHED — and it drops anchors from `n_trials` too, more aggressive than DSR-CONV) — extending to either is a DELIBERATE per-vertical wiring decision that re-decides recorded legs if careless. ⭐ **EXCLUSION IS NON-MONOTONE = NOT A LEVER:** a designed loser lowers the bar ONLY when genuinely far-out (|Sharpe|≥3, +0.145 median); a marginal "degenerate" near the mean RAISES the bar (V is a sample variance → dropping near-mean points widens it) ⇒ an arm qualifies BY DESIGN, ⛔ never by DECLARATION, and the degenerate list is PRE-REGISTERED before the run (declaring after = laundering). ⭐ **IT DOES NOT FIX SMALL FOLDS** — that residue is the `√(n_obs−1)` fold-count penalty (Sharpe 13.74 needed at 3 folds vs 2.42 at 13), NOT field-composition (the `dsr_ceiling` does NOT bind at 0.95 — 0.9772 at 3 folds) ⇒ residual small-fold nulls are CALENDAR-bound, ⛔ NOT a contender-set problem (the 0.95 whole-field bar stays).
- **🎲 `NGBRegressor(random_state=…)` DOES NOT SEED ITS BASE LEARNER — identical-spec NGBoost fits disagree by up to 0.30 in per-game σ (MH2.5, 2026-08-08; repo-wide):** seed the GLOBAL RNG for any NGBoost harness (MH2.5 now reproduces to 1e-12). ⚠️ earlier NGBoost σ figures across E1.9 / E7.9 / MH2.x are NOT re-derivable to 4 decimals — the VERDICTS survive (the noise is small vs the effects they measured) but the exact figures can't be reproduced; note the caveat where they're recorded, don't re-run. Any NEW NGBoost harness seeds the global RNG.
- **🔢 A CALLER / CALL-SITE COUNT MUST BE A CALL-SITE REGEX ON COMMENT-STRIPPED SOURCE — `grep` hits on a function NAME overcount, because this repo NAMES GATES IN DICT KEYS AFTER THE FUNCTION (DSR-CONV #690, 2026-08-08; cost 3 wrong answers in one session incl. a SHIPPED vacuous guard):** `grep -c dsr_gate` counts `"folds_needed_for_dsr_gate"` (a dict KEY naming the gate) as a call site — so a guard that LOOPED over `dsr_gate(` matches per harness ran its body ZERO times for NF-B3 (its only hit was a dict key) and PASSED ON NOTHING (the NF1.7(a)/INC-38 vacuous-guard class, occurring INSIDE the guard written to prevent it; the suite was green throughout — the defect was found by MEASURING which legs call what, not by a test). RULES: (a) count callers with a CALL-SITE regex on comment-stripped source, ⛔ never `grep -c` a name; (b) any guard that ITERATES matches MUST assert NON-VACUITY (an empty match set passes on nothing); (c) pin the caller set as a MEASURED registry with an anti-vacuity assertion (the INC-38 exhaustive-registry rule, one layer in).
- **🧪 A COST-REPOINT/FLIP 'PROOF' RUN THAT SELECTS ZERO MODELS PROVES NOTHING — a build that builds nothing puts zero statements on the warehouse whether the repoint works or NOT (E11.24 CI-WH #775, 2026-08-14):** a CI-warehouse repoint (GitHub dbt CI off COMPUTE_WH → CI_WH) was RECORDED as proven, but CI_WH had carried 12 statements in its whole history, ALL `warehouse_size IS NULL` / 0 bytes (metadata-only teardowns), and `SHOW WAREHOUSES` showed it SUSPENDED with `resumed_on == created_on` = never resumed for work. The cited proof-run had CHANGED `profiles.yml` + the workflow (neither is a dbt model) → it selected zero models → it occupied NO warehouse, so it was silent on COMPUTE_WH regardless of whether the repoint worked. ⭐ RULE: to prove a warehouse repoint/suspend/flip actually MOVED work, the proof run must OCCUPY the warehouse with a REAL WRITE — a seed `INSERT` (e.g. `ref_teams`, 32 rows), never a view-only/metadata-only pick (which reproduces the non-proof — the E11.24 'metadata-DDL is free' corollary facing the wrong way). SIBLING (two-sided control, same story): a verifier clause 'DBT_RW ran nothing on COMPUTE_WH' would report NOT-PROVEN on a WORKING repoint in nearly any window (the box pipeline puts DBT_RW on COMPUTE_WH 19 of 24 hours) — a check that ALWAYS FAILS is as useless as one that always passes; key it on a discriminator only the target writes (`schema_name='CI_BETTING'`, which the box never writes) and prove it two-sided (green on heavy box traffic, FIRES on the pre-repoint burst). The NF1.7(a)/INC-38/INC-39 family: a proof/guard that cannot distinguish the two outcomes is not a proof.
- **🕐 MEASURE A MIRROR'S LAG AT THE READER'S WORST CLOCK TIME, AND DIFF ON THE UNIQUE KEY (NOT A NET COUNT) — both hid a real defect (E11.24 #693, 2026-08-08):** (a) an S3 mirror's staleness was STRUCTURALLY INVISIBLE at the in-job read (s15) because the Snowflake writer it's compared against hadn't run yet either → both sources returned the same value; only the OFF-CYCLE reads (a 17:30 host cron) saw the 38.72h lag, which had already breached the 36h threshold ⇒ **measure a mirror's lag at the reader's WORST clock time, not an arbitrary one** (an in-job read structurally hides it). (b) a NET row-count delta is NOT a difference measurement — `eb_starter_posteriors` showed net **+1** while **131 keys differed in BOTH directions** (28 game_pks disputed), because a window-scoped SF merge never re-merges an old game a later probable-pitcher revision corrected ⇒ **diff on the declared UNIQUE KEY in both directions (only_in_A / only_in_B), never the net count** — and know that reading the S3 full-rebuild doesn't only drop ghosts, it can CORRECT frozen historical keys the incremental SF merge never revisited (which STRENGTHENS an S3-repoint, but "ghosts only" is the wrong rationale for it).
- **📉 posthog-js SILENTLY DROPS EVERY CAPTURE UNDER PLAYWRIGHT — so every analytics assertion is VACUOUS until the bot detection is defeated (G100-C1, 2026-08-08):** posthog-js suppresses `capture()` when it detects automation, and it detects it via BOTH `navigator.webdriver` AND `HeadlessChrome` in `userAgentData.brands` — both must be defeated in the test harness (`captureAnalytics`) or NO event fires, which makes every analytics E2E assertion pass on nothing — ESPECIALLY a "the event did NOT fire" assertion (the vacuous-guard class in the browser). Any frontend session asserting on PostHog/analytics events MUST verify the capture actually reaches the wire first. ⚠️ SIBLING (same story): the per-IP rate limiter is PROCESS-GLOBAL + STATEFUL → one test file's requests deplete the bucket and the next file gets 429s that surface as PAYLOAD-SHAPE failures, not throttling → reset the limiter between Python test files that touch the API.
- **🧪 A RED-PROOF MUTATION ANCHOR MUST BE UNIQUE — two functions with BYTE-IDENTICAL TAILS make `replace(old,new,1)` mutate the WRONG one, and the run comes back GREEN reporting a FALSE 'the guard is VACUOUS' (E11.24 prediction_log, 2026-08-16; the 3rd way a RED proof lies):** `_post_lineup_delete_sql` and the new `_prediction_log_delete_sql` have byte-identical tails, so a `replace(old_string, new_string, 1)` break landed on the WRONG function → the guard under test never saw its mutation → the harness reported 'guard is vacuous' when the guard was FINE and the break had MISSED. ⚠️ a FALSE VACUITY report is the DANGEROUS direction — it reads as a real finding and invites WEAKENING a correct guard. RULE: a RED-proof must assert its mutation anchor is UNIQUE in the file (the target substring appears exactly once, in the function under test) before applying it. (The RED-proof-lies family: #682 'the mutation didn't LAND'; #815 'it landed but didn't MOVE the asserted predicate'; this = 'it landed on the WRONG symbol'.)
- **🧪 A RED PROOF THAT ASSERTS A SUBSTRING MUST ALSO ASSERT THE TOKEN IS *GONE* — a mutation that WRITES but doesn't change the asserted predicate comes back GREEN, and an `x in src` guard is blind to a SUFFIX RENAME (E11.24 target-2 / #815, 2026-08-16; the #682 landing-assert refined):** #682's RED-proof discipline asserts the deliberate mutation LANDED ON DISK (catching a break that silently no-ops). But one #815 break landed AND still didn't bite: the harness appended `_XX` to a mart name and `mart_bullpen_effectiveness_XX` still CONTAINS the substring the clause asserts. ⇒ #682 catches a mutation that doesn't WRITE; it cannot catch one that writes and doesn't change the ASSERTED PREDICATE. RULE: a RED proof asserting `<token> in src` must ALSO assert the token is ABSENT after the mutation (not just that the file changed), and know that an `x in src` guard is insensitive to a suffix rename — a `_v2`/`_XX` rename of a source mart would NOT turn such a clause red (the NF1.7(a)/INC-38 vacuous-guard family, one level finer: a break that lands but doesn't move the assertion is a false GREEN). ⭐ SIBLING (view-flip verification) — **a view-flip's runtime gate can only PASS after the next dbt build actually RUNS on the model; a read taken before that build is a PRE-FLIP BASELINE, not a gate pass — measure the RELATION TYPE (`SHOW VIEWS`/`SHOW TABLES`), don't infer it from a green deploy:** #815's post-deploy reads turned out to be baselines — `SHOW TABLES` still returned the TRANSIENT table (54,146 rows) because nothing had run dbt on the model since the deploy, so a parity 'pass' compared the old TABLE to the parquet. The clean before/after gate is `SHOW VIEWS=1 / SHOW TABLES=0` AFTER the next build. ⭐ SIBLING (drift-baseline) — **read a DRIFTING coverage block against its RECENT baseline, not its HEALTHY one:** #815 carries `bullpen_eb` (bp_eb_xwoba), which had drifted 99.8%→87.0% BEFORE the flip (the still-open 2026-07-29 P2, partial not whole-slate: `date_outage_count=0`); reading it against 99.8% would make a benign continuation look like flip damage AND let real damage hide inside the gap — compare against 87.0%.
- **🕵️ `last_altered` CANNOT DATE A SNOWFLAKE VIEW FLIP, AND A CI-ONLY WAREHOUSE HAS TWO SILENT-FALLBACK TRAPS (E11.24, 2026-08-10):** (a) every `create or replace view` refreshes `information_schema.tables.last_altered`, so after flipping a `merge`-incremental to a VIEW the transition is INVISIBLE in `last_altered` — it is legible ONLY in the statement history, where the `merge into <table>` STOPS existing. ⇒ a view-flip is a DELETE-THE-STATEMENT lever, judged on the statement's PRESENCE/ABSENCE, never on `last_altered` or a wait COUNT. (b) `SNOWFLAKE_WAREHOUSE` is NOT read by dbt when `profiles.yml` hardcodes `warehouse:` — a declaration with no consumer (the wired-≠-invoked class); set the warehouse on the target's `env_var` and verify through dbt's OWN rendering, not the env. (c) an EMPTY secret makes the warehouse key vanish from the rendered profile and dbt SILENTLY falls back to the connection default (here COMPUTE_WH) while `dbt debug` prints 'All checks passed' → guard with a `||` fallback + assert the ACTUAL warehouse in `query_history`, never trust `dbt debug`.
- **🚨 A STANDING `dev→main` PR CAN SHIP AN EARLIER `dev` WITH EVERY SIGNAL GREEN — VERIFY `main`'s FILE CONTENT, NEVER THE MERGE STATUS OR A GREEN CD (E11.24, 2026-08-10):** an already-open `dev→main` PR (#718) merged 05:25Z carrying a `dev` snapshot from BEFORE #662/#675 landed; its CD ran `completed/success` and `main` had ZERO occurrences of the change (`materialized='view'`). A green CD + a merged PR proved NOTHING — only grepping `main`'s actual file content caught it. RULES: (a) after any `dev→main` merge, verify the CHANGE IS PRESENT IN `main` (`git show origin/main:<file> | grep -c <thing>` must be ≥1; `git rev-list --count origin/main..origin/dev` must be 0) — merge status and CD conclusion are both green for a STALE merge; (b) SUB-TRAP: a MERGED PR returns `mergeable=UNKNOWN` byte-identically to a not-yet-computed OPEN one, so a naive 'retry until mergeable is known' loop spins forever and reads as a GitHub outage — check `state` (merged/open/closed) BESIDE `mergeable`, never `mergeable` alone. SIBLING (extends INC-27): the INC-27 'grep every CONSUMER before dropping/flipping an object' rule is about READERS — but flipping a table to a VIEW also breaks every WRITER (`compute_starter_posteriors.py`/`compute_lineup_posteriors.py` MERGE INTO the flipped objects; a MERGE INTO a view fails). 'No reader blocks it' ≠ 'no writer does' — grep for `MERGE INTO`/`INSERT`/`UPDATE` on the object too (both here were unwired from every job so safe, but a hand-run now fails). And a FALSE PREMISE IN A CODE COMMENT is worse than in a doc — #675's comment claimed `_FRESHNESS_QUERY` was 'already DEAD (W8B_FRESHNESS_S3=1)' but it ran on 4 measured dates (the flag isn't in `env.required`) → the next reader takes `enabled=false` off the comment; the W7B documented-≠-set class, in a comment.
- **💵 A VENDOR COST API CAN CARRY TWO $-FIELDS THAT ANSWER DIFFERENT QUESTIONS — the wrong one is a STRUCTURAL ZERO that looks like a working integration (E9.62, 2026-08-12):** Vercel's FOCUS billing feed has `BilledCost` (the amount reaching the INVOICE — identically 0.00 while consumption sits inside the plan allowance; all 17,304 Aug lines were $0) and `EffectiveCost` (amortized/attributed — what the vendor's own dashboard shows). #740 summed `BilledCost`, read $0, the seat floor supplied a synthetic $20, and the page had no way to say the number wasn't measured — a working-LOOKING integration reporting a fabricated number. RULES: (a) validate any cost field against the VENDOR'S OWN DASHBOARD figure before trusting it (a $3.90 reconciliation proved which field was right); keep BOTH where they answer different questions (EffectiveCost = the cost/drawdown gauge; BilledCost-ex-seat = a binary 'have we crossed the allowance' alarm).
