Plus 3 more vacuous guards found by BREAKING SOURCE not by a green suite (a `product:"fantasy"` clause unfalsifiable at 4 carriers; an `item.link` substring clause that survives the render disabled = wired-not-invoked; a red-proof break that hit a stripped doc comment) — the NF1.7(a)/INC-38/NF-D17 class, reconfirmed.
- **🧬 A SHARED REGEX/PARSER CONSTANT THAT LOOKS REUSABLE CAN SILENTLY MATCH ZERO — no error, just an empty parse (NF-W2c-CBS, 2026-08-09):** the nfl.com row regex `_NFL_TR_RE` is a BARE `<tr>` (nfl.com's real HTML); reusing it for CBS — whose rows are `<tr class="TableBase-bodyTr">` — matched 0 rows and returned an empty parse with NO error. Caught only by testing the real MODULE function against real cached bytes (an ad-hoc scratch regex worked; the shared module constant didn't). RULES: (a) a parser reused across sources needs a PER-SOURCE constant verified against that source's REAL bytes — a shared constant that "looks reusable" is a silent-empty (the NF-C0e wrong-key / silent-empty class, on the parse side); (b) test the actual MODULE function on real captured bytes, ⛔ not a scratch regex that happens to work. SIBLING (same story): `build()` writes to HARDCODED report filenames regardless of which sources are in scope → a partial-source (CBS-only) run silently OVERWROTE the tracked full-source `nf_w2c_wayback_injuries.{json,md}` report (caught via `git status` before commit, restored). A runner that writes fixed output paths clobbers a prior run's artifact on any PARTIAL re-run — scope the output filename to the run, or `git status` before committing.
- **🗜️ ARCHIVED / FETCHED BYTES ARE BYTES — DECODING A GZIP BODY AS TEXT WITH `errors="ignore"` SILENTLY STRIPS THE MAGIC BYTE → MOJIBAKE THAT PARSES TO A FALSE NULL (NF-W2c, 2026-08-09; near-fatal — nearly killed the story's only viable lead):** Wayback bodies fetched + decoded with `errors="ignore"` turned 0.5–1.1MB of gzipped HTML/JSON into 40–60KB of mojibake that parsed to ZERO designations across ALL THREE sources → a confident "every lead is a dataless JS shell" verdict that was WRONG. Diagnosed by inspecting the RAW BYTE PREFIX (the gzip magic `1f 8b`); refetched as BYTES + gunzip → parseable. RULES: (a) fetch archive/web bodies as BYTES, check the gzip magic, gunzip BEFORE decoding — ⛔ never `.decode(errors="ignore")` a possibly-compressed body (it hides the corruption as SILENT DATA LOSS); (b) a "no data anywhere" verdict out of a fetch pipeline is SUSPECT — inspect the raw byte prefix before concluding the source is empty (the silent-empty class, on the fetch side). SIBLING (same story): a CDX-listed archive capture can 404 on REPLAY → skip-not-abort (count + log the missing coverage, ⛔ never zero-fill it), or one unreplayable capture aborts the whole crawl.
- **⏱️ A LAGGED ZERO IS NOT A MISSING EVENT — an analytics store's eventual-consistency lag makes a HEALTHY funnel present as its SCARIEST shape (G100-D0, 2026-08-09; the NF1.7(a) vacuous-anchor class facing OUTWARD at PostHog):** a `capture()` at 20:12:55 UTC was not queryable until ~20:18 — PostHog's client-batch→ClickHouse path ran 3–5 min. A diagnostic run seconds after a live walk read the already-fired `user_signup_completed` as ZERO and printed "SIGN-UP STARTED BUT NEVER COMPLETED … context LOST across the redirect" over a COMPLETELY HEALTHY funnel (cost an hour debugging working code + a false "signup is broken" report to the PM). ⭐ WORSE in a MULTI-STEP REDIRECT flow: the START event lands a batch EARLIER than the COMPLETE, so a healthy walk TRANSIENTLY shows start-without-complete = the exact false alarm. RULES: (a) a zero from an analytics read has TWO byte-identical causes (never-happened vs not-yet-materialized) — gate any "event missing" alarm behind a LIVE-EDGE grace window; (b) the grace window must be MEASURED, not guessed — the first 180s value was proven too tight (it would expire while the event was still invisible and re-arm the alarm it was written to prevent); set it from the observed lag (300s here). PostHog-scripting siblings: `GET /insights/?dashboards=<id>` 500s server-side → read `tiles[].insight` from the dashboard detail instead (also the better question — a name-matched global list can hit another dashboard's insight); `description` has an undocumented 400-char limit that 400s MID-apply (partial provision) → catch offline with a build-time raise so `--dry-run` fails first.
- **⚛️ A `"use client"` CONSTANT IS `undefined` IN A SERVER COMPONENT — builds pass, types pass, the prop is silently EMPTY (G100-D0, 2026-08-09; Next.js App Router):** a constant imported from a `"use client"` module INTO a SERVER component resolves to a client REFERENCE, not its value → the prop arrived `undefined` at runtime (here `surface` on the home page's `landing_view`), invisible to BOTH `tsc` and `next build`. CURE: split the PURE contract (constants/types — NO `"use client"`, no client-lib import) into its own module, separate from the EMITTERS (the `"use client"` + posthog side); server components import only the pure module. SIBLING (same story, also runtime-invisible): an event can OUTRUN the auth-session restore → a super-property (`free_paid_status`) arrives `undefined`; gate the emit on `!loading`.
- **🔬 VERIFY THE ARTIFACT YOU ACTUALLY SHIPPED, AT THE SCALE YOU SHIPPED IT — a clean single-slice SMOKE + a green BUILD LOG can hide a full-scale grain/dup defect (MLB Batter Props Ph1, 2026-08-09):** a name-variant grain split ("Matt Duffy"/"Matthew Duffy" → one batter_id) shipped 11,461 DUPLICATE keys — each pair carrying different lines/probabilities, splitting one player's books into two competing half-consensuses — behind a PASSING single-season smoke and a SUCCESSFUL build log. THREE of that story's four defects surfaced ONLY from post-hoc verification of the shipped artifact, never from CI or the build log. RULES: (a) verify the artifact at the ROW COUNT + GRAIN you actually published (a full-scale key-uniqueness/grain check), ⛔ not a small-slice smoke — a smoke that fits inside one season cannot see a cross-season dup; (b) a grain/uniqueness invariant belongs as a BUILDER-LEVEL RAISE, not a test alone (a test guards the code path; a raise guards the SHIPPED write); (c) a consensus/aggregation must key on RESOLVED IDENTITY, never a display-string (two spellings of one id = two competing half-consensuses — the NF-C0e wrong-key class, on the grain side). SIBLING (cost hygiene): a costed-pull budget figure must READ THE LIVE `x-requests-remaining` HEADER, ⛔ never a docstring's stale "~N credits/month" (shipped stale once, retracted). And a "population floor" claim (the earliest usable date / fold count) must be verified on the RIGHT endpoint — the repo's prior batter-props probe ran the WRONG endpoint and was never evidence; a data-limit that caps DSR power (here 6 half-season folds from a 2023-05-03 floor) is a design quantity to MEASURE, not inherit.
- **🔤 A `UnicodeDecodeError` OUT OF `conn.execute` IS A *DESTROYED DUCKDB DIAGNOSTIC*, NEVER A BAD BYTE IN YOUR SQL — and the reported OFFSET is the one-question test (INC-43, 2026-08-13; INC-37's carded follow-up, fixed at one call site, recurring at another):** the DuckDB client converts the C++ exception string to `str`; if that string carries raw bytes (an httpfs response body, a parquet fragment) **the conversion raises `UnicodeDecodeError` and the real error is thrown away** — you get `'utf-8' codec can't decode byte 0xfc in position 15639` and nothing else. INC-43 downed a whole slate and the card traced it to "a non-UTF-8 byte in the model SQL"; **measured: the model file is clean UTF-8, the extracted duckdb branch is 305 pure-ASCII bytes, and the whole statement is 350 bytes — 45× smaller than the reported offset**, so the byte provably came from DuckDB's own >15KB message. **RULES: (a) ⭐ COMPARE THE REPORTED OFFSET AGAINST THE SIZE OF THE THING YOU THINK IT INDEXES — an offset past the end of your SQL/file settles it in one measurement and stops the whole file-encoding hunt; (b) READ THE TRACEBACK'S FRAME — a `read_text()` failure raises at the READ line and inside `pathlib`, an execute failure at the `conn.execute` line; the card had the frame and reasoned past it; (c) the cure is `UnicodeDecodeError.object`, which carries the FULL undecodable bytes — decode with `errors="replace"` and re-raise IN FULL (⛔ never head-truncate, INC-42) — but install it ON THE CONNECTION, ONCE (`_DiagnosticDuckDBConn` in `run_w1_lakehouse.py`), NOT per call site: INC-37 wrapped one of 67 `conn.execute` sites and INC-43 landed on a different one; (d) the underlying failure is usually TRANSIENT S3/httpfs (the INC-42 family — the INC-43 statement is a `read_parquet('s3://…**/*.parquet')` view whose bind takes ~87s), so re-running the failed step is the mitigation, and the salvage is what makes the NEXT one diagnosable — the identity of THIS one is permanently unrecoverable.** ⛔ Do NOT "fix" it with `read_text(errors="ignore")` — that silently mangles SQL that then gets executed (NF-W2c). Guard: `betting_ml/tests/test_inc43_duckdb_error_salvage.py`; postmortem `docs/inc43_stg_batter_pitches_unicode.md`.
- **🧮 A COVERAGE/PRESENCE GUARD IS VACUOUS WHEREVER A GAP-FILLER EXISTS — count the rows that CARRY THE VALUE (projected / non-null), not the rows (NF-K1, 2026-08-16; the vacuous-guard family, publish-artifact edition):** the 2026 fantasy board shipped with K/DST silently GONE — but a naive `assert_published_position_coverage` that checks 'does each PROJECTABLE position have rows' would have PASSED the exact broken artifact it exists to catch, because `kdst_records` GAP-FILLS placeholder rows (the board carried 32 K + 32 DST rows with `pts: null`). The guard must count PROJECTED rows (a non-null projection), not rows. RULE: any presence/coverage guard on an artifact a gap-filler can pad is vacuous unless it counts the rows CARRYING the value — a row-count check is satisfied by the placeholders. ⭐ SIBLING (NF-INFRA1, 3rd instance + DATE THE ONSET) — **the K/DST parquet is the one artifact the box's publish chain READS but never WRITES → gitignored (`*.parquet`) → absent from the `COPY . .` image → `load_kdst_local` warn-and-continue → exit 0 → the board published without K/DST and every check passed** (after `sports.duckdb` + `ncaaf_nfl_rookie_projections.parquet`; enumerate the chain's OTHER read-but-never-written artifacts — this was the last). ⚠️ the ONSET was NOT the obvious suspect: the 08-15 by-hand publish was FINE (868 players, 42 K + 32 DST); the regression was the FIRST FIRE of the automated `sports_nfl_board_publish_schedule` (NF-INFRA1 had flipped it `default_status=RUNNING` the day before) — a schedule newly flipped RUNNING fires its first regression the next day. DATE THE ONSET (publisher × timestamp) before accepting a plausible cause, or a fix aims at the wrong change. CURE: local-first-then-LAKE read (the projection already lives in the lake) + the projected-row-count publish guard + the box-CD-path-filter (the fix reached the box only because the PR incidentally edited a `pipeline/` docstring → 8 box-executed paths were missing from `orchestration_cd.yml`; add them, guard by transitive-closure re-derivation — which caught `fantasy_engine`, the actual board scorer, one hop outside a direct-reference sweep).
- **💣 A GITIGNORED ARTIFACT A BUILD *READS* IS A DEPLOY-EPHEMERAL TIME BOMB — before running a laptop-proven chain on the box for the FIRST time, enumerate every artifact it READS but does not WRITE and check each against .gitignore (NF-INFRA1, 2026-08-15; bit TWICE in one story):** the `sports.duckdb` the NFL build reads is gitignored → absent from the `COPY . .` image, `/tmp` deploy-wiped, `/app` image-replaced → `duckdb.connect(read_only=True)` RAISES in 114ms → a bare `except` returns SUCCESS (the 19-green-runs class); then the SAME story's board build died on `ncaaf_nfl_rookie_projections.parquet` — also gitignored, also a laptop-run output, also never in the image. ⛔ **Copying such a file onto the box is NEVER the cure — `/app` is replaced by every deploy;** the fix is a persistent VOLUME (for a WRITTEN artifact) or a LAKE fallback (for a READ one, local-first so a laptop build stays byte-identical while the box reads the lake instead of dying). ⭐ SIBLING — **a POST-DROP coverage statistic is 100% BY CONSTRUCTION and cannot validate the join that produced it** — measure coverage BEFORE the drop, or the instrument can't tell a healthy crosswalk from a dead one (NF-INFRA1's refusal-floor derivation cited the post-drop rate — a self-fulfilling ~100% — and was corrected in #821; the floor's VALUE was right, its justification wasn't). ⭐ SIBLING — **`SPORTS_DUCKDB_PATH` unset across 4 owners had one gate reading a file NOTHING WROTE = permanently fail-open since it shipped** (the INC-30/36/38 one-logical-thing-many-owners class — a required path/flag goes in `env.required` + the box's live `.env`, verified in the job-EXECUTING container per FU-1; two DuckDB paths never bought isolation, they only produced a gate reading a file nothing wrote). ⭐ SIBLING — **a Dagster schedule that ships `default_status=STOPPED` and isn't in `check_monitors_healthy_op` can SILENTLY REVERT** — its ON state lives only in Dagster Postgres, so a volume reset / re-host un-sets it, the job freezes, and nothing pages (the 'a job that STOPS running' class, one level up from 'a job that runs and produces nothing'). And a red proof must run under the PROJECT interpreter (`uv run pytest`) — a bare `python3` with no pytest makes a missing-pytest non-zero exit read as 'the guard caught it' (a false RED).
- **🧊 A SCHEDULED WRITER ONLY FIXES STALENESS WHEN IT HAS SOMETHING LIVE TO WRITE — mirroring a DEAD SOURCE on a timer + an INC-41 SLA is a GREEN-FOREVER false-green reached from the WRITER side (stg_ref_players fix, 2026-08-18):** the card said 'schedule export_ref_players_to_s3.py + add an INC-41 freshness SLA.' It would NOT have worked: the Snowflake SOURCE (savant.ref_players) is itself DEAD — frozen 2025-10-13 (~308d, not the parquet's 53d), max mlb_played_last=2025, NO writer anywhere in the repo. A scheduled export would re-copy the same 25,900 rows on a timer, refreshing the object's MTIME while the CONTENT stayed 2025, and an INC-41 SLA reading that mtime would then read GREEN FOREVER — precisely the false-green INC-41 exists to prevent, reached from the WRITER side instead of the reader side. RULE: before scheduling a mirror/export to cure staleness, verify the SOURCE actually advances (its own last_altered / max(date) / a live writer) — a green SLA over dead content is worse than a visible gap. CURE here: layer a LIVE feed (player_profiles_raw, Statcast-derived) over the frozen export, and fix at the S3 PREFIX (not the 11 raw-string consumers — INC-27) so one column contract repairs all; the publish guard counts rows CARRYING the value, not rows (NF-K1). ⭐ SIBLING (a branch the data never SELECTS is untested, however green the suite): a prod BinderException (an out-of-scope `l.` alias in the LIVE CTE arm) bound fine for WEEKS because every smoke took the FALLBACK arm (`cast(null as varchar)`, no columns yet) — the aliased arm executed for the FIRST time in prod right after the backfill made it reachable. RULE: run BOTH arms of a data-conditional branch, PARAMETRISED, with a fixture assertion that each arm is genuinely REALISED (a smoke over data that only reaches one arm is structurally incapable of catching the other arm's bug). ⭐ SIBLING (a hand-run sequence has NO dependency graph): the pipeline WIRING enforced the INC-25 mirror-re-export-between-backfill-and-rebuild ordering correctly, but the operator RUNBOOK doing the same steps by hand OMITTED it → a runbook reproducing a pipeline's steps must also reproduce its ORDERING constraints (the mirror re-export is weekly; backfill writes Snowflake only; the builder reads the S3 mirror — nothing between them re-exports unless you say so).
- **🟢 19 GREEN 'SUCCESS' RUNS ARE EXACTLY WHAT TOTAL BREAKAGE LOOKS LIKE WHEN A WARN-TIER OP OPENS A GITIGNORED, DEPLOY-EPHEMERAL ARTIFACT — the run STATUS carries ZERO information (NF-FRESH1, 2026-08-15):** `sports_nfl_sleeper_injuries_schedule` ran 19 consecutive daily SUCCESS runs while `nfl/raw/sleeper_injuries` held ONE commit for 19 days. The op dies at `duckdb.connect()` in 114ms — BEFORE it ever calls Sleeper — because (a) `SPORTS_DUCKDB_PATH` is UNSET on the box (only `sports_dbt_ci.yml` sets it; two ops in the job fall back to THREE different default paths — ingest→`sports_dbt/sports.duckdb`, rebuild→`/tmp/sports_ncaaf.duckdb`, profiles.yml→a third = one path, three owners, none authoritative, the INC-30/36/38 shape), (b) `*.duckdb` is gitignored → ABSENT from the `COPY . .` image → `connect(read_only=True)` RAISES, (c) the op's bare `except Exception` catches it → returns SUCCESS (WARN-tier by design). And `/tmp` is wiped by a deploy, so a DAILY op depended on a WEEKLY-rebuilt deploy-ephemeral artifact (INC-25). ⭐ **RULES: (a) a heartbeat would have been GREEN all 19 days — only an ARTIFACT-FRESHNESS SLA read from INSIDE the Delta/parquet catches a WARN-tier op that succeeds while writing nothing (INC-41); (b) a WARN-tier op that opens a gitignored / `/tmp` / deploy-ephemeral file must FAIL LOUD on a zero-row land, not swallow-and-succeed; (c) 'the schedule is STOPPED' is a claim to VERIFY against the box run list — here BOX_OPERATIONS §10 documented BOTH NFL schedules STOPPED and both were RUNNING (the documented-≠-actual class, the other way).** ⭐ **SIBLING — A PARTIAL SILENT LAND IS WORSE THAN AN UNAMBIGUOUS BREAK:** the tempting fix ('make the duckdb optional, land Sleeper's native `gsis_id` rows') was MEASURED to land only ~17% (native `gsis_id` = 16.7% of rostered / 22.1% of flagged) and DROP 95 of 122 flagged players (incl. Waddle, Pacheco) while reporting SUCCESS and advancing the artifact daily — strictly worse than today's loud break. The '100% gsis-matched' docstring was native+crosswalk, misread as native-dominant → re-measure a coverage claim THROUGH THE MODULE'S CODE PATH before routing around a crosswalk. ⭐ **SIBLING — THE SEASONAL CRON CLIFF:** both NFL ingest schedules are month-scoped `3-8`, so from 09-01 nothing ingests rosters/depth/injuries through the opener + week 1, exactly when the injury report starts publishing — a month-range cron is a SEASONAL BOUNDARY HOLE (E9.48(c)/INC-37), and the Sep–Feb schedule rebuilds MARTS over raw that stopped advancing. Grep a schedule's month-range before trusting it covers the season you need.
- **🪞 FOUR E11.24-BUNDLE LESSONS FOR MIRROR/EXPORT + MERGE-WINDOW WORK (2026-08-15):** (a) ⭐ **A NEW S3 MIRROR MUST BE BORN DOWNSTREAM OF ITS WRITER** — adding a table to an existing exporter's DEFAULT set installs the INC-25 trail AT BIRTH (mirrored at lk10, the writer runs later, nothing re-exports after → permanently one cycle behind); the one-line-cheaper option IS the defective one. Wire the re-export as a fan-out LEAF downstream of the writer in EVERY job that runs it (INC-38), from day one. (b) ⭐ **DuckDB BINDS A PARQUET VIEW AT `CREATE` TIME, NOT LAZILY (measured, duckdb 1.5.3: `IOException: No files found` fires immediately)** — so a BATCH `register_lakehouse_views` over many prefixes aborts the WHOLE connection on the FIRST absent prefix, BEFORE any entry is read, blinding every downstream freshness check (the savant.batter_pitches 2026-07-06 outage, one layer earlier than the per-table try/except added for it can reach). Register PER-TABLE so one missing prefix SKIPs one entry; a NEW mirror's prefix doesn't exist until its leaf first runs, so this fires on the first post-deploy run. ⚠️ do NOT isolate a game-day-only probe (a dead `stg_statsapi_games` must fail LOUD or every game_day_only entry silently SKIPs). (c) **A FAST-GATE TEST MUST NEVER CALL THE REAL `duck()`** — it creates a DuckDB S3 secret needing a live AWS chain, so it PASSES on a credentialed laptop and FAILS in CI; verify locally with `env -u AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID …` before pushing (the box AKID-chain class, on the test side). (d) ⭐ **TEST-MERGE EVERY OPEN SIBLING PR BEFORE A MERGE WINDOW** — a textual conflict resolved by reflex `git checkout --theirs` takes the WHOLE file and can SILENTLY revert a sibling PR's flips (here #772's two source→s3 flips reverted to snowflake with NO test failing, because the bundle's own guard permitted exactly those two), and the sibling's guards may go RED on the union because they encode the world you just retired → re-anchor, don't delete. ⛔ And the framing: clearing one waker buys NO credit when wake is a QUEUE (#679) — do NOT book a saving for a flip that merely shortens the queue; the resume lands on whatever fires next.
- **⏱️ A PERIODIC JOB'S TIMEOUT IS ONLY MEANINGFUL RELATIVE TO ITS CADENCE — a default copied from a daily batch job IS "no timeout" on a 30-minute tick, and the reflex INC-32 diagnosis ("a subprocess is missing a timeout") sends you looking for a bug that is not there (E11.26, 2026-08-19; the 7/29 hang behind INC-36).** `intraday_schedule_job` ran **>1h** on 2026-07-29 and parked `deploy.sh`'s drain for its full `DRAIN_TIMEOUT`, which released the `orchestration-cd` concurrency group while the SSM command was still live and let a second deploy race the first (INC-36 fixed the DEPLOY; the hang stayed open). ⛔ **It was NOT a missing timeout — an AST sweep of `pipeline/`/`services/`/`scripts/` finds NO un-timed-out subprocess on that chain** (the A2.16/INC-32 cure reached `intraday_ops._run_script` on 2026-06-15). The defect is that **every leg carried the 1800s module default, which IS the tick's own cron cadence**, so the live 3-leg config (`TICK_SF_FREE=1`) was granted **5400s = 3.0x cadence** (rollback config + the dbt-runner 409 `RetryRequested` 40x30s = **10200s = 5.7x**), above which the ONLY ceiling was the global 4h `run_monitoring` cap sized for the Sunday full-refresh build (**8x cadence**). ⇒ ">1h" needed no hang at all; it was inside the budget the job was granted. **RULES: (a) size a tick's per-leg budget FROM ITS CRON (a design quantity), never from a module default — and pin `leg_cap < job_ceiling < cadence` in a guard test so the numbers cannot drift apart silently; (b) ⭐ give the job a `dagster/max_runtime` RUN TAG, because it bounds EVERY wait — subprocess, `requests` poll, retry backoff, pure in-process work — without anyone having to enumerate them, which is precisely why per-leg timeouts alone were insufficient; (c) a recurring job needs a `concurrency_group` tag or ticks STACK (`services/dagster/dagster.yaml` limits 1 per unique value — `intraday_schedule_job` was the only recurring job here without one), and concurrent ticks write the same S3 partitions and each run DuckDB with `threads=2` on a 2-vCPU box = the CPU saturation that starves the daemon (INC-32 by a different route); (d) `subprocess.run` kills only the DIRECT child — a "killed" leg can leave a grandchild holding half the box; use `betting_ml/utils/bounded_subprocess.run_bounded` (own process group, group killed on expiry AND on any `BaseException`, i.e. on a Dagster run-monitoring termination, which a `max_runtime` ceiling makes ROUTINE).** ⚠️⚠️ **AND THE TRAP INSIDE THAT CURE, found by the RED proof rather than by review: `killpg` on a child that ISN'T in its own group signals the CALLER — deleting `start_new_session=True` made the E11.26 RED proof SIGKILL ITSELF (exit 144, no output, and the deliberate break left ON DISK because a signal skips `finally`). In production that is the Dagster run worker killing itself: a bounded leg timeout becomes a lost run with no diagnosis. ⇒ never signal a process group you are a member of (`pgid == os.getpgid(0)` ⇒ fall back to `proc.kill()`), and a source-mutating RED proof must restore stale backups AT START-UP, because its own worst case is being killed mid-mutation.** 🪤 Two more vacuous-guard instances from the same story: **a SIGKILL escalation is UNTESTABLE against a `time.sleep` child** (it dies on SIGTERM) **and an elapsed-time assertion cannot see it either** (a SIGTERM-only runner still returns in ~11s because the post-kill drain bounds it) → drive it with a **SIGTERM-IGNORING** child and assert **THE CHILD IS DEAD**, not that the call returned quickly; and **a cron guard keyed on a bare `'"*/30 14-23 * * *" in src'` is satisfied by a DIFFERENT schedule in the same file** (`artifact_freshness_daytime` carries the identical cron) → read the cron off the NAMED `ScheduleDefinition`. Also bounded on the same path: `send_alert`'s SNS publish (botocore defaults allow a ~5-min stall, and it is called precisely WHEN an op is already failing and slow). `docs/e11_26_intraday_tick_budget.md`; guards `betting_ml/tests/test_e11_26_intraday_tick_budget.py` + `e11_26_red_proof.py` (22/22 RED).
- **📌 A CONSTANT WHOSE ONLY GUARD READS A *DIFFERENT* CONSTANT IS UNPINNED AND LOOKS PINNED — pin the RENDERED string, on every surface it appears (NF-INJ1-C, carried by NF-DTB-1 2026-08-23):** a suite can screen a copy constant's prose, assert a SIBLING constant is present, and enumerate the components that BIND a shared renderer, and still not have a single clause that would notice the string itself being reworded — because none of them reads the string a user sees. NF-C10's freshness line was in exactly that state: screened by the NF-C8 copy suite, rendered on FOUR surfaces (board, projections, player page, and the NF-C9 disclosure), and pinned verbatim on ONE. A reword that reached three of them would have shipped green. **RULES: (a) pin the RENDERED output (an e2e assertion on the string a reader meets), not the constant, and do it PER SURFACE — a pin on a sibling surface is not a pin on this one, especially when the surfaces read the value by different data paths (the player page reads `projections`, the tables read the manifest); (b) assert the RETIRED string ABSENT beside the new one — a presence-only pin structurally cannot see the surface a partial reword missed; (c) require the pin be DECLARED LIVE (`test(`, not `test.skip(`) — a skipped guard is the NF1.7 (a) vacuous anchor with a plausible excuse attached.** ⭐ AND THREE WAYS SUCH A PIN IS VACUOUS ON FIRST CUT, all three measured by NF-DTB-1's RED proof, each a GREEN on a genuine break: **(1)** the story's own EXPLANATORY COMMENT carries both wordings in prose, so an unstripped scan passes with every assertion deleted (INC-38); **(2)** the assertions spell the string inside a REGEX LITERAL (`/injury\/roster…`), so an un-de-escaped scan reads the escape and misses the real one; **(3)** ⭐ the most subtle — an assertion's own FAILURE MESSAGE quotes the retired wording to explain itself, so a bare-substring clause is satisfied by the message with the assertion removed. Match on the REGEX-LITERAL form (a leading `/`) over comment-stripped, de-escaped source. Same family, one layer in: a per-surface guard that greps for an identifier is satisfied by the IMPORT LINE alone — green with every CALL deleted (the NF-C0e wired≠invoked shape); match `name(`, not `name`.
- **🤫 A `CONFLICTING` PR CREATES **ZERO** GITHUB ACTIONS RUNS, SO "NO FAILING CHECKS" READS CLEAN — check `mergeable` EXPLICITLY before trusting a quiet PR (NF-INJ1-C, carried by NF-DTB-1 2026-08-23):** when GitHub cannot compute a PR's merge commit it creates no `pull_request` run at all, so the PR shows no RED checks and looks green-by-absence; "nothing is failing" and "nothing ran" are visually identical (the NF1.7 (a) class, on the PR page). Read `gh pr view <n> --json mergeable,mergeStateStatus,state` and require `mergeable=MERGEABLE` before reading checks at all. ⚠️ SUB-TRAP already recorded at E11.24: a MERGED PR returns `mergeable=UNKNOWN` byte-identically to a not-yet-computed OPEN one, so a "retry until mergeable is known" loop spins forever and reads as a GitHub outage — always check `state` BESIDE `mergeable`. This repo's commonest cause is the **changelog same-week block** (two stories both appending items to one Monday's `items` array), which is a routine, expected conflict rather than a signal of anything wrong; resolve it, push, and only then read the checks. Companion to the E9.60 family already here — `gh pr checks` can serve the PREVIOUS commit's run, so also compare the run's SHA (and `headRefOid`) against `git rev-parse HEAD` before trusting a green.
- **Parallel-session discipline:** when ≥2 sessions touch the same runtime surface (pipeline/serving/exporters), they WILL hit the same runtime bug independently → check this list first; if you hit a NEW landmine, tell the operator to add it here so the other sessions don't rediscover it.

## CI gates — REQUIRED before any handoff (never hand off red code)
⭐ **POLICY (operator 2026-08-08) — OFFLOAD THE GATES TO CI; do NOT run the full suite locally before the PR.** Push the branch, open the PR → `dev`, and let **CI run the unit-test fast+slow gates (the ~13 shard/gate checks) on the PR**; the session VALIDATES CI went green and states the **PR # + CI status** in the handoff. ⚠️ the **dbt-Build CI jobs are `main`-only** (they do NOT fire on a PR to `dev`) — but they're not the memory problem: `dbtf compile` is cheap, so if you touched dbt run it locally (or it's caught at the `dev→main` promotion). The operator gates on CI-green at merge (the PR is to `dev`, not a deploy — no branch protection, so CI-green-before-merge is the operator's manual gate exactly as before, just paid for by CI runners instead of the laptop). **WHY:** running both gates locally slowed every handoff, and the **slow gate is memory-intensive — `-n auto` spawns ~11 workers each doing full per-worker collection and nearly OOM'd a laptop (2026-08-08).** ⚠️ **What still runs LOCALLY:** (a) the ONE targeted test file / shard that exercises your change, as a quick smoke (seconds — catches an obvious break before burning a CI cycle); (b) if you genuinely must run a broad suite, use **`-n 4`, NEVER `-n auto`** (less than half the CPU/memory — `docs/ci_fast_gate_profile.md`), and ⛔ never run the slow gate under `-n auto` on the laptop. ⛔ **UNCHANGED: the 🟥 RUNTIME GATE** (a real box run for pipeline/serving/box/date-tz/boto3/DuckDB code — CI mocks IO) stays an operator step; CI-green is still necessary-not-sufficient there. The bullets below are the reference for HOW the gates are STRUCTURED + how to run one locally when you must:
- **Python → Unit Tests CI (E11.13 fast/slow split; **SHARDED 2026-07-27**):** Run only the tests that exercise the changed modules — find the matching test file(s) under `betting_ml/tests/` or the relevant test directory and run those directly (e.g. `uv run pytest betting_ml/tests/test_derivative_odds.py`). For a cross-cutting change (shared utilities, pipeline ops, dbt-runner paths) run the **fast gate**: `uv run pytest -m "not slow" -n auto` (**~38s** over ~2,560 tests — mirrors CI's `Unit Tests (fast gate)` roll-up and is what unblocks ships). If you touched anything under the `slow` marker (totals/strikeout Monte-Carlo calibration in `test_totals_distribution.py` / `test_prop_pricing.py`) **also** run `uv run pytest -m "slow and not research" -n auto` (**~33s** — mirrors the required "Unit Tests (slow)" job). **Both gates are the merge bar.** The full serial `uv run pytest` is ~4.5 min and unnecessary — prefer `-n auto`.
  - **🪪 REALITY CHECK (verified 2026-07-28) — "required for merge" is CONVENTION, NOT ENFORCEMENT.** `main` has **no branch protection and no rulesets** (`gh api repos/:owner/:repo/branches/main/protection` → 404 "Branch not protected"; `…/rulesets` → `[]`), so **nothing mechanically blocks a red merge** — the gate is the operator declining to merge on red. This is the repo's own *documented-but-never-set* landmine class (cf. `W7B_LAKEHOUSE_S3`), and the E11.13 roadmap entry's "⏭️ Operator: add the slow-tests check to BRANCH PROTECTION" was never actioned. ⇒ **A session must not treat "CI will catch it" as a safety net — run both gates locally and state the result in the handoff (which this section already requires).** The CI job NAMES (`Unit Tests (fast gate)`, `Unit Tests (slow — Monte-Carlo calibration)`) are nevertheless kept stable so enabling protection later is a one-click change with zero CI rework. ✅ **OPERATOR DECISION 2026-07-29 (TD2 escalation): KEEP IT CONVENTION — branch protection is DELIBERATELY NOT enabled** (the operator gates on red manually). The "add the slow-tests check to branch protection" item is CLOSED by decision, not pending. ⇒ do NOT enable protection, and do NOT re-open this as a TODO, without asking the operator; but the convention still binds — under the 2026-08-08 offload policy the PR's CI must be GREEN and the handoff states the **PR # + CI status**; the operator gates on CI-green at merge (no longer "run both gates locally").
  - **🌙 THIRD TIER — `research` (TD2, 2026-07-27): NOT on the merge bar.** The slow gate was ~283s serial, and ~94s of it guarded modules **no serving, pipeline, or API code imports** (`derivative_model_gate`, `line_microstructure`, `bakeoff_perside`, `f5_distribution`) — they only execute when a human runs a bake-off. Those tests are now `@pytest.mark.slow` **+ `@pytest.mark.research`**, excluded from the required job (`-m "slow and not research"`) and run nightly by `.github/workflows/research_tests.yml` (`-m research`, ~52s, non-blocking, plus `workflow_dispatch`). **`uv run pytest -m slow` still runs EVERYTHING locally** — the tier only changes what blocks a merge. ⚠️ **Tier rule (same shape as the HALT/WARN op contract): mark `research` ONLY after checking `grep -rn '<module>' scripts/ app/ pipeline/` comes back EMPTY; if a research module later gains a serving importer, DROP the marker in that same change.** Enforced by `test_fast_gate_hygiene.py::TestSlowResearchTierWiring` (partition + both CI selectors), so a test can never fall out of both jobs.
  - **🎲 TRIMMING A MONTE-CARLO TEST — cut DRAWS, never GAMES (TD2).** The three `TestCalibration` tests were 174s of the slow gate. Cost is **exactly linear in `n_games × n_draws`**: `draw_independent_samples` → `sample_gaussian_copula_negbin` → `scipy.stats.nbinom.ppf`, which inverts at only **~0.48M draws/s** — so 6000×2000×2 = 24M inversions ≈ 55s **per test**. ⭐ **`n_games` is the POWER knob: the flatness gate reads DECILE frequencies whose SE is `sqrt(0.1·0.9/n_games)` (0.0039 at n=6000, so the 0.025 tolerance sits ~6.5 SE out). Cutting games eats that margin → a flaky gate. Cutting DRAWS only refines each game's predictive CDF, already far finer than an integer-valued total needs.** `n_draws` 2000→500 = 3.9× faster with **no tolerance weakened**, backed by a 15-seed sweep (correct spec: worst decile dev 0.0113 vs 0.025 tol; too-tight control still DETECTED 15/15 at min 0.0432). **A trimmed gate must be re-proven to FAIL on known-bad input** — verified two-sided here (too-tight r=8.5 → c80 0.762; too-fat r=1.5 → c80 0.931; both reject on flatness AND coverage). If a serving path ever needs faster MC, `nbinom.ppf` is the hotspot, not the draw loop.
  - **⚙️ WHY IT COSTS WHAT IT COSTS — per-worker COLLECTION, not slow tests (2026-07-27 profile).** Every xdist worker imports ALL ~175 test modules before running its share, so collection is duplicated N times while the work is divided by N. Pre-fix that made `-n auto` burn **287s of CPU to do 91s of work** and be *slower* than a small pool (`-n 4` 35.5s < `-n 6` 37.1s < `-n auto`(11) 44s). Removing the import-time S3 fetch (below) closed most of that gap: `-n auto` and `-n 4` now tie on wall-clock (~38s), but **`-n 4` uses less than half the CPU (123s vs 261s)** — so prefer **`uv run pytest -m "not slow" -n 4`** on a busy laptop, and keep `-n auto` in CI (2–4-core runners). ⚠️ The duplicated-collection cost is structural: if the gate creeps back up, suspect a NEW expensive module-level import before you suspect a slow test. Full measurement: `docs/ci_fast_gate_profile.md`.
  - **🧩 The fast gate runs as 6 DOMAIN SHARDS in CI** (`football`, `baseball-models`, `prospect-milb`, `guards`, `serving-ops`, `core`) so a red check localises the blame and the longest unit of work is **32.3s instead of 91s**. The map is **`scripts/ci_shards.py` — the single source of truth**; the CI matrix in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` must list exactly `SHARD_NAMES`.
    - **`core` is a COMPUTED catch-all** ("every test file no named shard claims"), so **a new test file joins the merge bar automatically** — you never have to register it. Adding a shard rule is an optimisation (move an expensive new family off `core`), never a correctness requirement.
    - `betting_ml/tests/test_fast_gate_hygiene.py` (fast gate) FAILS on a broken partition, an empty/rotted shard rule, or a CI matrix that has drifted from `SHARD_NAMES`. **Run one shard locally exactly as CI does:** `uv run pytest $(uv run python scripts/ci_shards.py --shard football) -m "not slow" -n auto` (bash — zsh does not word-split; use `${=…}` there). List shards with `uv run python scripts/ci_shards.py --list`.
    - ⚠️ **DO NOT RENAME `Unit Tests (fast gate)`.** The matrix runs as `unit-tests-shard`; a roll-up job keeps that exact name. Protection is not enabled today (see the reality check above), but the name is the one a required check would reference — and a renamed required check stays pending forever and blocks every PR, so keeping it stable makes enabling protection free. Pinned by a guard test.
  - **Marker discipline (keep the suite from re-bloating):** any NEW test that takes **>5s** (heavy Monte-Carlo / large simulated fits / expensive expanding-window calibration) MUST get `@pytest.mark.slow` so it lands in the slow job, not the fast gate. Markers are registered in `pyproject.toml` under `[tool.pytest.ini_options] markers` and enforced by `--strict-markers` (an unregistered marker is a hard error). *(2026-07-27 audit: marker hygiene was already CLEAN — the slowest fast-gate test is 4.3s. Slow tests were never the bottleneck; per-worker collection was. Nothing was reclassified.)*
  - **🚫 NO GLOBAL STATE MUTATION AT IMPORT TIME — this is what makes the suite shardable.** pytest imports EVERY test module during collection, before a single test runs, so a module-level `sys.modules[...] = MagicMock()` / `os.environ[...] = …` / `os.chdir()` leaks into every other test in that worker. That is the classic "passes in isolation, fails in the full run" flake, it goes non-deterministic under `-n auto` (which tests share a worker varies per run), and it makes a test's result depend on which SHARD it landed in. Do it in a fixture with `monkeypatch` instead; for a by-path module load that needs heavy imports stubbed, use the restore-on-exit helper **`betting_ml/tests/_serving_store_loader.py`**. Enforced by `test_fast_gate_hygiene.py::test_module_does_not_mutate_global_state_at_import` (AST scan of every test file).
  - **🌐 NO NETWORK AT IMPORT EITHER.** All external IO (Snowflake/S3/network) in the suite is mocked — keep it that way (extend the `test_book_odds_leakage_guard.py` text-fixture pattern); the `integration` marker is reserved for any future genuinely-networked test so it, too, stays out of the fast gate. ⚠️ **A module-level call can smuggle real IO past that invariant:** `scripts/predict_today.py` ran `_calibrator = _load_calibrator()` at module scope = a live **S3 GET on import**, paid by every xdist worker during collection (11 test modules import it). Cure = a lazy memoized `_calibrator()` accessor, with `main()` resolving it up front so the run log is unchanged. **Never do expensive/IO work at module scope in a script that tests import** — put it behind a memoized accessor. Guard: `test_fast_gate_hygiene.py::test_predict_today_does_not_fetch_the_calibrator_at_import`.
- **dbt → BOTH dbt-Build CI jobs:** `dbtf build --select state:modified+` **and** `dbtf compile`
  - **🩹 E11.16 — `state:modified+` MUST pass `--state` locally.** Running `dbtf … --select state:modified+` *without* `--state` makes fusion auto-download a deferral manifest from dbt Platform for the active project in `~/.dbt/dbt_cloud.yml` (`441385`). This repo is **self-managed (no dbt Platform deployment)**, so it 404s (`dbt1203`), the `state:modified` baseline is lost, and the selection is unreliable — exactly the trap that cost a W1d session. **CI is already correct** (it passes `--state dbt/state --defer` and runs on clean runners with no `dbt_cloud.yml`). For local validation use **`scripts/dbt_state.sh build --select state:modified+ --target dev`** — it fetches the same prod-manifest baseline CI uses and injects `--state … --defer`. Passing `--state` suppresses the Platform call entirely (verified).
If the PR's CI is red, fix it (or flag it as a real blocker in the handoff) — don't hand the operator a red PR. State the PR # + CI status in the handoff.

## Session closeout — REQUIRED (every session, both tracks)
Every session works on **its own branch off `dev`** (🌿 above) and **ends with an `⏭️ Operator handoff`** so the repo doesn't drift:
0. **BRANCH + PR:** confirm you committed + pushed to your OWN session branch (never `dev`/`main`); the handoff hands the operator a **PR (branch → `dev`)** to merge. State the branch name. ⚠️ if another in-flight session shares files you touched, SAY SO (the operator rebases/merges in order).
1. **CI-gate result** — the PR's CI (unit-test shards + both dbt-Build jobs) is GREEN. ⚠️ CI runs the gates ON THE PR (2026-08-08 offload policy) — do NOT run the full suite locally; state the **PR # + CI status**, not a local run.
2. **Run-order commands** the operator must execute (Snowflake / `dbtf --select …` / `uv run …`, >1-min flagged). 🚨 **MANDATORY FORMAT for EVERY command you hand the operator (in closeout AND mid-session) — the operator loses time chasing this otherwise:** (a) state **WHERE to run it — the LAPTOP or the EC2 BOX** (a box command must be wrapped, e.g. `docker compose -f services/dagster/aws/docker-compose.yml exec -T dagster-codeloc python scripts/X.py …`, `-e AWS_DEFAULT_REGION=us-east-2` for DuckDB-S3; SSM in for box shell — see `BOX_OPERATIONS.md`); and (b) the **FULL, copy-pasteable command** (exact flags/paths/env — no "run the ingest script" prose, no placeholders left unfilled). Never give a partial command or omit the laptop-vs-box location.
3. **Commit list** — the files you committed on your branch: code, dbt models, `sub_model_registry.yaml`, `ablation_results/*.md`, and any guide/roadmap/`story_prompts.md` edits. (You commit these on your branch; the operator no longer runs a central `git add`.)
4. **Do NOT commit** large artifacts (`*.pkl`, `*.parquet`, model binaries) — those go to S3/registry and are gitignored; list them as excluded.
5. Model work: the validation gate result. App work: the `frontend/data/changelog.json` line + what to verify after deploy. 🌿 **PUBLISH/DEPLOY IS A POST-MERGE OPERATOR STEP:** if the DoD needs `export … --publish` (prod S3) or `infrastructure/lambda/deploy.sh`, DO NOT run it from your branch — list it as a command the operator runs AFTER merging the PR to `dev`, in the mandatory LAPTOP-vs-BOX + full-command format.
